6: #include "RoadCurbCut.h"
7: #include "Algo/Sort.h"
8: 
9: namespace
10: {
11: 	/** Border sample placed just outside the profile so the ramp starts from a vertex at full height. */
12: 	constexpr double CurbCutBorder = 5.0;
13: 
14: 	/** Shortest signed arc from B to A on a ring of length Period (Period <= 0 => plain difference). */
15: 	double ShortestArcDelta(double A, double B, double Period)
16: 	{
17: 		double Delta = A - B;
18: 		if (Period > UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
19: 		{
20: 			Delta = FMath::Fmod(Delta, Period);
21: 			if (Delta > Period * 0.5)
22: 			{
23: 				Delta -= Period;
24: 			}
25: 			else if (Delta < -Period * 0.5)
26: 			{
27: 				Delta += Period;
28: 			}
29: 		}
30: 		return Delta;
31: 	}
32: 
33: 	double WrapArc(double S, double Period)
34: 	{
35: 		if (Period <= UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
36: 		{
37: 			return S;
38: 		}
39: 		double Wrapped = FMath::Fmod(S, Period);
40: 		if (Wrapped < 0.0)
41: 		{
42: 			Wrapped += Period;
43: 		}
44: 		return Wrapped;
45: 	}
46: }
47: 
48: double FRoadCurbCut::GetSpan() const
49: {
50: 	const double SpanA = WidthA + 2.0 * (FalloffA + CurbCutBorder);
51: 	const double SpanB = bIsCross ? SpanA : (WidthB + 2.0 * (FalloffB + CurbCutBorder));
52: 	return FMath::Max(SpanA, SpanB);
53: }
54: 
55: FInterpCurve<double> FRoadCurbCut::GetCurveByProgress(double SCenter, double Progress) const
56: {
57: 	// Past the cut's lateral extent there is no cut at all. An EMPTY curve evaluates to the caller's default (0 =
58: 	// untouched), which is exactly what the height and sampling paths want.
59: 	if (!bIsCross && Progress > 1.0)
60: 	{
61: 		return {};
62: 	}
63: 
64: 	const double A = bIsCross ? 0.0 : FMath::Clamp(Progress, 0.0, 1.0);
65: 	const double Falloff = FMath::Lerp(FalloffA, FalloffB, A);
66: 	const double Width = FMath::Lerp(WidthA, WidthB, A);
67: 
68: 	FInterpCurve<double> Curve;
69: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter - Width * 0.5 - Falloff, 0.0);
70: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter - Width * 0.5, 1.0);
71: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter + Width * 0.5, 1.0);
72: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter + Width * 0.5 + Falloff, 0.0);
73: 
74: 	if (bSmooth)
75: 	{
76: 		for (auto& It : Curve.Points)
77: 		{
78: 			It.InterpMode = EInterpCurveMode::CIM_CurveAuto;
79: 		}
80: 	}
81: 
82: 	return Curve;
83: }
84: 
85: void FRoadCurbCut::GetAdditionalOffsets(double SCenter, double Progress, TArray<double>& OutS, double SPeriod) const
86: {

12: 	constexpr double CurbCutBorder = 5.0;

14: 	/** Shortest signed arc from B to A on a ring of length Period (Period <= 0 => plain difference). */
15: 	double ShortestArcDelta(double A, double B, double Period)
16: 	{
17: 		double Delta = A - B;
18: 		if (Period > UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
19: 		{
20: 			Delta = FMath::Fmod(Delta, Period);
21: 			if (Delta > Period * 0.5)
22: 			{
23: 				Delta -= Period;
24: 			}
25: 			else if (Delta < -Period * 0.5)
26: 			{
27: 				Delta += Period;
28: 			}
29: 		}
30: 		return Delta;
31: 	}
32: 
33: 	double WrapArc(double S, double Period)
34: 	{
35: 		if (Period <= UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
36: 		{
37: 			return S;
38: 		}
39: 		double Wrapped = FMath::Fmod(S, Period);
40: 		if (Wrapped < 0.0)
41: 		{
42: 			Wrapped += Period;
43: 		}
44: 		return Wrapped;
45: 	}
46: }

48: double FRoadCurbCut::GetSpan() const
49: {
50: 	const double SpanA = WidthA + 2.0 * (FalloffA + CurbCutBorder);
51: 	const double SpanB = bIsCross ? SpanA : (WidthB + 2.0 * (FalloffB + CurbCutBorder));
52: 	return FMath::Max(SpanA, SpanB);
53: }

55: FInterpCurve<double> FRoadCurbCut::GetCurveByProgress(double SCenter, double Progress) const
56: {
57: 	// Past the cut's lateral extent there is no cut at all. An EMPTY curve evaluates to the caller's default (0 =
58: 	// untouched), which is exactly what the height and sampling paths want.
59: 	if (!bIsCross && Progress > 1.0)
60: 	{
61: 		return {};
62: 	}
63: 
64: 	const double A = bIsCross ? 0.0 : FMath::Clamp(Progress, 0.0, 1.0);
65: 	const double Falloff = FMath::Lerp(FalloffA, FalloffB, A);
66: 	const double Width = FMath::Lerp(WidthA, WidthB, A);
67: 
68: 	FInterpCurve<double> Curve;
69: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter - Width * 0.5 - Falloff, 0.0);
70: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter - Width * 0.5, 1.0);
71: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter + Width * 0.5, 1.0);
72: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter + Width * 0.5 + Falloff, 0.0);
73: 
74: 	if (bSmooth)
75: 	{
76: 		for (auto& It : Curve.Points)
77: 		{
78: 			It.InterpMode = EInterpCurveMode::CIM_CurveAuto;
79: 		}
80: 	}
81: 
82: 	return Curve;
83: }

55: FInterpCurve<double> FRoadCurbCut::GetCurveByProgress(double SCenter, double Progress) const
56: {
57: 	// Past the cut's lateral extent there is no cut at all. An EMPTY curve evaluates to the caller's default (0 =
58: 	// untouched), which is exactly what the height and sampling paths want.
59: 	if (!bIsCross && Progress > 1.0)
60: 	{
61: 		return {};
62: 	}
63: 
64: 	const double A = bIsCross ? 0.0 : FMath::Clamp(Progress, 0.0, 1.0);
65: 	const double Falloff = FMath::Lerp(FalloffA, FalloffB, A);
66: 	const double Width = FMath::Lerp(WidthA, WidthB, A);
67: 
68: 	FInterpCurve<double> Curve;
69: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter - Width * 0.5 - Falloff, 0.0);
70: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter - Width * 0.5, 1.0);
71: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter + Width * 0.5, 1.0);
72: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter + Width * 0.5 + Falloff, 0.0);
73: 
74: 	if (bSmooth)
75: 	{
76: 		for (auto& It : Curve.Points)
77: 		{
78: 			It.InterpMode = EInterpCurveMode::CIM_CurveAuto;
79: 		}
80: 	}
81: 
82: 	return Curve;
83: }
84: 
85: void FRoadCurbCut::GetAdditionalOffsets(double SCenter, double Progress, TArray<double>& OutS, double SPeriod) const
86: {
87: 	const FInterpCurve<double> Curve = GetCurveByProgress(SCenter, Progress);
88: 	if (Curve.Points.Num() < 1)
89: 	{
90: 		return;
91: 	}
92: 
93: 	const int32 FirstAdded = OutS.Num();
94: 
95: 	OutS.Add(Curve.Points[0].InVal - CurbCutBorder);
96: 
97: 	for (int i = 0; i < Curve.Points.Num() - 1; ++i)
98: 	{
99: 		const double S0 = Curve.Points[i].InVal;
100: 		const double S1 = Curve.Points[i + 1].InVal;
101: 		const double Step = (S1 - S0) / MeshDensity;
102: 
103: 		for (int j = 0; j < MeshDensity; ++j)
104: 		{
105: 			OutS.Add(S0 + Step * j);
106: 		}
107: 	}
108: 
109: 	OutS.Add(Curve.Points.Last().InVal);
110: 	OutS.Add(Curve.Points.Last().InVal + CurbCutBorder);
111: 
112: 	// A closed host samples a fixed [0, Length] range, so a cut straddling the seam must hand back its far half
113: 	// wrapped to the other end rather than as an out-of-range value the sampler would silently drop.
114: 	if (SPeriod > UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
115: 	{
116: 		for (int32 i = FirstAdded; i < OutS.Num(); ++i)
117: 		{
118: 			OutS[i] = WrapArc(OutS[i], SPeriod);
119: 		}
120: 	}
121: }
122: 
123: double FRoadCurbCut::ApplyHeight(double SCenter, double S, double Progress, double InHeight, double SPeriod) const
124: {
125: 	// Full cut at the curb, fading to none at the extent; a cross cut keeps full strength all the way in.
126: 	const double HeightMul = bIsCross ? 1.0 : FMath::Lerp(1.0, 0.0, FMath::Clamp(Progress, 0.0, 1.0));
127: 	const FInterpCurve<double> Curve = GetCurveByProgress(SCenter, Progress);
128: 
---
30: 		return Delta;
31: 	}
32: 
33: 	double WrapArc(double S, double Period)
34: 	{
35: 		if (Period <= UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
36: 		{
37: 			return S;
38: 		}
39: 		double Wrapped = FMath::Fmod(S, Period);
40: 		if (Wrapped < 0.0)
41: 		{
42: 			Wrapped += Period;
43: 		}
44: 		return Wrapped;
45: 	}
46: }

55: FInterpCurve<double> FRoadCurbCut::GetCurveByProgress(double SCenter, double Progress) const
56: {
57: 	// Past the cut's lateral extent there is no cut at all. An EMPTY curve evaluates to the caller's default (0 =
58: 	// untouched), which is exactly what the height and sampling paths want.
59: 	if (!bIsCross && Progress > 1.0)
60: 	{
61: 		return {};
62: 	}
63: 
64: 	const double A = bIsCross ? 0.0 : FMath::Clamp(Progress, 0.0, 1.0);
65: 	const double Falloff = FMath::Lerp(FalloffA, FalloffB, A);
66: 	const double Width = FMath::Lerp(WidthA, WidthB, A);
67: 
68: 	FInterpCurve<double> Curve;
69: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter - Width * 0.5 - Falloff, 0.0);
70: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter - Width * 0.5, 1.0);
71: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter + Width * 0.5, 1.0);
72: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter + Width * 0.5 + Falloff, 0.0);
73: 
74: 	if (bSmooth)
75: 	{
76: 		for (auto& It : Curve.Points)
77: 		{
78: 			It.InterpMode = EInterpCurveMode::CIM_CurveAuto;
79: 		}
80: 	}
81: 
82: 	return Curve;
83: }

85: void FRoadCurbCut::GetAdditionalOffsets(double SCenter, double Progress, TArray<double>& OutS, double SPeriod) const
86: {
87: 	const FInterpCurve<double> Curve = GetCurveByProgress(SCenter, Progress);
88: 	if (Curve.Points.Num() < 1)
89: 	{
90: 		return;
91: 	}
92: 
93: 	const int32 FirstAdded = OutS.Num();
94: 
95: 	OutS.Add(Curve.Points[0].InVal - CurbCutBorder);
96: 
97: 	for (int i = 0; i < Curve.Points.Num() - 1; ++i)
98: 	{
99: 		const double S0 = Curve.Points[i].InVal;
100: 		const double S1 = Curve.Points[i + 1].InVal;
101: 		const double Step = (S1 - S0) / MeshDensity;
102: 
103: 		for (int j = 0; j < MeshDensity; ++j)
104: 		{
105: 			OutS.Add(S0 + Step * j);
106: 		}
107: 	}
108: 
109: 	OutS.Add(Curve.Points.Last().InVal);
110: 	OutS.Add(Curve.Points.Last().InVal + CurbCutBorder);
111: 
112: 	// A closed host samples a fixed [0, Length] range, so a cut straddling the seam must hand back its far half
113: 	// wrapped to the other end rather than as an out-of-range value the sampler would silently drop.
114: 	if (SPeriod > UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
115: 	{
116: 		for (int32 i = FirstAdded; i < OutS.Num(); ++i)
117: 		{
118: 			OutS[i] = WrapArc(OutS[i], SPeriod);
119: 		}
120: 	}
121: }

127: 	const FInterpCurve<double> Curve = GetCurveByProgress(SCenter, Progress);
128: 
129: 	// Measure along the shortest arc so a cut centred near the seam still reaches the vertices on the far side.
130: 	const double LocalS = SCenter + ShortestArcDelta(S, SCenter, SPeriod);

85: void FRoadCurbCut::GetAdditionalOffsets(double SCenter, double Progress, TArray<double>& OutS, double SPeriod) const
86: {
87: 	const FInterpCurve<double> Curve = GetCurveByProgress(SCenter, Progress);
88: 	if (Curve.Points.Num() < 1)
89: 	{
90: 		return;
91: 	}
92: 
93: 	const int32 FirstAdded = OutS.Num();
94: 
95: 	OutS.Add(Curve.Points[0].InVal - CurbCutBorder);
96: 
97: 	for (int i = 0; i < Curve.Points.Num() - 1; ++i)
98: 	{
99: 		const double S0 = Curve.Points[i].InVal;
100: 		const double S1 = Curve.Points[i + 1].InVal;
101: 		const double Step = (S1 - S0) / MeshDensity;
102: 
103: 		for (int j = 0; j < MeshDensity; ++j)
104: 		{
105: 			OutS.Add(S0 + Step * j);
106: 		}
107: 	}
108: 
109: 	OutS.Add(Curve.Points.Last().InVal);
110: 	OutS.Add(Curve.Points.Last().InVal + CurbCutBorder);
111: 
112: 	// A closed host samples a fixed [0, Length] range, so a cut straddling the seam must hand back its far half
113: 	// wrapped to the other end rather than as an out-of-range value the sampler would silently drop.
114: 	if (SPeriod > UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
115: 	{
116: 		for (int32 i = FirstAdded; i < OutS.Num(); ++i)
117: 		{
118: 			OutS[i] = WrapArc(OutS[i], SPeriod);
119: 		}
120: 	}
121: }

85: void FRoadCurbCut::GetAdditionalOffsets(double SCenter, double Progress, TArray<double>& OutS, double SPeriod) const
86: {
87: 	const FInterpCurve<double> Curve = GetCurveByProgress(SCenter, Progress);
88: 	if (Curve.Points.Num() < 1)
89: 	{
90: 		return;
91: 	}
92: 
93: 	const int32 FirstAdded = OutS.Num();
94: 
95: 	OutS.Add(Curve.Points[0].InVal - CurbCutBorder);
96: 
97: 	for (int i = 0; i < Curve.Points.Num() - 1; ++i)
98: 	{
99: 		const double S0 = Curve.Points[i].InVal;
100: 		const double S1 = Curve.Points[i + 1].InVal;
101: 		const double Step = (S1 - S0) / MeshDensity;
102: 
103: 		for (int j = 0; j < MeshDensity; ++j)
104: 		{
105: 			OutS.Add(S0 + Step * j);
106: 		}
107: 	}
108: 
109: 	OutS.Add(Curve.Points.Last().InVal);
110: 	OutS.Add(Curve.Points.Last().InVal + CurbCutBorder);
111: 
112: 	// A closed host samples a fixed [0, Length] range, so a cut straddling the seam must hand back its far half
113: 	// wrapped to the other end rather than as an out-of-range value the sampler would silently drop.
114: 	if (SPeriod > UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
115: 	{
116: 		for (int32 i = FirstAdded; i < OutS.Num(); ++i)
117: 		{
118: 			OutS[i] = WrapArc(OutS[i], SPeriod);
119: 		}
120: 	}
121: }
122: 
123: double FRoadCurbCut::ApplyHeight(double SCenter, double S, double Progress, double InHeight, double SPeriod) const
124: {
125: 	// Full cut at the curb, fading to none at the extent; a cross cut keeps full strength all the way in.
126: 	const double HeightMul = bIsCross ? 1.0 : FMath::Lerp(1.0, 0.0, FMath::Clamp(Progress, 0.0, 1.0));
127: 	const FInterpCurve<double> Curve = GetCurveByProgress(SCenter, Progress);
128: 
129: 	// Measure along the shortest arc so a cut centred near the seam still reaches the vertices on the far side.
130: 	const double LocalS = SCenter + ShortestArcDelta(S, SCenter, SPeriod);
131: 
132: 	return (1.0 - Curve.Eval(LocalS, 0.0) * HeightMul) * InHeight;
133: }
134: 
135: // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
---
65: 	const double Falloff = FMath::Lerp(FalloffA, FalloffB, A);
66: 	const double Width = FMath::Lerp(WidthA, WidthB, A);
67: 
68: 	FInterpCurve<double> Curve;
69: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter - Width * 0.5 - Falloff, 0.0);
70: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter - Width * 0.5, 1.0);
71: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter + Width * 0.5, 1.0);
72: 	Curve.AddPoint(SCenter + Width * 0.5 + Falloff, 0.0);
73: 
74: 	if (bSmooth)
75: 	{
76: 		for (auto& It : Curve.Points)
77: 		{
78: 			It.InterpMode = EInterpCurveMode::CIM_CurveAuto;
79: 		}
80: 	}
81: 
82: 	return Curve;
83: }
84: 
85: void FRoadCurbCut::GetAdditionalOffsets(double SCenter, double Progress, TArray<double>& OutS, double SPeriod) const
86: {
87: 	const FInterpCurve<double> Curve = GetCurveByProgress(SCenter, Progress);
88: 	if (Curve.Points.Num() < 1)
89: 	{
90: 		return;
91: 	}
92: 
93: 	const int32 FirstAdded = OutS.Num();
94: 
95: 	OutS.Add(Curve.Points[0].InVal - CurbCutBorder);
96: 
97: 	for (int i = 0; i < Curve.Points.Num() - 1; ++i)
98: 	{
99: 		const double S0 = Curve.Points[i].InVal;
100: 		const double S1 = Curve.Points[i + 1].InVal;
101: 		const double Step = (S1 - S0) / MeshDensity;
102: 
103: 		for (int j = 0; j < MeshDensity; ++j)
104: 		{
105: 			OutS.Add(S0 + Step * j);
106: 		}
107: 	}
108: 
109: 	OutS.Add(Curve.Points.Last().InVal);
110: 	OutS.Add(Curve.Points.Last().InVal + CurbCutBorder);
111: 
112: 	// A closed host samples a fixed [0, Length] range, so a cut straddling the seam must hand back its far half
113: 	// wrapped to the other end rather than as an out-of-range value the sampler would silently drop.
114: 	if (SPeriod > UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
115: 	{
116: 		for (int32 i = FirstAdded; i < OutS.Num(); ++i)
117: 		{
118: 			OutS[i] = WrapArc(OutS[i], SPeriod);
119: 		}
120: 	}
121: }
122: 
123: double FRoadCurbCut::ApplyHeight(double SCenter, double S, double Progress, double InHeight, double SPeriod) const
124: {
125: 	// Full cut at the curb, fading to none at the extent; a cross cut keeps full strength all the way in.
126: 	const double HeightMul = bIsCross ? 1.0 : FMath::Lerp(1.0, 0.0, FMath::Clamp(Progress, 0.0, 1.0));
127: 	const FInterpCurve<double> Curve = GetCurveByProgress(SCenter, Progress);
128: 
129: 	// Measure along the shortest arc so a cut centred near the seam still reaches the vertices on the far side.
130: 	const double LocalS = SCenter + ShortestArcDelta(S, SCenter, SPeriod);
131: 
132: 	return (1.0 - Curve.Eval(LocalS, 0.0) * HeightMul) * InHeight;
133: }

140: void BuildArcLengths(const TArray<FVector>& Points, TArray<double>& OutArcs)
141: {
142: 	const int32 Num = Points.Num();
143: 	OutArcs.Reset(Num + 1);
144: 	OutArcs.Add(0.0);
145: 	for (int32 i = 0; i < Num; ++i)
146: 	{
147: 		// Planar length throughout: every query into a ring comes from the 2D arrangement, so a 3D metric would
148: 		// make the two disagree on a sloped sidewalk.
149: 		OutArcs.Add(OutArcs.Last() + FVector2D::Distance(FVector2D(Points[i]), FVector2D(Points[(i + 1) % Num])));
150: 	}

154: {
155: 	double SignedArea2 = 0.0;
156: 	const int32 Num = Points.Num();

158: 	{
159: 		const FVector& A = Points[i];
160: 		const FVector& B = Points[(i + 1) % Num];
161: 		SignedArea2 += (A.X * B.Y - B.X * A.Y);
162: 	}
163: 	// CCW (positive area) => the interior is to the LEFT of the travel direction, which is the +1 sense of the
164: 	// left-hand normal used by SampleInward.
165: 	return SignedArea2 >= 0.0 ? 1.0 : -1.0;

80: 	}

200: FVector2D SampleInward(const TArray<FVector>& Points, const TArray<double>& Arcs,
201: 	double Arc, double InwardDistance, double InInwardSign)
202: {
203: 	const int32 Num = Points.Num();
204: 	if (Num < 2 || Arcs.Num() != Num + 1)
205: 	{
206: 		return Num > 0 ? FVector2D(Points[0]) : FVector2D::ZeroVector;
207: 	}
208: 
209: 	const double Total = Arcs.Last();
210: 	Arc = WrapArc(Arc, Total);
211: 
212: 	int32 Seg = 0;
213: 	while (Seg + 1 < Num && Arcs[Seg + 1] <= Arc)
214: 	{
215: 		++Seg;
216: 	}
217: 
---
353: 
354: 	double BestDistSq = TNumericLimits<double>::Max();
355: 	for (int32 i = 0; i + 1 < Num; ++i)
356: 	{
357: 		const FVector2D A = Chain[i];
358: 		const FVector2D B = Chain[i + 1];
359: 		const FVector2D AB = B - A;
360: 		const double L2 = AB.SizeSquared();
361: 		double T = (L2 > 1e-8) ? FVector2D::DotProduct(Query - A, AB) / L2 : 0.0;
362: 		T = FMath::Clamp(T, 0.0, 1.0);
363: 		const double DistSq = FVector2D::DistSquared(Query, A + AB * T);
364: 		if (DistSq < BestDistSq)
365: 		{
366: 			BestDistSq = DistSq;
367: 			OutArc = FMath::Lerp(Arcs[i], Arcs[i + 1], T);
368: 		}
369: 	}
370: 	OutDistance = FMath::Sqrt(BestDistSq);
371: }
372: 
373: } // namespace MetaRoad::CurbCutRing

3:  * Email: ivzhuk7@gmail.com
4:  */

6: #pragma once
7: 
8: #include "CoreMinimal.h"
9: #include "Math/InterpCurve.h"
10: #include "RoadCurbCut.generated.h"
11: 
12: /**
13:  * FRoadCurbCut â€” the geometry of ONE curb cut (a ramp where the sidewalk drops to road level), independent of
14:  * where it is authored.
15:  *
16:  * Two hosts use the same maths through a single NORMALIZED lateral coordinate, `Progress`:
17:  *   - a road LANE (FRoadLaneAttributeCurbCutValue): Progress = LaneAlpha / Alpha, i.e. the cut reaches
18:  *     `Alpha` of the lane width;
19:  *   - a closed RING fill (looped road zone / sidewalk spline): Progress = DistanceFromRing / Depth.
20:  * Progress 0 = at the curb (full cut), 1 = at the far edge of the cut (no cut), > 1 = outside the cut.
21:  *
22:  * The cut profile along S is a 4-point curve of cut STRENGTH (0 = untouched, 1 = fully lowered):
23:  *
24:  *     1.0        ______________
25:  *               /              \
26:  *     0.0  ____/                \____
27:  *          | F |     Width      | F |
28:  *
29:  * with Width/Falloff lerped from the (A) pair at Progress 0 to the (B) pair at Progress 1, so the cut can taper
30:  * as it goes inward. `bIsCross` pins the profile to the (A) pair at full strength across the whole extent â€” a
31:  * pedestrian-crossing style cut rather than a driveway ramp.
32:  *
33:  * Pure data + pure maths, in the RUNTIME module, so the editor attribute value AND the runtime sidewalk-spline
34:  * per-node settings can both drive it. The attribute keeps its own (identical) UPROPERTY block for
35:  * serialization compatibility with existing content and converts through FRoadLaneAttributeCurbCutValue::ToCurbCut().
36:  */
37: USTRUCT(BlueprintType)
38: struct METAROAD_API FRoadCurbCut
39: {
40: 	GENERATED_BODY()
41: 
42: 	/** Cross-walk style: full-strength cut of constant (A) width across the whole extent. Disables the B pair. */
43: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut)
44: 	bool bIsCross = false;
45: 
46: 	/** Width of the fully-lowered part at the curb [cm]. */
47: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 10000, ClampMax = 1000))
48: 	double WidthA = 200;
49: 
50: 	/** Ramp length added on each side of WidthA, at the curb [cm]. */
51: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 1000, ClampMax = 1000))
52: 	double FalloffA = 50;
53: 
54: 	/** Width of the fully-lowered part at the far edge of the cut [cm]. */
55: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 10000, ClampMax = 1000, EditCondition = "!bIsCross"))
56: 	double WidthB = 200;
57: 
58: 	/** Ramp length at the far edge of the cut [cm]. */
59: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 1000, ClampMax = 1000, EditCondition = "!bIsCross"))
60: 	double FalloffB = 0;
61: 
62: 	/** How far the cut reaches INWARD from the ring [cm]. The ring-host counterpart of the lane host's `Alpha`. */
63: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 2000, ClampMax = 10000))
64: 	double Depth = 200;
65: 
66: 	/** Subdivisions per profile span â€” the topology density of the ramp. */
67: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 1, ClampMin = 1, UIMax = 10, ClampMax = 10))
68: 	int MeshDensity = 4;
69: 
70: 	/** Smooth (CIM_CurveAuto) instead of straight ramps. */
71: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut)
72: 	bool bSmooth = false;
73: 
74: 	/** Widest S span the cut can touch (the larger end, falloffs and the border samples included) [cm]. */
75: 	double GetSpan() const;
76: 
77: 	/**
78: 	 * Cut-strength profile against ABSOLUTE S, at the normalized lateral `Progress`.
79: 	 * Empty curve (evaluates to 0 = no cut) once Progress leaves [0,1] on a non-cross cut.
80: 	 */
81: 	FInterpCurve<double> GetCurveByProgress(double SCenter, double Progress) const;
82: 
83: 	/**
84: 	 * S positions the host must sample so the ramp has topology: a border sample, then MeshDensity samples per
85: 	 * profile span, then the profile's end + a border sample. Appends; emits nothing outside the cut.
86: 	 * `SPeriod > 0` wraps the emitted values into [0, SPeriod) â€” a closed ring's seam.
87: 	 */
88: 	void GetAdditionalOffsets(double SCenter, double Progress, TArray<double>& OutS, double SPeriod = 0.0) const;
89: 
90: 	/**
---

2:  * Copyright (c) 2026 Ivan Zhukov. All Rights Reserved.
3:  * Email: ivzhuk7@gmail.com
4:  */

49: 
50: 	/** Ramp length added on each side of WidthA, at the curb [cm]. */
51: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 1000, ClampMax = 1000))
52: 	double FalloffA = 50;
53: 
54: 	/** Width of the fully-lowered part at the far edge of the cut [cm]. */
55: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 10000, ClampMax = 1000, EditCondition = "!bIsCross"))
56: 	double WidthB = 200;
57: 
58: 	/** Ramp length at the far edge of the cut [cm]. */
59: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 1000, ClampMax = 1000, EditCondition = "!bIsCross"))
60: 	double FalloffB = 0;
61: 
62: 	/** How far the cut reaches INWARD from the ring [cm]. The ring-host counterpart of the lane host's `Alpha`. */
63: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 2000, ClampMax = 10000))
64: 	double Depth = 200;
65: 
66: 	/** Subdivisions per profile span â€” the topology density of the ramp. */
67: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 1, ClampMin = 1, UIMax = 10, ClampMax = 10))
68: 	int MeshDensity = 4;
69: 
70: 	/** Smooth (CIM_CurveAuto) instead of straight ramps. */
71: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut)
72: 	bool bSmooth = false;
73: 
74: 	/** Widest S span the cut can touch (the larger end, falloffs and the border samples included) [cm]. */
75: 	double GetSpan() const;
76: 
77: 	/**
78: 	 * Cut-strength profile against ABSOLUTE S, at the normalized lateral `Progress`.
79: 	 * Empty curve (evaluates to 0 = no cut) once Progress leaves [0,1] on a non-cross cut.
80: 	 */
81: 	FInterpCurve<double> GetCurveByProgress(double SCenter, double Progress) const;
82: 
83: 	/**
84: 	 * S positions the host must sample so the ramp has topology: a border sample, then MeshDensity samples per
85: 	 * profile span, then the profile's end + a border sample. Appends; emits nothing outside the cut.
86: 	 * `SPeriod > 0` wraps the emitted values into [0, SPeriod) â€” a closed ring's seam.
87: 	 */
88: 	void GetAdditionalOffsets(double SCenter, double Progress, TArray<double>& OutS, double SPeriod = 0.0) const;
89: 
90: 	/**
91: 	 * The lowered height at (S, Progress): `(1 - Strength(S) * HeightMul) * InHeight`, where HeightMul fades the
92: 	 * cut out laterally (full at Progress 0, none at Progress 1; constant for a cross cut).
93: 	 * `SPeriod > 0` measures S - SCenter along the shortest arc of a closed ring, so a cut may straddle the seam.
94: 	 */
95: 	double ApplyHeight(double SCenter, double S, double Progress, double InHeight, double SPeriod = 0.0) const;
96: };
97: 
98: /**
99:  * One curb cut placed on a closed boundary ring, at `ArcOffset` centimetres along it. The transport form used by
100:  * FRoadPolygonData (sidewalk-spline fills) â€” the producer resolves per-node authoring into these.
101:  */
102: USTRUCT(BlueprintType)
103: struct METAROAD_API FRoadCurbCutPlacement
104: {
105: 	GENERATED_BODY()
106: 
107: 	/** Centre of the cut, as arc length along the owning ring [cm]. */
108: 	UPROPERTY()
109: 	double ArcOffset = 0.0;
110: 
111: 	UPROPERTY()
112: 	FRoadCurbCut Cut;
113: };
114: 
---
15:  *
16:  * Two hosts use the same maths through a single NORMALIZED lateral coordinate, `Progress`:
17:  *   - a road LANE (FRoadLaneAttributeCurbCutValue): Progress = LaneAlpha / Alpha, i.e. the cut reaches
18:  *     `Alpha` of the lane width;
19:  *   - a closed RING fill (looped road zone / sidewalk spline): Progress = DistanceFromRing / Depth.
20:  * Progress 0 = at the curb (full cut), 1 = at the far edge of the cut (no cut), > 1 = outside the cut.
21:  *
22:  * The cut profile along S is a 4-point curve of cut STRENGTH (0 = untouched, 1 = fully lowered):
23:  *
24:  *     1.0        ______________
25:  *               /              \
26:  *     0.0  ____/                \____
27:  *          | F |     Width      | F |
28:  *
29:  * with Width/Falloff lerped from the (A) pair at Progress 0 to the (B) pair at Progress 1, so the cut can taper
30:  * as it goes inward. `bIsCross` pins the profile to the (A) pair at full strength across the whole extent â€” a
31:  * pedestrian-crossing style cut rather than a driveway ramp.
32:  *
33:  * Pure data + pure maths, in the RUNTIME module, so the editor attribute value AND the runtime sidewalk-spline
34:  * per-node settings can both drive it. The attribute keeps its own (identical) UPROPERTY block for
35:  * serialization compatibility with existing content and converts through FRoadLaneAttributeCurbCutValue::ToCurbCut().
36:  */
37: USTRUCT(BlueprintType)
38: struct METAROAD_API FRoadCurbCut
39: {
40: 	GENERATED_BODY()
41: 
42: 	/** Cross-walk style: full-strength cut of constant (A) width across the whole extent. Disables the B pair. */
43: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut)
44: 	bool bIsCross = false;
45: 
46: 	/** Width of the fully-lowered part at the curb [cm]. */
47: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 10000, ClampMax = 1000))
48: 	double WidthA = 200;
49: 
50: 	/** Ramp length added on each side of WidthA, at the curb [cm]. */
51: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 1000, ClampMax = 1000))
52: 	double FalloffA = 50;
53: 
54: 	/** Width of the fully-lowered part at the far edge of the cut [cm]. */
55: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 10000, ClampMax = 1000, EditCondition = "!bIsCross"))
56: 	double WidthB = 200;
57: 
58: 	/** Ramp length at the far edge of the cut [cm]. */
59: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 1000, ClampMax = 1000, EditCondition = "!bIsCross"))
60: 	double FalloffB = 0;
61: 
62: 	/** How far the cut reaches INWARD from the ring [cm]. The ring-host counterpart of the lane host's `Alpha`. */
63: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 2000, ClampMax = 10000))
64: 	double Depth = 200;
65: 
66: 	/** Subdivisions per profile span â€” the topology density of the ramp. */
67: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 1, ClampMin = 1, UIMax = 10, ClampMax = 10))
68: 	int MeshDensity = 4;
69: 
70: 	/** Smooth (CIM_CurveAuto) instead of straight ramps. */
71: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut)
72: 	bool bSmooth = false;
73: 
74: 	/** Widest S span the cut can touch (the larger end, falloffs and the border samples included) [cm]. */
75: 	double GetSpan() const;
76: 
77: 	/**
78: 	 * Cut-strength profile against ABSOLUTE S, at the normalized lateral `Progress`.
79: 	 * Empty curve (evaluates to 0 = no cut) once Progress leaves [0,1] on a non-cross cut.
80: 	 */
81: 	FInterpCurve<double> GetCurveByProgress(double SCenter, double Progress) const;
82: 
83: 	/**
84: 	 * S positions the host must sample so the ramp has topology: a border sample, then MeshDensity samples per
85: 	 * profile span, then the profile's end + a border sample. Appends; emits nothing outside the cut.
86: 	 * `SPeriod > 0` wraps the emitted values into [0, SPeriod) â€” a closed ring's seam.
87: 	 */
88: 	void GetAdditionalOffsets(double SCenter, double Progress, TArray<double>& OutS, double SPeriod = 0.0) const;
89: 
90: 	/**
91: 	 * The lowered height at (S, Progress): `(1 - Strength(S) * HeightMul) * InHeight`, where HeightMul fades the
92: 	 * cut out laterally (full at Progress 0, none at Progress 1; constant for a cross cut).
93: 	 * `SPeriod > 0` measures S - SCenter along the shortest arc of a closed ring, so a cut may straddle the seam.
94: 	 */
95: 	double ApplyHeight(double SCenter, double S, double Progress, double InHeight, double SPeriod = 0.0) const;
96: };

99:  * One curb cut placed on a closed boundary ring, at `ArcOffset` centimetres along it. The transport form used by
100:  * FRoadPolygonData (sidewalk-spline fills) â€” the producer resolves per-node authoring into these.
101:  */
102: USTRUCT(BlueprintType)
103: struct METAROAD_API FRoadCurbCutPlacement
104: {
105: 	GENERATED_BODY()
106: 
107: 	/** Centre of the cut, as arc length along the owning ring [cm]. */
108: 	UPROPERTY()
109: 	double ArcOffset = 0.0;
110: 
111: 	UPROPERTY()
112: 	FRoadCurbCut Cut;
113: };
114: 
115: /**
116:  * Ring geometry shared by every closed-ring curb-cut host that has no spline frame of its own (sidewalk-spline
117:  * fills and their curbs). The looped ROAD zone does NOT use these: its ring is the reference line, so it reads
118:  * (S, distance-from-ring) straight out of the spline frame instead.
119:  *
120:  * All rings here are CLOSED and carry NO closing duplicate â€” vertex i connects to (i+1) % Num.
121:  */
122: namespace MetaRoad::CurbCutRing
123: {
124: 	/** Cumulative arc length at each vertex (Out[0] == 0), plus the wrap step; Out has Points.Num() + 1 entries,
125: 	 *  so Out.Last() is the ring's total length. */
126: 	METAROAD_API void BuildArcLengths(const TArray<FVector>& Points, TArray<double>& OutArcs);
127: 
---
31:  * pedestrian-crossing style cut rather than a driveway ramp.
32:  *
33:  * Pure data + pure maths, in the RUNTIME module, so the editor attribute value AND the runtime sidewalk-spline
34:  * per-node settings can both drive it. The attribute keeps its own (identical) UPROPERTY block for
35:  * serialization compatibility with existing content and converts through FRoadLaneAttributeCurbCutValue::ToCurbCut().
36:  */
37: USTRUCT(BlueprintType)
38: struct METAROAD_API FRoadCurbCut
39: {
40: 	GENERATED_BODY()
41: 
42: 	/** Cross-walk style: full-strength cut of constant (A) width across the whole extent. Disables the B pair. */
43: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut)
44: 	bool bIsCross = false;
45: 
46: 	/** Width of the fully-lowered part at the curb [cm]. */
47: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 10000, ClampMax = 1000))
48: 	double WidthA = 200;
49: 
50: 	/** Ramp length added on each side of WidthA, at the curb [cm]. */
51: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 1000, ClampMax = 1000))
52: 	double FalloffA = 50;
53: 
54: 	/** Width of the fully-lowered part at the far edge of the cut [cm]. */
55: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 10000, ClampMax = 1000, EditCondition = "!bIsCross"))
56: 	double WidthB = 200;
57: 
58: 	/** Ramp length at the far edge of the cut [cm]. */
59: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 1000, ClampMax = 1000, EditCondition = "!bIsCross"))
60: 	double FalloffB = 0;
61: 
62: 	/** How far the cut reaches INWARD from the ring [cm]. The ring-host counterpart of the lane host's `Alpha`. */
63: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 2000, ClampMax = 10000))
64: 	double Depth = 200;
65: 
66: 	/** Subdivisions per profile span â€” the topology density of the ramp. */
67: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 1, ClampMin = 1, UIMax = 10, ClampMax = 10))
68: 	int MeshDensity = 4;
69: 
70: 	/** Smooth (CIM_CurveAuto) instead of straight ramps. */
71: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut)
72: 	bool bSmooth = false;
73: 
74: 	/** Widest S span the cut can touch (the larger end, falloffs and the border samples included) [cm]. */
75: 	double GetSpan() const;
76: 
77: 	/**
78: 	 * Cut-strength profile against ABSOLUTE S, at the normalized lateral `Progress`.
79: 	 * Empty curve (evaluates to 0 = no cut) once Progress leaves [0,1] on a non-cross cut.
80: 	 */
81: 	FInterpCurve<double> GetCurveByProgress(double SCenter, double Progress) const;
82: 
83: 	/**
84: 	 * S positions the host must sample so the ramp has topology: a border sample, then MeshDensity samples per
85: 	 * profile span, then the profile's end + a border sample. Appends; emits nothing outside the cut.
86: 	 * `SPeriod > 0` wraps the emitted values into [0, SPeriod) â€” a closed ring's seam.
87: 	 */
88: 	void GetAdditionalOffsets(double SCenter, double Progress, TArray<double>& OutS, double SPeriod = 0.0) const;
89: 
90: 	/**
91: 	 * The lowered height at (S, Progress): `(1 - Strength(S) * HeightMul) * InHeight`, where HeightMul fades the
92: 	 * cut out laterally (full at Progress 0, none at Progress 1; constant for a cross cut).
93: 	 * `SPeriod > 0` measures S - SCenter along the shortest arc of a closed ring, so a cut may straddle the seam.
94: 	 */
95: 	double ApplyHeight(double SCenter, double S, double Progress, double InHeight, double SPeriod = 0.0) const;
96: };

142: 	 *
143: 	 * Returns the OLD -> NEW index map (one entry per input vertex) so the caller can rebase every index that
---
47: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 10000, ClampMax = 1000))

53: 
54: 	/** Width of the fully-lowered part at the far edge of the cut [cm]. */
55: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 10000, ClampMax = 1000, EditCondition = "!bIsCross"))
56: 	double WidthB = 200;
57: 
58: 	/** Ramp length at the far edge of the cut [cm]. */
59: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 1000, ClampMax = 1000, EditCondition = "!bIsCross"))
60: 	double FalloffB = 0;
61: 
62: 	/** How far the cut reaches INWARD from the ring [cm]. The ring-host counterpart of the lane host's `Alpha`. */
63: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 10, ClampMin = 10, UIMax = 2000, ClampMax = 10000))
64: 	double Depth = 200;
65: 
66: 	/** Subdivisions per profile span â€” the topology density of the ramp. */
67: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut, meta = (UIMin = 1, ClampMin = 1, UIMax = 10, ClampMax = 10))
68: 	int MeshDensity = 4;
69: 
70: 	/** Smooth (CIM_CurveAuto) instead of straight ramps. */
71: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = CurbCut)
72: 	bool bSmooth = false;
73: 
74: 	/** Widest S span the cut can touch (the larger end, falloffs and the border samples included) [cm]. */
75: 	double GetSpan() const;
76: 
77: 	/**
78: 	 * Cut-strength profile against ABSOLUTE S, at the normalized lateral `Progress`.
79: 	 * Empty curve (evaluates to 0 = no cut) once Progress leaves [0,1] on a non-cross cut.
80: 	 */
81: 	FInterpCurve<double> GetCurveByProgress(double SCenter, double Progress) const;
82: 
83: 	/**
84: 	 * S positions the host must sample so the ramp has topology: a border sample, then MeshDensity samples per
85: 	 * profile span, then the profile's end + a border sample. Appends; emits nothing outside the cut.
86: 	 * `SPeriod > 0` wraps the emitted values into [0, SPeriod) â€” a closed ring's seam.
87: 	 */
88: 	void GetAdditionalOffsets(double SCenter, double Progress, TArray<double>& OutS, double SPeriod = 0.0) const;
89: 
90: 	/**
91: 	 * The lowered height at (S, Progress): `(1 - Strength(S) * HeightMul) * InHeight`, where HeightMul fades the
92: 	 * cut out laterally (full at Progress 0, none at Progress 1; constant for a cross cut).
93: 	 * `SPeriod > 0` measures S - SCenter along the shortest arc of a closed ring, so a cut may straddle the seam.
94: 	 */
95: 	double ApplyHeight(double SCenter, double S, double Progress, double InHeight, double SPeriod = 0.0) const;
96: };
97:

101:  */

152: 	 * to. `OutArcs` are ABSOLUTE and strictly increasing â€” a chain that wrapped carries arcs past the ring length,
153: 	 * which is exactly what the cut's own wrap-aware evaluation expects.
154: 	 *
155: 	 * Exists so a cut can be evaluated against a SMALL polyline instead of the whole ring: the build applies every
156: 	 * cut to every vertex of the scope, and projecting onto a thousand-vertex ring per vertex per cut is not
157: 	 * affordable.
158: 	 */
159: 	METAROAD_API void ExtractWindow(const TArray<FVector>& Points, const TArray<double>& Arcs,
160: 		double ArcCentre, double HalfSpan, TArray<FVector2D>& OutChain, TArray<double>& OutArcs);
161:

163: 	METAROAD_API void ProjectOnChain(const TArray<FVector2D>& Chain, const TArray<double>& Arcs,
164: 		const FVector2D& Query, double& OutArc, double& OutDistance);

22: /**
23:  * One magnetized span of a producer's boundary/line: the index range [StartIndex, EndIndex] into the owning Vertices
24:  * array. Those Vertices are only an APPROXIMATION of the target (a Hermite tangent fit); the editor-side mesh build
25:  * uses them as a QUERY to find the real arrangement path underneath (MetaRoad::ArrangementMatch) and follows that
26:  * instead â€” which is what picks up the curb-cut / crossing vertices created during triangulation. No path found =>
27:  * the Vertices are used as-is.
28:  *
29:  * The span carries no target identity on purpose: matching is geometric, so it needs no runtime->editor mapping and
30:  * works against any geometry in the arrangement rather than road lanes only.
31:  */
32: USTRUCT()
33: struct METAROAD_API FMagneticSpan
34: {
35: 	GENERATED_BODY()
36: 
37: 	UPROPERTY()
38: 	int32 StartIndex = 0;
39: 
40: 	UPROPERTY()
41: 	int32 EndIndex = 0;
42: };
43: 
44: /**
45:  * One inner contour of a fill: world space, ring order, NO closing duplicate -- exactly the convention
46:  * FRoadPolygonData::Vertices uses.
47:  *
48:  * A wrapper struct rather than a bare TArray<TArray<FVector>> because UHT rejects nested containers, and this has
49:  * to stay reflected: FRoadPolygonData crosses the Blueprint boundary through IRoadFeatureInterface::ReceiveGeneratePolygons.
50:  */
51: USTRUCT(BlueprintType)
52: struct FRoadPolygonRing
53: {
54: 	GENERATED_BODY()
55: 
56: 	/** In World space */
57: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, BlueprintReadWrite, Category = RoadPolygonData)
58: 	TArray<FVector> Vertices{};
59: };
60: 
61: /** Polygon data for prcedure generation (a filled surface region contributed by a component). */
62: USTRUCT(BlueprintType)
63: struct FRoadPolygonData
64: {
65: 	GENERATED_BODY()
66: 
67: 	/** In World space */
68: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, BlueprintReadWrite, Category = RoadPolygonData)
69: 	TArray<FVector> Vertices{};
70: 
71: 	/**
72: 	 * Inner contours carved out of Vertices (world space, same winding-agnostic ring convention). Empty => a solid
73: 	 * fill, bit for bit as before.
74: 	 *
75: 	 * A SEPARATE array and not more entries in Vertices, on purpose: VertexHeights is 1:1 with Vertices,
76: 	 * MagneticSpans index into it, and CurbCuts are arc-length offsets ALONG it. All three are properties of the
77: 	 * OUTER ring only and would be silently corrupted by appending hole vertices to it.
78: 	 */
79: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, BlueprintReadWrite, Category = RoadPolygonData)
80: 	TArray<FRoadPolygonRing> Holes{};
81: 
82: 	/** Optional per-vertex surface height [cm], 1:1 with Vertices. Empty => flat zone DefaultHeight (today's
83: 	 *  behaviour). Consumed by FProceduralPolygon_Simple to ramp a sidewalk fill between per-node heights. */
84: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, BlueprintReadWrite, Category = RoadPolygonData)
85: 	TArray<double> VertexHeights{};
86: 
87: 	/** Magnetized boundary spans (index ranges into Vertices) that should reuse road-lane arrangement geometry
88: 	 *  instead of the sampled Vertices. Empty => the whole ring is triangulated from Vertices as today. */
89: 	UPROPERTY()
90: 	TArray<FMagneticSpan> MagneticSpans;
91: 
92: 	/** Curb cuts placed on this fill's boundary ring, by arc length along Vertices. The producer has already
93: 	 *  baked them into VertexHeights (so the curb follows); these drive the INTERIOR ramp and the extra

121: 	TSoftObjectPtr<class URoadMarkProfile> Profile;
122: 
123: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = MarkProfile, meta = (InlineEditConditionToggle))
124: 	bool bOverrideMaterial = false;
125: 
126: 	/** Override default material from profile */
127: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = MarkProfile, meta = (EditCondition = "ProfileSource == ERoadLaneMarkProfile::UsePreset && bOverrideMaterial", EditConditionHides))
128: 	TObjectPtr<UMaterialInterface> OverrideMaterial = nullptr;
129: 
130: 	/**
131: 	 * Use a custom profile if ProfileSource = ERoadLaneMarkProfile::UseCustom.
132: 	 * ATTENTION BUG: If this option is disabled in UI, just reselect the item. This is a UE bug. Hope it will be fixed in future versions of UE
133: 	 */
134: 	UPROPERTY(EditAnywhere, Category = MarkProfile, Meta = (ExcludeBaseStruct, EditCondition = "ProfileSource == ERoadLaneMarkProfile::UseCustom", EditConditionHides))
135: 	TInstancedStruct<FRoadLaneMarkProfile> CustomProfile;
136:

139: 	const TInstancedStruct<FRoadLaneMarkProfile>& GetProfile() const;

24: 
25: void FRoadTriangulationData::AddDebugLines(const TArray<FIndex2i>& InBoundaries, const FColor& Color, float Thickness)
26: {
27: 	FDebugLineBuffer::FBatch Batch;
28: 	Batch.Color = Color;
29: 	Batch.Thickness = Thickness;
30: 
31: 	for (auto& Ind : InBoundaries)
32: 	{
33: 		Batch.Lines.Add({ Vertices3d[Ind.A].Vertex, Vertices3d[Ind.B].Vertex });
34: 	}
35: 
36: 	DebugDraw.Add(MoveTemp(Batch));
37: }
38: 
39: void FRoadTriangulationData::BuildBoundaryVertexIndex()
40: {
41: 	bBoundaryVertex.Init(false, Vertices3d.Num());
42: 	for (const TArray<FIndex2i>& Boundary : Boundaries)
43: 	{
44: 		for (const FIndex2i& Edge : Boundary)
45: 		{
46: 			if (bBoundaryVertex.IsValidIndex(Edge.A)) { bBoundaryVertex[Edge.A] = true; }
47: 			if (bBoundaryVertex.IsValidIndex(Edge.B)) { bBoundaryVertex[Edge.B] = true; }
48: 		}
49: 	}
50: }
51: 
52: void FRoadTriangulationData::BuildClaimedTriangleIndex()
53: {
54: 	bClaimedTriangle.Init(false, Triangles.Num());
55: 	for (const TUniquePtr<FProceduralPolygon>& Poly : Polygons)
56: 	{
57: 		for (int32 TID : Poly->TrianglesIDs)
58: 		{
59: 			if (bClaimedTriangle.IsValidIndex(TID))
60: 			{
61: 				bClaimedTriangle[TID] = true;
62: 			}
63: 		}
64: 	}
65: }
66: 
67: bool FRoadTriangulationData::IsTriangleClaimed(int TID) const
68: {
69: 	return bClaimedTriangle.IsValidIndex(TID) && bClaimedTriangle[TID];
70: }
71: 
72: bool FRoadTriangulationData::IsBoundaryVertex(int VID) const
73: {
74: 	// The index is built once, right after Boundaries is final. Before that -- and only then -- fall back to
75: 	// the scan, so a caller that runs mid-build still gets the right answer instead of a silent "false".
76: 	if (bBoundaryVertex.Num() > 0)
77: 	{
78: 		return bBoundaryVertex.IsValidIndex(VID) && bBoundaryVertex[VID];
79: 	}
80: 
81: 	for (auto& Boundary : Boundaries)
82: 	{
83: 		if (Boundary.FindByPredicate([VID](const FIndex2i& Index) { return Index.A == VID || Index.B == VID; }))
84: 		{
85: 			return true;
86: 		}
87: 	}
88: 	return false;
89: }
90: 
91: void FSpatialQuery::Build(TConstArrayView<FArrangementVertex3d> Vertices3d, const TArray<FIndex3i>& Triangles)
92: {
93: 	// Idempotent: clear any previous geometry so Build() can be called more than once on the same query
94: 	// (the Snap to Ground pass re-finalizes the geometry on the game thread after overriding Z).
95: 	Mesh3d.Clear();
96: 	Mesh2d.Clear();
---
144: bool FRoadTriangulationData::IsInsideMesh(const FVector2D& Point, double TolCm) const
145: {
146: 	return Spatial.IsInside(Point, TolCm * TolCm);
147: }
148: 
149: void FRoadTriangulationData::FinalizeVertexGeometry(bool bCotanSmoothZ, float SmoothSpeed, float Smoothness,
150:                                                     bool bRebuildSpatial, FProgressCancel* Progress)
151: {
152: 
153: 	const int32 NumVerts = Vertices3d.Num();
154: 
155: 	// ========================== Create DynamicMesh and Compute normals ==========================
156: 	// THE MESH IS BUILT WITHOUT THE EDGE PROFILE -- Z + ProfileDrop is the surface exactly as it was before
157: 	// ApplyEdgeProfile subtracted the gutter, and the drop is put back after the smoothing below.
158: 	//
159: 	// The cotan pass is a low-pass filter over Z, and the gutter is precisely the kind of sharp, deliberately
160: 	// authored Z field it erases: BoundarySmoothAlpha = 0 pins the kerb (already at full depth) and the end caps
161: 	// (which carry no drop, being exempt from the distance measurement), then drags the interior between them.
162: 	// The carriageway therefore sags to gutter depth wherever nothing pins it and stays up near the caps, which
163: 	// reads as the cross-section varying ALONG S. And because Finalize only asks for smoothing when the scope
164: 	// holds MORE THAN ONE spline, the symptom appeared to be "a second, attribute-less road bends its
165: 	// neighbour's profile" -- the neighbour's geometry, attributes and connections never entered into it.
166: 	//
167: 	// ProfileDrop is 0 on every vertex whenever the feature is off, so this path stays bit-identical there.
168: 	bool bZWillChange = false;
169: 	FDynamicMesh3 DynamicMesh(true, false, false, false);
170: 	for (int VID = 0; VID < NumVerts; ++VID)
171: 	{
172: 		const FArrangementVertex3d& V = Vertices3d[VID];
173: 		bZWillChange |= (V.ProfileDrop != 0.0);
174: 		int32 NewVID = DynamicMesh.AppendVertex(FVector3d(V.Vertex.X, V.Vertex.Y, V.Vertex.Z + V.ProfileDrop));
175: 		check(NewVID == VID);
176: 	}
177: 	for (int TID = 0; TID < Triangles.Num(); ++TID)
178: 	{
179: 		DynamicMesh.InsertTriangle(TID, Triangles[TID]);
180: 	}
181: 	FMeshNormals::QuickComputeVertexNormals(DynamicMesh);
182: 	ROADOP_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
183: 
184: 	// ========================== CotanSmoothingOp ==========================
185: 	if (bCotanSmoothZ)
186: 	{
187: 		FSmoothingOpBase::FOptions SmoothingOptions;
188: 		SmoothingOptions.SmoothAlpha = SmoothSpeed;
189: 		SmoothingOptions.BoundarySmoothAlpha = 0.0;
190: 		double NonlinearT = FMathd::Pow(Smoothness, 2.0);
191: 		// this is an empirically-determined hack that seems to work OK to normalize the smoothing result for variable vertex count...
192: 		double ScaledPower = (NonlinearT / 50.0) * NumVerts;
193: 		SmoothingOptions.SmoothPower = ScaledPower;
194: 		SmoothingOptions.bUniform = false;
195: 		SmoothingOptions.bUseImplicit = true;
196: 		SmoothingOptions.NormalOffset = 0.0f;
197: 
198: 		FCotanSmoothingOp SmoothingOp(&DynamicMesh, SmoothingOptions);
199: 		SmoothingOp.CalculateResult(Progress);
200: 		ROADOP_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
201: 
202: 		auto SmoothedMesh = SmoothingOp.ExtractResult();
203: 		if (SmoothedMesh && SmoothedMesh->VertexCount() == DynamicMesh.VertexCount())
204: 		{
205: 			// Smoothed height MINUS the gutter: the profile is authored on top of the resolved surface, so it
206: 			// survives the filter instead of being averaged away by it.
207: 			for (int VID = 0; VID < NumVerts; ++VID)
208: 			{
209: 				Vertices3d[VID].Vertex.Z = SmoothedMesh->GetVertexRef(VID).Z - Vertices3d[VID].ProfileDrop;
210: 			}
211: 			bZWillChange = true;
212: 			ROADOP_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
213: 		}
214: 		else
215: 		{
216: 			ResultInfo.AddWarning({ 0, LOCTEXT("CalculateResultFail_Smoothing", "FRoadTriangulationData: Can't smooth mesh") });
217: 		}
218: 	}
219: 
220: 	// ========================== Set vertex normals ==========================
221: 	// Normals come from the FINAL surface -- smoothing AND the edge profile. The mesh still holds the heights it
222: 	// was built from: FSmoothingOpBase works on its own copy and never writes back, so the second
223: 	// QuickComputeVertexNormals call this replaces was re-deriving the same normals from the unsmoothed mesh.
224: 	if (bZWillChange)
225: 	{
226: 		for (int VID = 0; VID < NumVerts; ++VID)
227: 		{
228: 			DynamicMesh.SetVertex(VID, Vertices3d[VID].Vertex);
229: 		}
230: 		FMeshNormals::QuickComputeVertexNormals(DynamicMesh);
231: 		ROADOP_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
232: 	}
233: 
234: 	for (int VID = 0; VID < NumVerts; ++VID)
235: 	{
236: 		auto Normal = DynamicMesh.GetVertexNormal(VID);
237: 		Vertices3d[VID].Normal = FVector{ Normal.X, Normal.Y, Normal.Z };
238: 	}
239: 	ROADOP_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
240: 
241: 	// ========================== AABBTree ==========================
242: 	if (bRebuildSpatial)
243: 	{
244: 		Spatial.Build(Vertices3d.View(), Triangles);
---
145: {
146: 	return Spatial.IsInside(Point, TolCm * TolCm);
147: }
148: 
149: void FRoadTriangulationData::FinalizeVertexGeometry(bool bCotanSmoothZ, float SmoothSpeed, float Smoothness,
150:                                                     bool bRebuildSpatial, FProgressCancel* Progress)
151: {
152: 
153: 	const int32 NumVerts = Vertices3d.Num();
154: 
155: 	// ========================== Create DynamicMesh and Compute normals ==========================
156: 	// THE MESH IS BUILT WITHOUT THE EDGE PROFILE -- Z + ProfileDrop is the surface exactly as it was before
157: 	// ApplyEdgeProfile subtracted the gutter, and the drop is put back after the smoothing below.
158: 	//
159: 	// The cotan pass is a low-pass filter over Z, and the gutter is precisely the kind of sharp, deliberately
160: 	// authored Z field it erases: BoundarySmoothAlpha = 0 pins the kerb (already at full depth) and the end caps
161: 	// (which carry no drop, being exempt from the distance measurement), then drags the interior between them.
162: 	// The carriageway therefore sags to gutter depth wherever nothing pins it and stays up near the caps, which
163: 	// reads as the cross-section varying ALONG S. And because Finalize only asks for smoothing when the scope
164: 	// holds MORE THAN ONE spline, the symptom appeared to be "a second, attribute-less road bends its
165: 	// neighbour's profile" -- the neighbour's geometry, attributes and connections never entered into it.
166: 	//
167: 	// ProfileDrop is 0 on every vertex whenever the feature is off, so this path stays bit-identical there.
168: 	bool bZWillChange = false;
169: 	FDynamicMesh3 DynamicMesh(true, false, false, false);
170: 	for (int VID = 0; VID < NumVerts; ++VID)
171: 	{
172: 		const FArrangementVertex3d& V = Vertices3d[VID];
173: 		bZWillChange |= (V.ProfileDrop != 0.0);
174: 		int32 NewVID = DynamicMesh.AppendVertex(FVector3d(V.Vertex.X, V.Vertex.Y, V.Vertex.Z + V.ProfileDrop));
175: 		check(NewVID == VID);
176: 	}
177: 	for (int TID = 0; TID < Triangles.Num(); ++TID)
178: 	{
179: 		DynamicMesh.InsertTriangle(TID, Triangles[TID]);
180: 	}
181: 	FMeshNormals::QuickComputeVertexNormals(DynamicMesh);
182: 	ROADOP_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
183: 
184: 	// ========================== CotanSmoothingOp ==========================
185: 	if (bCotanSmoothZ)
186: 	{
187: 		FSmoothingOpBase::FOptions SmoothingOptions;
188: 		SmoothingOptions.SmoothAlpha = SmoothSpeed;
189: 		SmoothingOptions.BoundarySmoothAlpha = 0.0;
190: 		double NonlinearT = FMathd::Pow(Smoothness, 2.0);
191: 		// this is an empirically-determined hack that seems to work OK to normalize the smoothing result for variable vertex count...
192: 		double ScaledPower = (NonlinearT / 50.0) * NumVerts;
193: 		SmoothingOptions.SmoothPower = ScaledPower;
194: 		SmoothingOptions.bUniform = false;
195: 		SmoothingOptions.bUseImplicit = true;
196: 		SmoothingOptions.NormalOffset = 0.0f;
197: 
198: 		FCotanSmoothingOp SmoothingOp(&DynamicMesh, SmoothingOptions);
199: 		SmoothingOp.CalculateResult(Progress);
200: 		ROADOP_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
201: 
202: 		auto SmoothedMesh = SmoothingOp.ExtractResult();
203: 		if (SmoothedMesh && SmoothedMesh->VertexCount() == DynamicMesh.VertexCount())
204: 		{
205: 			// Smoothed height MINUS the gutter: the profile is authored on top of the resolved surface, so it
206: 			// survives the filter instead of being averaged away by it.
207: 			for (int VID = 0; VID < NumVerts; ++VID)
208: 			{
209: 				Vertices3d[VID].Vertex.Z = SmoothedMesh->GetVertexRef(VID).Z - Vertices3d[VID].ProfileDrop;
210: 			}
211: 			bZWillChange = true;
212: 			ROADOP_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
213: 		}
214: 		else
215: 		{
216: 			ResultInfo.AddWarning({ 0, LOCTEXT("CalculateResultFail_Smoothing", "FRoadTriangulationData: Can't smooth mesh") });
217: 		}
218: 	}
219: 
220: 	// ========================== Set vertex normals ==========================
221: 	// Normals come from the FINAL surface -- smoothing AND the edge profile. The mesh still holds the heights it
222: 	// was built from: FSmoothingOpBase works on its own copy and never writes back, so the second
223: 	// QuickComputeVertexNormals call this replaces was re-deriving the same normals from the unsmoothed mesh.
224: 	if (bZWillChange)
225: 	{
226: 		for (int VID = 0; VID < NumVerts; ++VID)
227: 		{
228: 			DynamicMesh.SetVertex(VID, Vertices3d[VID].Vertex);
229: 		}
230: 		FMeshNormals::QuickComputeVertexNormals(DynamicMesh);
231: 		ROADOP_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
232: 	}
233: 
234: 	for (int VID = 0; VID < NumVerts; ++VID)
235: 	{
236: 		auto Normal = DynamicMesh.GetVertexNormal(VID);
237: 		Vertices3d[VID].Normal = FVector{ Normal.X, Normal.Y, Normal.Z };
238: 	}
239: 	ROADOP_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
240: 
241: 	// ========================== AABBTree ==========================
242: 	if (bRebuildSpatial)
243: 	{
244: 		Spatial.Build(Vertices3d.View(), Triangles);
245: 		ROADOP_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
---
297: 	//
298: 	// Only ends leaving the scope are recorded: a connection to a spline inside the same scope already shares
299: 	// its vertices, so its profile resolves through the ordinary contour attribution.
300: 	for (int32 SplineIndex = 0; SplineIndex < Splines.Num(); ++SplineIndex)
301: 	{
302: 		const URoadSplineComponent* Spline = Splines[SplineIndex];
303: 		auto SnapshotEnd = [&](const URoadConnection* Connection, bool bAtStart)
304: 		{
305: 			if (!Connection || !Connection->IsConnected())
306: 			{
307: 				return;
308: 			}
309: 			const ULaneConnection* Outer = Connection->GetOuterConnection();
310: 			const URoadSplineComponent* Far = Outer ? Outer->GetOwnedRoadSpline() : nullptr;
311: 			if (!Far || Splines.Contains(Far))
312: 			{
313: 				return; // unresolved, or inside this scope -- nothing to hand over
314: 			}
315: 
316: 			// The far road's profile at the joint: its own end nearest to us. Which end that is does not matter
317: 			// for the value as long as the attribute is constant there, and taking the connected section is the
318: 			// closest thing to "at the joint" we can name without projecting geometry.
319: 			const FRoadLayout& FarLayout = Far->GetRoadLayout();
320: 			const int32 FarSectionIndex = FMath::Clamp(Outer->GetSectionIndex(), 0, FarLayout.Sections.Num() - 1);
321: 			if (!FarLayout.Sections.IsValidIndex(FarSectionIndex))
322: 			{
323: 				return;
324: 			}
325: 			for (const TPair<TSoftClassPtr<URoadLaneAttributeDescriptor>, FRoadLaneAttribute>& It : FarLayout.Sections[FarSectionIndex].Attributes)
326: 			{
327: 				if (It.Value.IsChildOf<FRoadLaneAttributeCrossSectionValue>())
328: 				{
329: 					// Explicitly Depth = 0 -- see MakeEdgeProfileOff: the "no profile" sentinel must not inherit
330: 					// FRoadEdgeProfileParams::Depth's authoring default.
331: 					static const FRoadLaneAttributeCrossSectionValue Off = MakeEdgeProfileOff();
332: 					FRoadExternalEdgeProfile Entry;
333: 					Entry.SplineIndex = SplineIndex;
334: 					Entry.bAtStart = bAtStart;
335: 					Entry.Params = It.Value.Evaluate(bAtStart ? 0.0 : Far->GetSplineLength(), Off).Profile;
336: 					Result->Inputs.ExternalEdgeProfiles.Add(Entry);
337: 					break;
338: 				}
339: 			}
340: 		};
341: 		SnapshotEnd(Spline->GetPredecessorConnection(), /*bAtStart*/ true);
342: 		SnapshotEnd(Spline->GetSuccessorConnection(), /*bAtStart*/ false);
343: 	}
344: 
345: 	TArray<UActorComponent*> RoadPolygons = Actor->GetComponentsByInterface(URoadFeatureInterface::StaticClass());
346: 	for (auto& It : RoadPolygons)
347: 	{
348: 		if (!IsInGroup(It))
349: 		{
350: 			continue;
351: 		}
352: 		if (const auto* Polygon = Cast<IRoadFeatureInterface>(It))
353: 		{
354: 			// Root the producer BEFORE reading it: what it hands us below are UObject pointers inside plain structs,
355: 			// invisible to GC (see FRoadTriangulationData::FeatureComponents). The component owns them all, so this
356: 			// one ref keeps the whole contribution alive until the sweep ops have consumed it.
357: 			Result->Inputs.FeatureComponents.Emplace(It);
358: 
359: 			TArray<FRoadPolygonData> Polygons;
360: 			Polygon->GeneratePolygons(Polygons);
361: 			Result->Inputs.SimplePolygons.Append(MoveTemp(Polygons));
362: 
363: 			// Profile-follows-spline feature lines (curbs/marks). Runs on this worker thread, so the component
364: 			// copies soft profile refs only; the consuming ops resolve them in their constructors, on the game thread.
365: 			TArray<FRoadFeatureLine> Lines;
366: 			Polygon->GenerateFeatureLines(Lines);
367: 			Result->Inputs.FeatureLines.Append(MoveTemp(Lines));
368: 		}
369: 	}
370: 
371: }

373: void FRoadTriangulationOp::CalculateResult(FProgressCancel* Progress)
374: {
375: 	//SCOPE_LOG_TIME_IN_SECONDS(TEXT("    Full time"), nullptr);
376: 
377: 	// THIS FUNCTION IS THE ORDER. Every stage body lives in MetaRoad::BuildStages (RoadBuildStages.h), which
378: 	// documents what each one may assume about the ones before it -- the order is load-bearing in ways that are
379: 	// not visible from here. A stage returns false when the build must stop; Data.ResultInfo already carries
380: 	// whether that was a cancel or a failure, so there is nothing to plumb.
381: 	using namespace MetaRoad::BuildStages;
382: 
383: 	Result->ResultInfo.Result = EGeometryResultType::InProgress;
384: 	Result->Params = Params;
385: 
386: 	if (!Initialize(*Result, Progress)) { return; }
387: 
388: 	// Arrange -- THE OPEN PHASE, and this brace is what it is. Inserting requires a FRoadArrangementBuilder, the
389: 	// builder exists only in here, and Close() (which sizes the vertex table and so establishes the hug contract)
390: 	// is the last thing it does. Order inside is load-bearing: a later insert splits the edges already present.
391: 	{
392: 		FRoadArrangementBuilder Builder(*Result, Build.VertexSnapTol);
393: 
394: 		if (!AddLanePolygons(*Result, Builder, Build, Progress)) { return; }
395: 		if (!AddLoopPolygons(*Result, Builder, Progress)) { return; }
396: 		if (!AddSimplePolygons(*Result, Builder, Progress)) { return; }
397: 		if (!InsertWeldedFeatureLines(*Result, Builder, Progress)) { return; }
---
305: 			if (!Connection || !Connection->IsConnected())
306: 			{
307: 				return;
308: 			}
309: 			const ULaneConnection* Outer = Connection->GetOuterConnection();
310: 			const URoadSplineComponent* Far = Outer ? Outer->GetOwnedRoadSpline() : nullptr;
311: 			if (!Far || Splines.Contains(Far))
312: 			{
313: 				return; // unresolved, or inside this scope -- nothing to hand over
314: 			}
315: 
316: 			// The far road's profile at the joint: its own end nearest to us. Which end that is does not matter
317: 			// for the value as long as the attribute is constant there, and taking the connected section is the
318: 			// closest thing to "at the joint" we can name without projecting geometry.
319: 			const FRoadLayout& FarLayout = Far->GetRoadLayout();
320: 			const int32 FarSectionIndex = FMath::Clamp(Outer->GetSectionIndex(), 0, FarLayout.Sections.Num() - 1);
321: 			if (!FarLayout.Sections.IsValidIndex(FarSectionIndex))
322: 			{
323: 				return;
324: 			}
325: 			for (const TPair<TSoftClassPtr<URoadLaneAttributeDescriptor>, FRoadLaneAttribute>& It : FarLayout.Sections[FarSectionIndex].Attributes)
326: 			{
327: 				if (It.Value.IsChildOf<FRoadLaneAttributeCrossSectionValue>())
328: 				{
329: 					// Explicitly Depth = 0 -- see MakeEdgeProfileOff: the "no profile" sentinel must not inherit
330: 					// FRoadEdgeProfileParams::Depth's authoring default.
331: 					static const FRoadLaneAttributeCrossSectionValue Off = MakeEdgeProfileOff();
332: 					FRoadExternalEdgeProfile Entry;
333: 					Entry.SplineIndex = SplineIndex;
334: 					Entry.bAtStart = bAtStart;
335: 					Entry.Params = It.Value.Evaluate(bAtStart ? 0.0 : Far->GetSplineLength(), Off).Profile;
336: 					Result->Inputs.ExternalEdgeProfiles.Add(Entry);
337: 					break;
338: 				}
339: 			}
340: 		};
341: 		SnapshotEnd(Spline->GetPredecessorConnection(), /*bAtStart*/ true);
342: 		SnapshotEnd(Spline->GetSuccessorConnection(), /*bAtStart*/ false);
343: 	}
344: 
345: 	TArray<UActorComponent*> RoadPolygons = Actor->GetComponentsByInterface(URoadFeatureInterface::StaticClass());
346: 	for (auto& It : RoadPolygons)
347: 	{
348: 		if (!IsInGroup(It))
349: 		{
350: 			continue;
351: 		}
352: 		if (const auto* Polygon = Cast<IRoadFeatureInterface>(It))
353: 		{
354: 			// Root the producer BEFORE reading it: what it hands us below are UObject pointers inside plain structs,
355: 			// invisible to GC (see FRoadTriangulationData::FeatureComponents). The component owns them all, so this
356: 			// one ref keeps the whole contribution alive until the sweep ops have consumed it.
357: 			Result->Inputs.FeatureComponents.Emplace(It);
358: 
359: 			TArray<FRoadPolygonData> Polygons;
360: 			Polygon->GeneratePolygons(Polygons);
361: 			Result->Inputs.SimplePolygons.Append(MoveTemp(Polygons));
362: 
363: 			// Profile-follows-spline feature lines (curbs/marks). Runs on this worker thread, so the component
364: 			// copies soft profile refs only; the consuming ops resolve them in their constructors, on the game thread.
365: 			TArray<FRoadFeatureLine> Lines;
366: 			Polygon->GenerateFeatureLines(Lines);
367: 			Result->Inputs.FeatureLines.Append(MoveTemp(Lines));
368: 		}
369: 	}

377: 	// THIS FUNCTION IS THE ORDER. Every stage body lives in MetaRoad::BuildStages (RoadBuildStages.h), which
378: 	// documents what each one may assume about the ones before it -- the order is load-bearing in ways that are
379: 	// not visible from here. A stage returns false when the build must stop; Data.ResultInfo already carries
380: 	// whether that was a cancel or a failure, so there is nothing to plumb.

383: 	Result->ResultInfo.Result = EGeometryResultType::InProgress;

401: 		Builder.Close();

405: 	if (!CompleteArrangement(*Result, Progress)) { return; }

373: void FRoadTriangulationOp::CalculateResult(FProgressCancel* Progress)
374: {
375: 	//SCOPE_LOG_TIME_IN_SECONDS(TEXT("    Full time"), nullptr);
376: 
377: 	// THIS FUNCTION IS THE ORDER. Every stage body lives in MetaRoad::BuildStages (RoadBuildStages.h), which
378: 	// documents what each one may assume about the ones before it -- the order is load-bearing in ways that are
379: 	// not visible from here. A stage returns false when the build must stop; Data.ResultInfo already carries
380: 	// whether that was a cancel or a failure, so there is nothing to plumb.
381: 	using namespace MetaRoad::BuildStages;
382: 
383: 	Result->ResultInfo.Result = EGeometryResultType::InProgress;
384: 	Result->Params = Params;
385: 
386: 	if (!Initialize(*Result, Progress)) { return; }
387: 
388: 	// Arrange -- THE OPEN PHASE, and this brace is what it is. Inserting requires a FRoadArrangementBuilder, the
389: 	// builder exists only in here, and Close() (which sizes the vertex table and so establishes the hug contract)
390: 	// is the last thing it does. Order inside is load-bearing: a later insert splits the edges already present.
391: 	{
392: 		FRoadArrangementBuilder Builder(*Result, Build.VertexSnapTol);
393: 
394: 		if (!AddLanePolygons(*Result, Builder, Build, Progress)) { return; }
395: 		if (!AddLoopPolygons(*Result, Builder, Progress)) { return; }
396: 		if (!AddSimplePolygons(*Result, Builder, Progress)) { return; }
397: 		if (!InsertWeldedFeatureLines(*Result, Builder, Progress)) { return; }
398: 		if (!MatchMagnetizedFeatureLines(*Result, Builder, Progress)) { return; }
399: 		if (!ConditionBoundaries(*Result, Builder, Progress)) { return; }
400: 
401: 		Builder.Close();
402: 	}
403: 
404: 	// Lift + recover. After this the graph's edges are final and everything derived from them is valid.
405: 	if (!CompleteArrangement(*Result, Progress)) { return; }
406: 	if (!RefineSurfaces(*Result, Progress)) { return; }
407: 
---
311: 			if (!Far || Splines.Contains(Far))
312: 			{
313: 				return; // unresolved, or inside this scope -- nothing to hand over
314: 			}
315: 
316: 			// The far road's profile at the joint: its own end nearest to us. Which end that is does not matter
317: 			// for the value as long as the attribute is constant there, and taking the connected section is the
318: 			// closest thing to "at the joint" we can name without projecting geometry.
319: 			const FRoadLayout& FarLayout = Far->GetRoadLayout();
320: 			const int32 FarSectionIndex = FMath::Clamp(Outer->GetSectionIndex(), 0, FarLayout.Sections.Num() - 1);
321: 			if (!FarLayout.Sections.IsValidIndex(FarSectionIndex))
322: 			{
323: 				return;
324: 			}
325: 			for (const TPair<TSoftClassPtr<URoadLaneAttributeDescriptor>, FRoadLaneAttribute>& It : FarLayout.Sections[FarSectionIndex].Attributes)
326: 			{
327: 				if (It.Value.IsChildOf<FRoadLaneAttributeCrossSectionValue>())
328: 				{
329: 					// Explicitly Depth = 0 -- see MakeEdgeProfileOff: the "no profile" sentinel must not inherit
330: 					// FRoadEdgeProfileParams::Depth's authoring default.
331: 					static const FRoadLaneAttributeCrossSectionValue Off = MakeEdgeProfileOff();
332: 					FRoadExternalEdgeProfile Entry;
333: 					Entry.SplineIndex = SplineIndex;
334: 					Entry.bAtStart = bAtStart;
335: 					Entry.Params = It.Value.Evaluate(bAtStart ? 0.0 : Far->GetSplineLength(), Off).Profile;
336: 					Result->Inputs.ExternalEdgeProfiles.Add(Entry);
337: 					break;
338: 				}
339: 			}
340: 		};
341: 		SnapshotEnd(Spline->GetPredecessorConnection(), /*bAtStart*/ true);
342: 		SnapshotEnd(Spline->GetSuccessorConnection(), /*bAtStart*/ false);
343: 	}
344: 
345: 	TArray<UActorComponent*> RoadPolygons = Actor->GetComponentsByInterface(URoadFeatureInterface::StaticClass());
346: 	for (auto& It : RoadPolygons)
347: 	{
348: 		if (!IsInGroup(It))
349: 		{
350: 			continue;
351: 		}
352: 		if (const auto* Polygon = Cast<IRoadFeatureInterface>(It))
353: 		{
354: 			// Root the producer BEFORE reading it: what it hands us below are UObject pointers inside plain structs,
355: 			// invisible to GC (see FRoadTriangulationData::FeatureComponents). The component owns them all, so this
356: 			// one ref keeps the whole contribution alive until the sweep ops have consumed it.
357: 			Result->Inputs.FeatureComponents.Emplace(It);
358: 
359: 			TArray<FRoadPolygonData> Polygons;
360: 			Polygon->GeneratePolygons(Polygons);
361: 			Result->Inputs.SimplePolygons.Append(MoveTemp(Polygons));
362: 
363: 			// Profile-follows-spline feature lines (curbs/marks). Runs on this worker thread, so the component
364: 			// copies soft profile refs only; the consuming ops resolve them in their constructors, on the game thread.
365: 			TArray<FRoadFeatureLine> Lines;
366: 			Polygon->GenerateFeatureLines(Lines);
367: 			Result->Inputs.FeatureLines.Append(MoveTemp(Lines));
368: 		}
369: 	}
370: 
371: }
372: 
373: void FRoadTriangulationOp::CalculateResult(FProgressCancel* Progress)
374: {
375: 	//SCOPE_LOG_TIME_IN_SECONDS(TEXT("    Full time"), nullptr);
376: 
377: 	// THIS FUNCTION IS THE ORDER. Every stage body lives in MetaRoad::BuildStages (RoadBuildStages.h), which
378: 	// documents what each one may assume about the ones before it -- the order is load-bearing in ways that are
379: 	// not visible from here. A stage returns false when the build must stop; Data.ResultInfo already carries
380: 	// whether that was a cancel or a failure, so there is nothing to plumb.
381: 	using namespace MetaRoad::BuildStages;
382: 
383: 	Result->ResultInfo.Result = EGeometryResultType::InProgress;
384: 	Result->Params = Params;
385: 
386: 	if (!Initialize(*Result, Progress)) { return; }
387: 
388: 	// Arrange -- THE OPEN PHASE, and this brace is what it is. Inserting requires a FRoadArrangementBuilder, the
389: 	// builder exists only in here, and Close() (which sizes the vertex table and so establishes the hug contract)
390: 	// is the last thing it does. Order inside is load-bearing: a later insert splits the edges already present.
391: 	{
392: 		FRoadArrangementBuilder Builder(*Result, Build.VertexSnapTol);
393: 
394: 		if (!AddLanePolygons(*Result, Builder, Build, Progress)) { return; }
395: 		if (!AddLoopPolygons(*Result, Builder, Progress)) { return; }
396: 		if (!AddSimplePolygons(*Result, Builder, Progress)) { return; }
397: 		if (!InsertWeldedFeatureLines(*Result, Builder, Progress)) { return; }
398: 		if (!MatchMagnetizedFeatureLines(*Result, Builder, Progress)) { return; }
399: 		if (!ConditionBoundaries(*Result, Builder, Progress)) { return; }
400: 
401: 		Builder.Close();
402: 	}
403: 
404: 	// Lift + recover. After this the graph's edges are final and everything derived from them is valid.
405: 	if (!CompleteArrangement(*Result, Progress)) { return; }
406: 	if (!RefineSurfaces(*Result, Progress)) { return; }
407: 
408: 	// Triangulate + claim. The Delaunay is kept alive across both: the claim re-fills per polygon against it.
409: 	if (!FindBoundaries(*Result, Progress)) { return; }
410: 
411: 	FDelaunay2 Delaunay;
---
399: 		if (!ConditionBoundaries(*Result, Builder, Progress)) { return; }
400: 
401: 		Builder.Close();
402: 	}
403: 
404: 	// Lift + recover. After this the graph's edges are final and everything derived from them is valid.
405: 	if (!CompleteArrangement(*Result, Progress)) { return; }
406: 	if (!RefineSurfaces(*Result, Progress)) { return; }
407: 
408: 	// Triangulate + claim. The Delaunay is kept alive across both: the claim re-fills per polygon against it.
409: 	if (!FindBoundaries(*Result, Progress)) { return; }
410: 
411: 	FDelaunay2 Delaunay;
412: 	if (!Triangulate(*Result, Delaunay, Progress)) { return; }
413: 	if (!ClaimTriangles(*Result, Delaunay, Progress)) { return; }
414: 
415: 	// Resolve the one 3D surface. bHasSurfaceInfo is threaded rather than stored: it is a fact about THIS
416: 	// height pass (which vertices may drive Z), not about the mesh, and nothing downstream has any use for it.
417: 	// Curb cuts, once the vertex set is final: a ramp belongs to a PLACE, so it is applied to every vertex in
418: 	// reach rather than by whichever polygon authored it (which stopped at the seam with its neighbour).
419: 	if (!ApplyCurbCuts(*Result, Progress)) { return; }
420: 
421: 	TArray<bool> bHasSurfaceInfo;
422: 	if (!ResolveHeight(*Result, Build, bHasSurfaceInfo, Progress)) { return; }
423: 	if (!BlendOverlaps(*Result, Build, bHasSurfaceInfo, Progress)) { return; }
424: 	if (!ApplyEdgeProfile(*Result, Progress)) { return; }
425: 	if (!Finalize(*Result, Build, Progress)) { return; }
426: 
427: 	// Last, and only on a build that got this far: a hash of what came out, so a refactor can be A/B'd
428: 	// against the build before it instead of against a screenshot. Off unless meta.road.BuildFingerprint.
429: 	BuildFingerprint::LogBaseMesh(*Result);
430: 
431: 	Result->ResultInfo.SetSuccess();
432: }
433: 
434: #undef LOCTEXT_NAMESPACE
---

=== Source\MetaRoadEditor\Private\RoadMeshBuild\Ops\RoadBuildStages.cpp ===
596: 			}
597: 		}
598: 	}
599: 
600: 	// Claims are final from here on. RegionUtils::GetFilledIslandTriangles used to answer "did anyone claim
601: 	// this?" by scanning every polygon's TrianglesIDs for every triangle -- quadratic, and run twice per build.
602: 	Data.BuildClaimedTriangleIndex();
603: 
604: 	int32 NumUnclaimed = 0;
605: 	for (int32 TID = 0; TID < Data.Triangles.Num(); ++TID)
606: 	{
607: 		NumUnclaimed += Data.IsTriangleClaimed(TID) ? 0 : 1;
608: 	}
609: 	UE_LOG(LogMetaRoad, Log, TEXT("MetaRoad build: %d triangles, %d claims, %d unmatched, %d unclaimed triangles"),
610: 		Data.Triangles.Num(), NumClaimed, NumUnmatched, NumUnclaimed);
611: 
612: 	ROADBUILD_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
613: 	return true;
614: }
615: 
616: bool ApplyCurbCuts(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, FProgressCancel* Progress)
617: {
618: 	if (Data.CurbCutSites.Num() == 0)
619: 	{
620: 		return true; // bit-for-bit the build before the feature
621: 	}
622: 
623: 	checkf(Data.Vertices3d.MatchesGraph(Data.GetGraph()),
624: 		TEXT("ApplyCurbCuts: vertex table (%d) does not match the graph (%d)"),
625: 		Data.Vertices3d.Num(), Data.GetGraph().MaxVertexID());
626: 
627: 	const FDynamicGraph2d& Graph = Data.GetGraph();
628: 
629: 	int32 NumTouched = 0;
630: 	double DeepestScale = 1.0;
631: 
632: 	for (int32 VID = 0; VID < Data.Vertices3d.Num(); ++VID)
633: 	{
634: 		if (!Graph.IsVertex(VID))
635: 		{
636: 			continue;
637: 		}
638: 
639: 		const FVector2d V2d = Graph.GetVertex(VID);
640: 		const FVector2D Query(V2d.X, V2d.Y);
641: 		const double ScaleBefore = Data.Vertices3d[VID].HeightScale;
642: 
643: 		for (const FRoadCurbCutSite& Site : Data.CurbCutSites)
644: 		{
645: 			// The cheap reject first — most vertices are nowhere near any cut, and the projection below is the
646: 			// only part that costs anything.
647: 			if (!Site.Influence.IsInside(Query))
648: 			{
649: 				continue;
650: 			}
651: 
652: 			double Arc = 0.0;
653: 			double Distance = 0.0;
654: 			MetaRoad::CurbCutRing::ProjectOnChain(Site.Chain, Site.Arcs, Query, Arc, Distance);
655: 
656: 			// UNSIGNED, deliberately: the ramp is symmetric about the ring, so it carries across into whatever
657: 			// abuts it. A neighbouring sidewalk lane ramps with the fill instead of standing a centimetre away at
658: 			// full height; a driving lane is untouched anyway, because this scales a lift that is already 0.
659: 			const double Progress01 = Distance / Site.Cut.Depth;
660: 			const double Scale = Site.Cut.ApplyHeight(Site.ArcOffset, Arc, Progress01, 1.0, Site.RingLength);
661: 			if (Scale < 1.0)
662: 			{
663: 				Data.Vertices3d[VID].ApplyHeightScale(Scale);
664: 			}
665: 		}
666: 
667: 		const double ScaleAfter = Data.Vertices3d[VID].HeightScale;
668: 		if (ScaleAfter < ScaleBefore)
669: 		{
670: 			++NumTouched;
671: 			DeepestScale = FMath::Min(DeepestScale, ScaleAfter);
672: 		}
673: 	}
674: 
675: 	// This stage is invisible when it does nothing, and "nothing" has several silent causes: no site registered,
676: 	// every site out of reach, or a neighbour whose lift is 0 so the multiplier cannot show. A ramp that looks
677: 	// coarse is also read here: the count is the number of vertices the cut had to work with, so a count near the
678: 	// profile's own sample count means nothing off the ring existed to bend over.
679: 	UE_LOG(LogMetaRoad, Log, TEXT("MetaRoad build: %d curb cut site(s) -> %d vertices scaled, deepest %.3f"),
680: 		Data.CurbCutSites.Num(), NumTouched, DeepestScale);
681: 
682: 	ROADBUILD_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
683: 	return true;
684: }
685: 
686: bool ResolveHeight(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, const FRoadBuildSettings& Settings,
687:                    TArray<bool>& OutHasSurfaceInfo, FProgressCancel* Progress)
688: {
689: 	// The hug contract again, at the second stage a mis-size corrupts rather than crashes: this walks every VID
690: 	// in the table and reads the graph at the same index.
691: 	checkf(Data.Vertices3d.MatchesGraph(Data.GetGraph()),
692: 		TEXT("ResolveHeight: vertex table (%d) does not match the graph (%d)"),
693: 		Data.Vertices3d.Num(), Data.GetGraph().MaxVertexID());
694: 
695: 	// Set max or min z value for all Vertices3d.
696: 	// OutHasSurfaceInfo[VID]: the vertex is covered by at least one NON-decal surface polygon (lane/loop/simple).
---
598: 	}
599: 
600: 	// Claims are final from here on. RegionUtils::GetFilledIslandTriangles used to answer "did anyone claim
601: 	// this?" by scanning every polygon's TrianglesIDs for every triangle -- quadratic, and run twice per build.
602: 	Data.BuildClaimedTriangleIndex();
603: 
604: 	int32 NumUnclaimed = 0;
605: 	for (int32 TID = 0; TID < Data.Triangles.Num(); ++TID)
606: 	{
607: 		NumUnclaimed += Data.IsTriangleClaimed(TID) ? 0 : 1;
608: 	}
609: 	UE_LOG(LogMetaRoad, Log, TEXT("MetaRoad build: %d triangles, %d claims, %d unmatched, %d unclaimed triangles"),
610: 		Data.Triangles.Num(), NumClaimed, NumUnmatched, NumUnclaimed);
611: 
612: 	ROADBUILD_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
613: 	return true;
614: }

616: bool ApplyCurbCuts(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, FProgressCancel* Progress)
617: {

620: 		return true; // bit-for-bit the build before the feature
621: 	}
622:

628:

630: 	double DeepestScale = 1.0;

632: 	for (int32 VID = 0; VID < Data.Vertices3d.Num(); ++VID)
633: 	{
634: 		if (!Graph.IsVertex(VID))
635: 		{
636: 			continue;
637: 		}
638: 
639: 		const FVector2d V2d = Graph.GetVertex(VID);
640: 		const FVector2D Query(V2d.X, V2d.Y);
641: 		const double ScaleBefore = Data.Vertices3d[VID].HeightScale;

643: 		for (const FRoadCurbCutSite& Site : Data.CurbCutSites)
644: 		{
645: 			// The cheap reject first — most vertices are nowhere near any cut, and the projection below is the
646: 			// only part that costs anything.
647: 			if (!Site.Influence.IsInside(Query))
648: 			{
649: 				continue;
650: 			}
651: 
652: 			double Arc = 0.0;
653: 			double Distance = 0.0;
654: 			MetaRoad::CurbCutRing::ProjectOnChain(Site.Chain, Site.Arcs, Query, Arc, Distance);
655: 
656: 			// UNSIGNED, deliberately: the ramp is symmetric about the ring, so it carries across into whatever
657: 			// abuts it. A neighbouring sidewalk lane ramps with the fill instead of standing a centimetre away at
658: 			// full height; a driving lane is untouched anyway, because this scales a lift that is already 0.
659: 			const double Progress01 = Distance / Site.Cut.Depth;

661: 			if (Scale < 1.0)
662: 			{

665: 		}
666:
