668: 		if (ScaleAfter < ScaleBefore)
669: 		{
670: 			++NumTouched;
671: 			DeepestScale = FMath::Min(DeepestScale, ScaleAfter);
672: 		}
673: 	}
674: 
675: 	// This stage is invisible when it does nothing, and "nothing" has several silent causes: no site registered,
676: 	// every site out of reach, or a neighbour whose lift is 0 so the multiplier cannot show. A ramp that looks
677: 	// coarse is also read here: the count is the number of vertices the cut had to work with, so a count near the
678: 	// profile's own sample count means nothing off the ring existed to bend over.
679: 	UE_LOG(LogMetaRoad, Log, TEXT("MetaRoad build: %d curb cut site(s) -> %d vertices scaled, deepest %.3f"),
680: 		Data.CurbCutSites.Num(), NumTouched, DeepestScale);
681: 
682: 	ROADBUILD_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
683: 	return true;
684: }

686: bool ResolveHeight(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, const FRoadBuildSettings& Settings,
687:                    TArray<bool>& OutHasSurfaceInfo, FProgressCancel* Progress)
688: {
689: 	// The hug contract again, at the second stage a mis-size corrupts rather than crashes: this walks every VID

693: 		Data.Vertices3d.Num(), Data.GetGraph().MaxVertexID());
694: 
695: 	// Set max or min z value for all Vertices3d.
696: 	// OutHasSurfaceInfo[VID]: the vertex is covered by at least one NON-decal surface polygon (lane/loop/simple).
697: 	// A vertex with only decal Infos is off the real surface -- a welded feature line poking past the mesh edge, or
698: 	// a floating decal. Its Z is the authored fallback (Z_WithoutDecals), which must NOT drive real surface height
---
604: 	int32 NumUnclaimed = 0;
605: 	for (int32 TID = 0; TID < Data.Triangles.Num(); ++TID)
606: 	{
607: 		NumUnclaimed += Data.IsTriangleClaimed(TID) ? 0 : 1;
608: 	}

610: 		Data.Triangles.Num(), NumClaimed, NumUnmatched, NumUnclaimed);
611: 
612: 	ROADBUILD_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
613: 	return true;
614: }

686: bool ResolveHeight(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, const FRoadBuildSettings& Settings,
687:                    TArray<bool>& OutHasSurfaceInfo, FProgressCancel* Progress)
688: {
689: 	// The hug contract again, at the second stage a mis-size corrupts rather than crashes: this walks every VID
690: 	// in the table and reads the graph at the same index.
691: 	checkf(Data.Vertices3d.MatchesGraph(Data.GetGraph()),
692: 		TEXT("ResolveHeight: vertex table (%d) does not match the graph (%d)"),
693: 		Data.Vertices3d.Num(), Data.GetGraph().MaxVertexID());
694: 
695: 	// Set max or min z value for all Vertices3d.
696: 	// OutHasSurfaceInfo[VID]: the vertex is covered by at least one NON-decal surface polygon (lane/loop/simple).
697: 	// A vertex with only decal Infos is off the real surface -- a welded feature line poking past the mesh edge, or
698: 	// a floating decal. Its Z is the authored fallback (Z_WithoutDecals), which must NOT drive real surface height
699: 	// in the OverlapRadius smoothing below (else a high marking pulls the mesh-edge vertices up toward it).
700: 	OutHasSurfaceInfo.Init(false, Data.Vertices3d.Num());
701: 
702: 	for (int VID = 0; VID < Data.Vertices3d.Num(); ++VID)
703: 	{
704: 		auto& Vertex3d = Data.Vertices3d[VID];
---
623: 	checkf(Data.Vertices3d.MatchesGraph(Data.GetGraph()),
624: 		TEXT("ApplyCurbCuts: vertex table (%d) does not match the graph (%d)"),
625: 		Data.Vertices3d.Num(), Data.GetGraph().MaxVertexID());
626: 
627: 	const FDynamicGraph2d& Graph = Data.GetGraph();
628: 
629: 	int32 NumTouched = 0;
630: 	double DeepestScale = 1.0;
631: 
632: 	for (int32 VID = 0; VID < Data.Vertices3d.Num(); ++VID)
633: 	{
634: 		if (!Graph.IsVertex(VID))
635: 		{
636: 			continue;
637: 		}
638: 
639: 		const FVector2d V2d = Graph.GetVertex(VID);
640: 		const FVector2D Query(V2d.X, V2d.Y);
641: 		const double ScaleBefore = Data.Vertices3d[VID].HeightScale;
642: 
643: 		for (const FRoadCurbCutSite& Site : Data.CurbCutSites)
644: 		{
645: 			// The cheap reject first — most vertices are nowhere near any cut, and the projection below is the
646: 			// only part that costs anything.
647: 			if (!Site.Influence.IsInside(Query))
648: 			{
649: 				continue;
650: 			}
651: 
652: 			double Arc = 0.0;
653: 			double Distance = 0.0;
654: 			MetaRoad::CurbCutRing::ProjectOnChain(Site.Chain, Site.Arcs, Query, Arc, Distance);
655: 
656: 			// UNSIGNED, deliberately: the ramp is symmetric about the ring, so it carries across into whatever
657: 			// abuts it. A neighbouring sidewalk lane ramps with the fill instead of standing a centimetre away at
658: 			// full height; a driving lane is untouched anyway, because this scales a lift that is already 0.
659: 			const double Progress01 = Distance / Site.Cut.Depth;
660: 			const double Scale = Site.Cut.ApplyHeight(Site.ArcOffset, Arc, Progress01, 1.0, Site.RingLength);
661: 			if (Scale < 1.0)
662: 			{
663: 				Data.Vertices3d[VID].ApplyHeightScale(Scale);
664: 			}
665: 		}
666: 
667: 		const double ScaleAfter = Data.Vertices3d[VID].HeightScale;
668: 		if (ScaleAfter < ScaleBefore)
669: 		{
670: 			++NumTouched;
671: 			DeepestScale = FMath::Min(DeepestScale, ScaleAfter);
672: 		}
673: 	}
674: 
675: 	// This stage is invisible when it does nothing, and "nothing" has several silent causes: no site registered,
676: 	// every site out of reach, or a neighbour whose lift is 0 so the multiplier cannot show. A ramp that looks
677: 	// coarse is also read here: the count is the number of vertices the cut had to work with, so a count near the
678: 	// profile's own sample count means nothing off the ring existed to bend over.
679: 	UE_LOG(LogMetaRoad, Log, TEXT("MetaRoad build: %d curb cut site(s) -> %d vertices scaled, deepest %.3f"),
680: 		Data.CurbCutSites.Num(), NumTouched, DeepestScale);
681: 
682: 	ROADBUILD_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
683: 	return true;
684: }
685: 
686: bool ResolveHeight(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, const FRoadBuildSettings& Settings,
687:                    TArray<bool>& OutHasSurfaceInfo, FProgressCancel* Progress)
688: {
689: 	// The hug contract again, at the second stage a mis-size corrupts rather than crashes: this walks every VID
690: 	// in the table and reads the graph at the same index.
691: 	checkf(Data.Vertices3d.MatchesGraph(Data.GetGraph()),
692: 		TEXT("ResolveHeight: vertex table (%d) does not match the graph (%d)"),
693: 		Data.Vertices3d.Num(), Data.GetGraph().MaxVertexID());
694: 
695: 	// Set max or min z value for all Vertices3d.
696: 	// OutHasSurfaceInfo[VID]: the vertex is covered by at least one NON-decal surface polygon (lane/loop/simple).
697: 	// A vertex with only decal Infos is off the real surface -- a welded feature line poking past the mesh edge, or
698: 	// a floating decal. Its Z is the authored fallback (Z_WithoutDecals), which must NOT drive real surface height
699: 	// in the OverlapRadius smoothing below (else a high marking pulls the mesh-edge vertices up toward it).
700: 	OutHasSurfaceInfo.Init(false, Data.Vertices3d.Num());
701: 
702: 	for (int VID = 0; VID < Data.Vertices3d.Num(); ++VID)
703: 	{
704: 		auto& Vertex3d = Data.Vertices3d[VID];
705: 		if (!ensure(Vertex3d.Infos.Num()))
706: 		{
707: 			Data.ResultInfo.SetFailed({ LOCTEXT("CalculateResultFail_MeshBroken", "FRoadTriangulationData: Mesh is broken") });
708: 			return false;
709: 		}
710: 
711: 		//  Here we are only interested in the X, Y coordinates. Z needs to be calculated below.
712: 		Vertex3d.Vertex = Vertex3d.Infos.begin()->Value.Pos.Location;
713: 
714: 		double TargetZ_WithoutDecals = Vertex3d.Vertex.Z;
715: 		double TargetZ_WithDecals = 0;
716: 
717: 		bool bZWasSet = false;
718: 
719: 		for (auto& [Poly, Info] : Vertex3d.Infos)
720: 		{
721: 			// Resolved at THIS vertex's S: a RoadZone lane attribute key may name a zone type whose
722: 			// bIsDecal differs from the lane's own, and that decides whether the vertex drives real
723: 			// surface height. Without the attribute this is the polygon-wide zone, as before.
---
634: 		if (!Graph.IsVertex(VID))
635: 		{
636: 			continue;
637: 		}
638: 
639: 		const FVector2d V2d = Graph.GetVertex(VID);
640: 		const FVector2D Query(V2d.X, V2d.Y);
641: 		const double ScaleBefore = Data.Vertices3d[VID].HeightScale;
642: 
643: 		for (const FRoadCurbCutSite& Site : Data.CurbCutSites)
644: 		{
645: 			// The cheap reject first — most vertices are nowhere near any cut, and the projection below is the
646: 			// only part that costs anything.
647: 			if (!Site.Influence.IsInside(Query))
648: 			{
649: 				continue;
650: 			}
651: 
652: 			double Arc = 0.0;
653: 			double Distance = 0.0;
654: 			MetaRoad::CurbCutRing::ProjectOnChain(Site.Chain, Site.Arcs, Query, Arc, Distance);
655: 
656: 			// UNSIGNED, deliberately: the ramp is symmetric about the ring, so it carries across into whatever
657: 			// abuts it. A neighbouring sidewalk lane ramps with the fill instead of standing a centimetre away at
658: 			// full height; a driving lane is untouched anyway, because this scales a lift that is already 0.
659: 			const double Progress01 = Distance / Site.Cut.Depth;
660: 			const double Scale = Site.Cut.ApplyHeight(Site.ArcOffset, Arc, Progress01, 1.0, Site.RingLength);
661: 			if (Scale < 1.0)
662: 			{
663: 				Data.Vertices3d[VID].ApplyHeightScale(Scale);
664: 			}
665: 		}
666: 
667: 		const double ScaleAfter = Data.Vertices3d[VID].HeightScale;
668: 		if (ScaleAfter < ScaleBefore)
669: 		{
670: 			++NumTouched;
671: 			DeepestScale = FMath::Min(DeepestScale, ScaleAfter);
672: 		}
673: 	}
674: 
675: 	// This stage is invisible when it does nothing, and "nothing" has several silent causes: no site registered,
676: 	// every site out of reach, or a neighbour whose lift is 0 so the multiplier cannot show. A ramp that looks
677: 	// coarse is also read here: the count is the number of vertices the cut had to work with, so a count near the
678: 	// profile's own sample count means nothing off the ring existed to bend over.
679: 	UE_LOG(LogMetaRoad, Log, TEXT("MetaRoad build: %d curb cut site(s) -> %d vertices scaled, deepest %.3f"),
680: 		Data.CurbCutSites.Num(), NumTouched, DeepestScale);
681: 
682: 	ROADBUILD_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
683: 	return true;
684: }

686: bool ResolveHeight(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, const FRoadBuildSettings& Settings,
687:                    TArray<bool>& OutHasSurfaceInfo, FProgressCancel* Progress)
688: {
689: 	// The hug contract again, at the second stage a mis-size corrupts rather than crashes: this walks every VID
690: 	// in the table and reads the graph at the same index.
691: 	checkf(Data.Vertices3d.MatchesGraph(Data.GetGraph()),
692: 		TEXT("ResolveHeight: vertex table (%d) does not match the graph (%d)"),
693: 		Data.Vertices3d.Num(), Data.GetGraph().MaxVertexID());
694: 
695: 	// Set max or min z value for all Vertices3d.
696: 	// OutHasSurfaceInfo[VID]: the vertex is covered by at least one NON-decal surface polygon (lane/loop/simple).
697: 	// A vertex with only decal Infos is off the real surface -- a welded feature line poking past the mesh edge, or
698: 	// a floating decal. Its Z is the authored fallback (Z_WithoutDecals), which must NOT drive real surface height
699: 	// in the OverlapRadius smoothing below (else a high marking pulls the mesh-edge vertices up toward it).

705: 		if (!ensure(Vertex3d.Infos.Num()))
706: 		{
707: 			Data.ResultInfo.SetFailed({ LOCTEXT("CalculateResultFail_MeshBroken", "FRoadTriangulationData: Mesh is broken") });
708: 			return false;
709: 		}
710: 
711: 		//  Here we are only interested in the X, Y coordinates. Z needs to be calculated below.
712: 		Vertex3d.Vertex = Vertex3d.Infos.begin()->Value.Pos.Location;
713: 
714: 		double TargetZ_WithoutDecals = Vertex3d.Vertex.Z;
715: 		double TargetZ_WithDecals = 0;
716: 
717: 		bool bZWasSet = false;
718: 
719: 		for (auto& [Poly, Info] : Vertex3d.Infos)
720: 		{
721: 			// Resolved at THIS vertex's S: a RoadZone lane attribute key may name a zone type whose
722: 			// bIsDecal differs from the lane's own, and that decides whether the vertex drives real
723: 			// surface height. Without the attribute this is the polygon-wide zone, as before.
724: 			//
725: 			// Pos.SOffset, not the seam-aware GetSOffset() that GetRoadZoneForTri uses -- that one needs a
726: 			// triangle and this pass is per VERTEX. On a CLOSED-loop lane a seam vertex therefore resolves
727: 			// to the lane's own zone (S reads as 0) while the triangles around it resolve to the last span.
728: 			// Accepted: it is one vertex, it only picks bIsDecal, and the lane zone is the sane default --
729: 			// a seam vertex belongs to both ends, so there is no single correct answer to compute.
730: 			const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>& VertexZone = Poly->GetRoadZoneAt(Info.Pos.SOffset);
731: 			const FRoadZoneTypeDetails* Details = VertexZone.IsValid() ? VertexZone.Get<FRoadZone>().ZoneType.GetDetails() : nullptr;
732: 			const bool bIsDecal = Details ? Details->bIsDecal : true;
733: 
734: 			// SnapToGround uses the Max path here only as a fallback (overridden per-vertex by the
---
660: 			const double Scale = Site.Cut.ApplyHeight(Site.ArcOffset, Arc, Progress01, 1.0, Site.RingLength);
661: 			if (Scale < 1.0)
662: 			{
663: 				Data.Vertices3d[VID].ApplyHeightScale(Scale);
664: 			}
665: 		}
666: 
667: 		const double ScaleAfter = Data.Vertices3d[VID].HeightScale;
668: 		if (ScaleAfter < ScaleBefore)
669: 		{
670: 			++NumTouched;
671: 			DeepestScale = FMath::Min(DeepestScale, ScaleAfter);
672: 		}
673: 	}
674: 
675: 	// This stage is invisible when it does nothing, and "nothing" has several silent causes: no site registered,
676: 	// every site out of reach, or a neighbour whose lift is 0 so the multiplier cannot show. A ramp that looks
677: 	// coarse is also read here: the count is the number of vertices the cut had to work with, so a count near the
678: 	// profile's own sample count means nothing off the ring existed to bend over.
679: 	UE_LOG(LogMetaRoad, Log, TEXT("MetaRoad build: %d curb cut site(s) -> %d vertices scaled, deepest %.3f"),
680: 		Data.CurbCutSites.Num(), NumTouched, DeepestScale);
681: 
682: 	ROADBUILD_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
683: 	return true;

703: 	{

705: 		if (!ensure(Vertex3d.Infos.Num()))
706: 		{
707: 			Data.ResultInfo.SetFailed({ LOCTEXT("CalculateResultFail_MeshBroken", "FRoadTriangulationData: Mesh is broken") });
708: 			return false;

711: 		//  Here we are only interested in the X, Y coordinates. Z needs to be calculated below.

728: 			// Accepted: it is one vertex, it only picks bIsDecal, and the lane zone is the sane default --

730: 			const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>& VertexZone = Poly->GetRoadZoneAt(Info.Pos.SOffset);
731: 			const FRoadZoneTypeDetails* Details = VertexZone.IsValid() ? VertexZone.Get<FRoadZone>().ZoneType.GetDetails() : nullptr;
732: 			const bool bIsDecal = Details ? Details->bIsDecal : true;
733: 
734: 			// SnapToGround uses the Max path here only as a fallback (overridden per-vertex by the
735: 			// game-thread line-trace pass; this value survives only where the trace misses).
736: 			TargetZ_WithoutDecals = Settings.OverlapStrategy != ERoadOverlapStrategy::UseMinZ
737: 				? FMath::Max(TargetZ_WithoutDecals, Info.Pos.Location.Z)

739: 
740: 			if (!bIsDecal)
741: 			{

743: 				{
744: 					TargetZ_WithDecals = Info.Pos.Location.Z;
745: 				}
746: 				else
747: 				{
748: 					TargetZ_WithDecals = Settings.OverlapStrategy != ERoadOverlapStrategy::UseMinZ
749: 						? FMath::Max(TargetZ_WithDecals, Info.Pos.Location.Z)
750: 						: FMath::Min(TargetZ_WithDecals, Info.Pos.Location.Z);
751: 				}
752: 				bZWasSet = true;
753: 			}
754: 		}
755: 
756: 		Vertex3d.Vertex.Z = bZWasSet ? TargetZ_WithDecals : TargetZ_WithoutDecals;
757: 		OutHasSurfaceInfo[VID] = bZWasSet;
758: 	}
759: 
760: 	ROADBUILD_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
---
701: 
702: 	for (int VID = 0; VID < Data.Vertices3d.Num(); ++VID)

763: 
764: bool BlendOverlaps(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, const FRoadBuildSettings& Settings,
765:                    const TArray<bool>& HasSurfaceInfo, FProgressCancel* Progress)
766: {
767: 	// Skipped for SnapToGround: per-vertex Z is replaced by the line-trace pass, so cross-spline
768: 	// overlap blending here would be wasted work.
769: 	if (Settings.OverlapStrategy == ERoadOverlapStrategy::SnapToGround || Settings.OverlapRadius <= KINDA_SMALL_NUMBER)
770: 	{
771: 		return true;
772: 	}
773: 
774: 	const auto& Graph = Data.GetGraph();
775: 
776: 	for (int VID = 0; VID < Data.Vertices3d.Num(); ++VID)
777: 	{
778: 		// A decal-only vertex (a welded feature line past the mesh edge, or a floating decal) holds an authored
779: 		// fallback Z, not a real surface height -- it must not raise/lower nearby surface vertices toward itself.
780: 		if (!HasSurfaceInfo[VID])
781: 		{
782: 			continue;
783: 		}
784: 		auto& Vertex3d = Data.Vertices3d[VID];
785: 		auto DistanceSqFunc = [VID_A = VID, &Graph](int VID_B)
786: 		{
787: 			return DistanceSquared(Graph.GetVertex(VID_A), Graph.GetVertex(VID_B));
788: 		};
789: 		auto IgnoreFunc = [&Data, CurVID = VID](const int& VID)
790: 		{
791: 			if (CurVID == VID)
792: 			{
793: 				return true;
794: 			}
795: 			if (Data.IsBoundaryVertex(VID))
796: 			{
797: 				return true;
798: 			}
799: 			return false;
800: 		};
801: 		auto Points = Data.GetArrangement().PointHash.FindAllInRadius(Graph.GetVertex(VID), Settings.OverlapRadius, DistanceSqFunc, IgnoreFunc);
---
703: 	{
704: 		auto& Vertex3d = Data.Vertices3d[VID];
705: 		if (!ensure(Vertex3d.Infos.Num()))
706: 		{
707: 			Data.ResultInfo.SetFailed({ LOCTEXT("CalculateResultFail_MeshBroken", "FRoadTriangulationData: Mesh is broken") });
708: 			return false;
709: 		}
710: 
711: 		//  Here we are only interested in the X, Y coordinates. Z needs to be calculated below.
712: 		Vertex3d.Vertex = Vertex3d.Infos.begin()->Value.Pos.Location;
713: 
714: 		double TargetZ_WithoutDecals = Vertex3d.Vertex.Z;
715: 		double TargetZ_WithDecals = 0;
716: 
717: 		bool bZWasSet = false;
718: 
719: 		for (auto& [Poly, Info] : Vertex3d.Infos)
720: 		{
721: 			// Resolved at THIS vertex's S: a RoadZone lane attribute key may name a zone type whose
722: 			// bIsDecal differs from the lane's own, and that decides whether the vertex drives real
723: 			// surface height. Without the attribute this is the polygon-wide zone, as before.
724: 			//
725: 			// Pos.SOffset, not the seam-aware GetSOffset() that GetRoadZoneForTri uses -- that one needs a
726: 			// triangle and this pass is per VERTEX. On a CLOSED-loop lane a seam vertex therefore resolves
727: 			// to the lane's own zone (S reads as 0) while the triangles around it resolve to the last span.
728: 			// Accepted: it is one vertex, it only picks bIsDecal, and the lane zone is the sane default --
729: 			// a seam vertex belongs to both ends, so there is no single correct answer to compute.
730: 			const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>& VertexZone = Poly->GetRoadZoneAt(Info.Pos.SOffset);
731: 			const FRoadZoneTypeDetails* Details = VertexZone.IsValid() ? VertexZone.Get<FRoadZone>().ZoneType.GetDetails() : nullptr;
732: 			const bool bIsDecal = Details ? Details->bIsDecal : true;
733: 
734: 			// SnapToGround uses the Max path here only as a fallback (overridden per-vertex by the
735: 			// game-thread line-trace pass; this value survives only where the trace misses).
736: 			TargetZ_WithoutDecals = Settings.OverlapStrategy != ERoadOverlapStrategy::UseMinZ
737: 				? FMath::Max(TargetZ_WithoutDecals, Info.Pos.Location.Z)
738: 				: FMath::Min(TargetZ_WithoutDecals, Info.Pos.Location.Z);
739: 
740: 			if (!bIsDecal)
741: 			{
742: 				if (!bZWasSet)
743: 				{
744: 					TargetZ_WithDecals = Info.Pos.Location.Z;
745: 				}
746: 				else
747: 				{
748: 					TargetZ_WithDecals = Settings.OverlapStrategy != ERoadOverlapStrategy::UseMinZ
749: 						? FMath::Max(TargetZ_WithDecals, Info.Pos.Location.Z)
750: 						: FMath::Min(TargetZ_WithDecals, Info.Pos.Location.Z);
751: 				}
752: 				bZWasSet = true;
753: 			}
754: 		}
755: 
756: 		Vertex3d.Vertex.Z = bZWasSet ? TargetZ_WithDecals : TargetZ_WithoutDecals;
757: 		OutHasSurfaceInfo[VID] = bZWasSet;
758: 	}
759: 
760: 	ROADBUILD_BAIL_IF_CANCELLED();
761: 	return true;
762: }
763: 
764: bool BlendOverlaps(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, const FRoadBuildSettings& Settings,
765:                    const TArray<bool>& HasSurfaceInfo, FProgressCancel* Progress)
766: {
767: 	// Skipped for SnapToGround: per-vertex Z is replaced by the line-trace pass, so cross-spline
768: 	// overlap blending here would be wasted work.
769: 	if (Settings.OverlapStrategy == ERoadOverlapStrategy::SnapToGround || Settings.OverlapRadius <= KINDA_SMALL_NUMBER)
770: 	{
771: 		return true;
772: 	}
773: 
774: 	const auto& Graph = Data.GetGraph();
775: 
776: 	for (int VID = 0; VID < Data.Vertices3d.Num(); ++VID)
777: 	{
778: 		// A decal-only vertex (a welded feature line past the mesh edge, or a floating decal) holds an authored
779: 		// fallback Z, not a real surface height -- it must not raise/lower nearby surface vertices toward itself.
780: 		if (!HasSurfaceInfo[VID])
781: 		{
782: 			continue;
783: 		}
784: 		auto& Vertex3d = Data.Vertices3d[VID];
785: 		auto DistanceSqFunc = [VID_A = VID, &Graph](int VID_B)
786: 		{
787: 			return DistanceSquared(Graph.GetVertex(VID_A), Graph.GetVertex(VID_B));
788: 		};
789: 		auto IgnoreFunc = [&Data, CurVID = VID](const int& VID)
790: 		{
791: 			if (CurVID == VID)
792: 			{
793: 				return true;
794: 			}
795: 			if (Data.IsBoundaryVertex(VID))
796: 			{
797: 				return true;
798: 			}
799: 			return false;
800: 		};
801: 		auto Points = Data.GetArrangement().PointHash.FindAllInRadius(Graph.GetVertex(VID), Settings.OverlapRadius, DistanceSqFunc, IgnoreFunc);
802: 		for (auto& [NearVID, DistSq] : Points)
803: 		{

=== Source\MetaRoadEditor\Private\RoadMeshBuild\Ops\RoadBuildStages.h ===
85:  *  Close() is what established THE HUG CONTRACT (Vertices3d sized to Graph.MaxVertexID(), so index i is the 3D
86:  *  lift of graph vertex i). What a polygon may still write arrives as FRoadCompletionSink. */
87: bool CompleteArrangement(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, FProgressCancel* Progress);
88: 
89: /** Surface refinement across R (TargetEdgeLength) -- MetaRoad::SurfaceRefine. Adds ONLY isolated
90:  *  vertices; see the pass's own header for why that is the whole of its safety argument. */
91: bool RefineSurfaces(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, FProgressCancel* Progress);
92: 
93: /** Outer silhouette contours, as directed VID edge lists. Fails the build when none are found. */
94: bool FindBoundaries(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, FProgressCancel* Progress);
95: 
96: /** One FDelaunay2 over the whole graph, then Data.Triangles = the filled subset, wound for rendering.
97:  *  OutDelaunay is kept alive for ClaimTriangles, which re-fills per polygon against the same triangulation. */
98: bool Triangulate(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, UE::Geometry::FDelaunay2& OutDelaunay, FProgressCancel* Progress);
99: 
100: /** Each polygon claims its triangle subset (TrianglesIDs) and, in doing so, gives every vertex it covers a
101:  *  FRoadVertexInfo -- including the densified ones, which is how they get their metadata. */
102: bool ClaimTriangles(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, UE::Geometry::FDelaunay2& Delaunay, FProgressCancel* Progress);
103: 
104: /**
105:  * Apply every registered curb cut (Data.CurbCutSites) to FArrangementVertex3d::HeightScale.
106:  *
107:  * AFTER ClaimTriangles because it must see the FINAL vertex set -- the densified vertices included -- and BEFORE
108:  * ResolveHeight only because that is where the scale starts being read. It owns no geometry and reads no Infos:
109:  * a ramp is a property of a PLACE, so every vertex within a site's reach takes it, whatever polygon owns it.
110:  * That is the whole reason this is a stage and not a line in some polygon's FillVertexInfo, where it reached only
111:  * the author's own vertices and stopped dead at the seam with the neighbouring lane.
112:  *
113:  * Distance to the ring is UNSIGNED: the ramp continues into whatever is on the far side. A driving lane is
114:  * unaffected for free (its lift is 0, and this is a multiplier on it).
115:  */
116: bool ApplyCurbCuts(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, FProgressCancel* Progress);
117: 
118: /** Reduce each vertex's per-polygon Infos to one Z. FAILS the build ("Mesh is broken") on a vertex with no
119:  *  Info at all -- there is no fallback, and that is deliberate: it means a vertex nothing owns reached the
120:  *  mesh. OutHasSurfaceInfo marks the vertices covered by a NON-decal polygon; only those may drive Z below. */
121: bool ResolveHeight(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, const FRoadBuildSettings& Settings,
122:                    TArray<bool>& OutHasSurfaceInfo, FProgressCancel* Progress);
123: 
124: /** Cross-spline Z blending inside OverlapRadius. In-place and order-dependent by design. Skipped entirely for
125:  *  SnapToGround, whose per-vertex trace replaces Z anyway. */
126: bool BlendOverlaps(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, const FRoadBuildSettings& Settings,
127:                    const TArray<bool>& HasSurfaceInfo, FProgressCancel* Progress);
128: 
129: /** The gutter: lower the driving surface towards its lateral edges. AFTER BlendOverlaps -- see the body. */
130: bool ApplyEdgeProfile(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, FProgressCancel* Progress);
131: 
132: /** Normals, optional cotan smoothing of Z, the spatial AABB, and the optional boundary debug draw. */
133: bool Finalize(FRoadTriangulationData& Data, const FRoadBuildSettings& Settings, FProgressCancel* Progress);
134: 
135: } // namespace BuildStages
136: } // namespace MetaRoad

31: void FProceduralPolygon_Simple::MatchMagneticSpans(const TArray<FVector2D>& Points2D, const TArray<FMagneticSpan>& Spans,
32: 	TBitArray<>& OutSkipSeg)
33: {
34: 	// A span may only be skipped once we know we can tag a real path in its place. Skipping one we cannot tag leaves a
35: 	// gap -> FindPolyline fails -> the fill adds no Infos -> its OWN already-inserted vertices are left unowned -> the
36: 	// height pass rejects the whole mesh. So an unmatched span stays ordinary geometry.
37: 	const MetaRoad::ArrangementMatch::FParams MatchParams;
38: 	const int32 NumPts = Points2D.Num();
39: 
40: 	for (const FMagneticSpan& Span : Spans)
41: 	{
42: 		if (!Points2D.IsValidIndex(Span.StartIndex) || !Points2D.IsValidIndex(Span.EndIndex) || Span.StartIndex == Span.EndIndex)
43: 		{
44: 			continue;
45: 		}
46: 
47: 		FMagneticSpanRec Rec;
48: 		Rec.VidA = MetaRoad::ArrangementMatch::FindPinnedVertex(Owner.GetArrangement(), Points2D[Span.StartIndex]);
49: 		Rec.VidB = MetaRoad::ArrangementMatch::FindPinnedVertex(Owner.GetArrangement(), Points2D[Span.EndIndex]);
50: 		Rec.Span = Span;
51: 		if (Rec.VidA < 0 || Rec.VidB < 0)
52: 		{
53: 			// EXPECTED, not a warning: magnetism is now an opt-in per-segment flag, so a magnetic span can have
54: 			// ends that do not coincide with any road-mesh vertex — an unanchored segment, or one anchored to
55: 			// another spline rather than the road. There is nothing to attach it to here, so it silently stays its
56: 			// own geometry (best-effort). Only a span whose ends ARE on vertices but that fails to route is worth a
57: 			// warning (below). (Road-corner-anchored ends are pinned onto their vertices, so they don't reach here.)
58: 			continue;
59: 		}
60: 
61: 		// The span's own samples are the match QUERY: they approximate the target, and the path we want is the real
62: 		// geometry underneath them.
63: 		TArray<FVector2d> Approx;
64: 		for (int32 j = Span.StartIndex; ; j = (j + 1) % NumPts)
65: 		{
66: 			Approx.Add(Points2D[j]);
67: 			if (j == Span.EndIndex)
68: 			{
69: 				break;
70: 			}
71: 		}
72: 
73: 		MetaRoad::ArrangementMatch::FReport Report;
74: 		if (!MetaRoad::ArrangementMatch::MatchPolyline(Owner.GetArrangement(), Approx, Rec.VidA, Rec.VidB, MatchParams, Rec.Path, &Report))
75: 		{
76: 			// Name the actual cause: "no route" and "route too far off" want opposite fixes (connectivity /
77: 			// walkability vs the deviation limit), and guessing between them wastes an editor round-trip.
78: 			const FText Cause = Report.bPathFound
79: 				? FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_MagneticSpanTooFar",
80: 					"best route deviates {0} cm on average (limit {1})"),
81: 					FMath::RoundToInt(Report.BestMeanDeviation), FMath::RoundToInt(MatchParams.MaxMeanDeviation))
82: 				: FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_MagneticSpanNoRoute",
83: 					"no route between them through walkable edges (corridor widened to {0} cm, {1} candidate vertices)"),
84: 					FMath::RoundToInt(Report.RadiusTried), Report.CorridorVertices);
85: 
86: 			ResultInfo.AddWarning({ 0, FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_MagneticSpanNoMatch",
87: 				"FProceduralPolygon_Simple: {0}: magnetic span VID {1}->{2} did not match: {3} -- falling back to its own geometry"),
88: 				GetDescription(), Rec.VidA, Rec.VidB, Cause) });
89: 			continue;
90: 		}
91:

99: 
100: FProceduralPolygon_Simple::FProceduralPolygon_Simple(const FRoadTriangulationData& Owner, FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, int SimplePolygonIndex)
101: 	: FProceduralPolygon(Owner)
102: 	, SimplePolygonIndex(SimplePolygonIndex)
103: {
104: 	ResultInfo = { EGeometryResultType::InProgress };
105: 
106: 	const TArray<FVector>& Vertices = Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex].Vertices;
107: 
108: 	// Curb cuts are anchored by arc length along the SOURCE ring (the producer's own Vertices, not the cleaned
109: 	// Points2D below), so the prefix sums are built from it once and reused by every per-vertex evaluation.
110: 	if (Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex].CurbCuts.Num() > 0 && Vertices.Num() >= 3)
111: 	{
112: 		MetaRoad::CurbCutRing::BuildArcLengths(Vertices, RingArcs);
113: 	}
114: 
115: 	TArray<FVector2D> Points2D;
116: 	Points2D.Reserve(Vertices.Num());
117: 	for (auto& It : Vertices)
118: 	{
119: 		Points2D.Add(FVector2D{ It });
120: 	}
121: 
122: 	// Match the magnetized spans BEFORE deciding anything else: whether this behaves as a magnetic fill or as a plain
123: 	// one depends on whether a real path was actually found, not merely on spans being present. Road lanes + loops are
124: 	// already inserted (they precede Simple polygons), so the target geometry is fully available here.
125: 	const TArray<FMagneticSpan>& Spans = Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex].MagneticSpans;
126: 	TBitArray<> SkipSeg(false, Points2D.Num());
127: 	if (Spans.Num() > 0)
128: 	{
129: 		MatchMagneticSpans(Points2D, Spans, SkipSeg);
130: 	}
131: 	const bool bMagneticFill = MagneticSpanRecs.Num() > 0;
132: 
133: 	if (!bMagneticFill)
134: 	{
135: 		// Collapse near-coincident vertices (exact closing dup, the loop seam, and any sub-tolerance
136: 		// vertices) before triangulation — they otherwise produce degenerate sliver triangles / holes.
137: 		// This MUST also run when spans were present but none matched: we then insert the full boundary like any
138: 		// plain fill, and skipping the cleanup there is what leaves unfilled slivers. It is skipped only for a real
139: 		// magnetic fill, whose span indices into Points2D the (index-shifting) cleanup would invalidate.
140: 		GeomUtils::RemoveClosePolylineVertices(Points2D, GeomUtils::DefSnapDistance, /*bClosed*/ true);
141: 		GeomUtils::RemovedPolylineSelfIntersection(Points2D);
142: 	}
143: 
144: 	FVector2D IntersectionPoint;
145: 	FIndex2i IntersectionIndex;
146: 	if (GeomUtils::FindPolylineSelfIntersection(Points2D, IntersectionPoint, IntersectionIndex))
147: 	{
148: 		ResultInfo.SetFailed(FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_SelfIntersectionFailed", "FProceduralPolygon_Simple: {0}: Self intersection found"), GetDescription()));
149: 		return;
150: 	}
151: 
152: 	if (!IntersectApproximation.UpdateAABBTree(Vertices))
153: 	{
154: 		ResultInfo.SetFailed(FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_UpdateAABBTreeFailed", "FProceduralPolygon_Simple: {0}: UpdateAABBTree() failed"), GetDescription()));
155: 		return;
156: 	}
157: 
158: 	int GID = SelectSurfaceGID(GetRoadZone());
159: 
160: 	// A magnetic fill omits its matched spans here (SkipSeg) -- the real path is tagged in OnCompleteArrangement instead
161: 	// of inserted; everything else is inserted as a normal closed ring.
162: 	LineInfo = Builder.InsertClosedRing(Points2D, GID, bMagneticFill ? &SkipSeg : nullptr);
163: 
164: 	if (!LineInfo.IsValid())
165: 	{
166: 		ResultInfo.SetFailed(FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_OutsideLineFailed", "FProceduralPolygon_Simple: {0}: polygon failed "), GetDescription()));
167: 		return;
168: 	}
169: 
170: 	// Holes go in AFTER the boundary of the same polygon and BEFORE its curb cuts. The order is byte-observable
171: 	// (every VID downstream is assigned in insertion order) -- fix it and do not permute it.
172: 	InsertHoleRings(Builder, Points2D, GID);
173: 
174: 	InsertCurbCutContours(Builder, Points2D);
175: 
176: 	ResultInfo.SetSuccess();
177: }
178: 
179: void FProceduralPolygon_Simple::InsertHoleRings(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& OuterPoints2D, int GID)
180: {
181: 	const FRoadPolygonData& Data = Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex];
182: 	if (Data.Holes.Num() == 0)
183: 	{
184: 		return;
185: 	}
186: 
187: 	auto Reject = [this](int32 HoleIndex, const FText& Reason)
188: 	{
189: 		ResultInfo.AddWarning({ 0, FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_HoleRejected",
190: 			"FProceduralPolygon_Simple: {0}: hole {1} {2} -- dropped, the fill is solid there"),
---
92: 		for (int32 j = Span.StartIndex; j != Span.EndIndex; j = (j + 1) % NumPts)
93: 		{
94: 			OutSkipSeg[j] = true;
95: 		}
96: 		MagneticSpanRecs.Add(MoveTemp(Rec));
97: 	}
98: }

100: FProceduralPolygon_Simple::FProceduralPolygon_Simple(const FRoadTriangulationData& Owner, FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, int SimplePolygonIndex)
101: 	: FProceduralPolygon(Owner)
102: 	, SimplePolygonIndex(SimplePolygonIndex)
103: {
104: 	ResultInfo = { EGeometryResultType::InProgress };
105: 
106: 	const TArray<FVector>& Vertices = Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex].Vertices;
107: 
108: 	// Curb cuts are anchored by arc length along the SOURCE ring (the producer's own Vertices, not the cleaned
109: 	// Points2D below), so the prefix sums are built from it once and reused by every per-vertex evaluation.
110: 	if (Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex].CurbCuts.Num() > 0 && Vertices.Num() >= 3)
111: 	{
112: 		MetaRoad::CurbCutRing::BuildArcLengths(Vertices, RingArcs);
113: 	}
114: 
115: 	TArray<FVector2D> Points2D;
116: 	Points2D.Reserve(Vertices.Num());
117: 	for (auto& It : Vertices)
118: 	{
119: 		Points2D.Add(FVector2D{ It });
120: 	}
121: 
122: 	// Match the magnetized spans BEFORE deciding anything else: whether this behaves as a magnetic fill or as a plain
123: 	// one depends on whether a real path was actually found, not merely on spans being present. Road lanes + loops are
124: 	// already inserted (they precede Simple polygons), so the target geometry is fully available here.
125: 	const TArray<FMagneticSpan>& Spans = Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex].MagneticSpans;
126: 	TBitArray<> SkipSeg(false, Points2D.Num());
127: 	if (Spans.Num() > 0)
128: 	{
129: 		MatchMagneticSpans(Points2D, Spans, SkipSeg);
130: 	}
131: 	const bool bMagneticFill = MagneticSpanRecs.Num() > 0;
132: 
133: 	if (!bMagneticFill)
134: 	{
135: 		// Collapse near-coincident vertices (exact closing dup, the loop seam, and any sub-tolerance
136: 		// vertices) before triangulation — they otherwise produce degenerate sliver triangles / holes.
137: 		// This MUST also run when spans were present but none matched: we then insert the full boundary like any
138: 		// plain fill, and skipping the cleanup there is what leaves unfilled slivers. It is skipped only for a real
139: 		// magnetic fill, whose span indices into Points2D the (index-shifting) cleanup would invalidate.
140: 		GeomUtils::RemoveClosePolylineVertices(Points2D, GeomUtils::DefSnapDistance, /*bClosed*/ true);
141: 		GeomUtils::RemovedPolylineSelfIntersection(Points2D);
142: 	}
143: 
144: 	FVector2D IntersectionPoint;
145: 	FIndex2i IntersectionIndex;
146: 	if (GeomUtils::FindPolylineSelfIntersection(Points2D, IntersectionPoint, IntersectionIndex))
147: 	{
148: 		ResultInfo.SetFailed(FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_SelfIntersectionFailed", "FProceduralPolygon_Simple: {0}: Self intersection found"), GetDescription()));
149: 		return;
150: 	}
151: 
152: 	if (!IntersectApproximation.UpdateAABBTree(Vertices))
153: 	{
154: 		ResultInfo.SetFailed(FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_UpdateAABBTreeFailed", "FProceduralPolygon_Simple: {0}: UpdateAABBTree() failed"), GetDescription()));
155: 		return;
156: 	}
157: 
158: 	int GID = SelectSurfaceGID(GetRoadZone());
159: 
160: 	// A magnetic fill omits its matched spans here (SkipSeg) -- the real path is tagged in OnCompleteArrangement instead
161: 	// of inserted; everything else is inserted as a normal closed ring.
162: 	LineInfo = Builder.InsertClosedRing(Points2D, GID, bMagneticFill ? &SkipSeg : nullptr);
163: 
164: 	if (!LineInfo.IsValid())
165: 	{
166: 		ResultInfo.SetFailed(FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_OutsideLineFailed", "FProceduralPolygon_Simple: {0}: polygon failed "), GetDescription()));
167: 		return;
168: 	}
169: 
170: 	// Holes go in AFTER the boundary of the same polygon and BEFORE its curb cuts. The order is byte-observable
171: 	// (every VID downstream is assigned in insertion order) -- fix it and do not permute it.
172: 	InsertHoleRings(Builder, Points2D, GID);
173: 
174: 	InsertCurbCutContours(Builder, Points2D);
175: 
176: 	ResultInfo.SetSuccess();
177: }

178: 
179: void FProceduralPolygon_Simple::InsertHoleRings(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& OuterPoints2D, int GID)
180: {
181: 	const FRoadPolygonData& Data = Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex];
182: 	if (Data.Holes.Num() == 0)
183: 	{
184: 		return;
185: 	}
186: 
187: 	auto Reject = [this](int32 HoleIndex, const FText& Reason)
188: 	{
189: 		ResultInfo.AddWarning({ 0, FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_HoleRejected",
190: 			"FProceduralPolygon_Simple: {0}: hole {1} {2} -- dropped, the fill is solid there"),
191: 			GetDescription(), HoleIndex, Reason) });
192: 	};
193: 
194: 	// The CLEANED boundary, not Data.Vertices: a hole has to be inside what actually went into the arrangement,
195: 	// which after RemoveClosePolylineVertices / RemovedPolylineSelfIntersection is not quite the producer's ring.
196: 	// (Same reason InsertCurbCutContours takes RingPoints2D rather than re-deriving it.)
197: 	const FPolygon2d OuterPoly(OuterPoints2D);
198: 
199: 	HoleLineInfos.Reserve(Data.Holes.Num());
200: 	HolePoints2D.Reserve(Data.Holes.Num());
201: 
202: 	for (int32 HoleIndex = 0; HoleIndex < Data.Holes.Num(); ++HoleIndex)
203: 	{
204: 		const TArray<FVector>& Source = Data.Holes[HoleIndex].Vertices;
205: 		if (Source.Num() < 3)
206: 		{
207: 			Reject(HoleIndex, LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_HoleTooFewPoints", "has fewer than 3 vertices"));
208: 			continue;
209: 		}
210: 
211: 		TArray<FVector2D> Ring;
212: 		Ring.Reserve(Source.Num());
213: 		for (const FVector& V : Source)
214: 		{
215: 			Ring.Add(FVector2D{ V });
216: 		}
217: 
218: 		// The same cleanup the boundary gets, and UNCONDITIONALLY -- unlike the boundary there is no magnetic-span
219: 		// exemption to make: FMagneticSpan indices address Vertices, i.e. the OUTER ring only, so this
220: 		// index-shifting cleanup has nothing to invalidate here.
221: 		GeomUtils::RemoveClosePolylineVertices(Ring, GeomUtils::DefSnapDistance, /*bClosed*/ true);
222: 		GeomUtils::RemovedPolylineSelfIntersection(Ring);
223: 		if (Ring.Num() < 3)
224: 		{
225: 			Reject(HoleIndex, LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_HoleDegenerate", "collapsed to fewer than 3 vertices"));
226: 			continue;
227: 		}
228: 
229: 		FVector2D IntersectionPoint;
230: 		FIndex2i IntersectionIndex;
231: 		if (GeomUtils::FindPolylineSelfIntersection(Ring, IntersectionPoint, IntersectionIndex))
232: 		{
233: 			Reject(HoleIndex, LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_HoleSelfIntersects", "self-intersects"));
234: 			continue;
235: 		}
236: 
237: 		const FPolygon2d HolePoly(Ring);
238: 
239: 		// STRICTLY inside: FPolygon2d::Contains(FPolygon2d) is all-vertices-inside AND no edge crossing, so a hole
240: 		// coincident with, touching or straddling the boundary is rejected here. That matters more than it looks --
241: 		// a hole equal to the boundary leaves an EMPTY annulus, GetFilledTriangles then returns nothing, the fill
242: 		// claims no triangle, and its own already-inserted vertices end up owned by nobody, which hard-fails the
243: 		// whole scope at the height pass with "Mesh is broken".
244: 		if (!OuterPoly.Contains(HolePoly))
245: 		{
246: 			Reject(HoleIndex, LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_HoleNotInside", "is not strictly inside the boundary"));
247: 			continue;
248: 		}
249: 
250: 		// Overlapping holes: reject the LATER one. Two overlapping CW rings sum to winding -2 in their intersection,
251: 		// which cancels back to a FILLED island inside the hole under NonZeroWinding -- a sliver nobody authored.
252: 		// Merging them would need a boolean and belongs to the editor, not to the build.
253: 		bool bOverlaps = false;
254: 		for (const TArray<FVector2D>& Accepted : HolePoints2D)
---
176: 	ResultInfo.SetSuccess();
177: }
178: 
179: void FProceduralPolygon_Simple::InsertHoleRings(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& OuterPoints2D, int GID)
180: {
181: 	const FRoadPolygonData& Data = Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex];
182: 	if (Data.Holes.Num() == 0)
183: 	{
184: 		return;
185: 	}
186: 
187: 	auto Reject = [this](int32 HoleIndex, const FText& Reason)
188: 	{
189: 		ResultInfo.AddWarning({ 0, FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_HoleRejected",
190: 			"FProceduralPolygon_Simple: {0}: hole {1} {2} -- dropped, the fill is solid there"),
191: 			GetDescription(), HoleIndex, Reason) });
192: 	};
193: 
194: 	// The CLEANED boundary, not Data.Vertices: a hole has to be inside what actually went into the arrangement,
195: 	// which after RemoveClosePolylineVertices / RemovedPolylineSelfIntersection is not quite the producer's ring.
196: 	// (Same reason InsertCurbCutContours takes RingPoints2D rather than re-deriving it.)
197: 	const FPolygon2d OuterPoly(OuterPoints2D);
198: 
199: 	HoleLineInfos.Reserve(Data.Holes.Num());
200: 	HolePoints2D.Reserve(Data.Holes.Num());
201: 
202: 	for (int32 HoleIndex = 0; HoleIndex < Data.Holes.Num(); ++HoleIndex)
203: 	{
204: 		const TArray<FVector>& Source = Data.Holes[HoleIndex].Vertices;
205: 		if (Source.Num() < 3)
206: 		{
207: 			Reject(HoleIndex, LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_HoleTooFewPoints", "has fewer than 3 vertices"));
208: 			continue;
209: 		}
210: 
211: 		TArray<FVector2D> Ring;
212: 		Ring.Reserve(Source.Num());
213: 		for (const FVector& V : Source)
214: 		{
215: 			Ring.Add(FVector2D{ V });
216: 		}
217: 
218: 		// The same cleanup the boundary gets, and UNCONDITIONALLY -- unlike the boundary there is no magnetic-span
219: 		// exemption to make: FMagneticSpan indices address Vertices, i.e. the OUTER ring only, so this
220: 		// index-shifting cleanup has nothing to invalidate here.
221: 		GeomUtils::RemoveClosePolylineVertices(Ring, GeomUtils::DefSnapDistance, /*bClosed*/ true);
222: 		GeomUtils::RemovedPolylineSelfIntersection(Ring);
223: 		if (Ring.Num() < 3)
224: 		{
225: 			Reject(HoleIndex, LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_HoleDegenerate", "collapsed to fewer than 3 vertices"));
226: 			continue;
227: 		}
228: 
229: 		FVector2D IntersectionPoint;
230: 		FIndex2i IntersectionIndex;
231: 		if (GeomUtils::FindPolylineSelfIntersection(Ring, IntersectionPoint, IntersectionIndex))
232: 		{
233: 			Reject(HoleIndex, LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_HoleSelfIntersects", "self-intersects"));
234: 			continue;
235: 		}
236: 
237: 		const FPolygon2d HolePoly(Ring);
238: 
239: 		// STRICTLY inside: FPolygon2d::Contains(FPolygon2d) is all-vertices-inside AND no edge crossing, so a hole
240: 		// coincident with, touching or straddling the boundary is rejected here. That matters more than it looks --
241: 		// a hole equal to the boundary leaves an EMPTY annulus, GetFilledTriangles then returns nothing, the fill
242: 		// claims no triangle, and its own already-inserted vertices end up owned by nobody, which hard-fails the
243: 		// whole scope at the height pass with "Mesh is broken".
244: 		if (!OuterPoly.Contains(HolePoly))
245: 		{
246: 			Reject(HoleIndex, LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_HoleNotInside", "is not strictly inside the boundary"));
247: 			continue;
248: 		}
249: 
250: 		// Overlapping holes: reject the LATER one. Two overlapping CW rings sum to winding -2 in their intersection,
251: 		// which cancels back to a FILLED island inside the hole under NonZeroWinding -- a sliver nobody authored.
252: 		// Merging them would need a boolean and belongs to the editor, not to the build.
253: 		bool bOverlaps = false;
254: 		for (const TArray<FVector2D>& Accepted : HolePoints2D)
255: 		{
256: 			if (FPolygon2d(Accepted).Overlaps(HolePoly))
257: 			{
258: 				bOverlaps = true;
259: 				break;
260: 			}
261: 		}
262: 		if (bOverlaps)
263: 		{
264: 			Reject(HoleIndex, LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_HoleOverlaps", "overlaps another hole"));
265: 			continue;
266: 		}
267: 
268: 		// The SAME GID as the boundary: a hole edge IS a surface boundary (the surface ends there), so it has to
269: 		// read as one to FindBoundaries' `GID != CenterLine` filter and to the boundary vertex-colour pass. Its own
270: 		// PID -- which InsertClosedRing allocates per call -- is what makes it recoverable independently.
271: 		const FLineInfo Info = Builder.InsertClosedRing(Ring, GID);
272: 		if (!Info.IsValid())
273: 		{
274: 			Reject(HoleIndex, LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_HoleInsertFailed", "could not be inserted"));
275: 			continue;
276: 		}
---
425: 		const double Dot = uix * ujx + uiy * ujy;
426: 		const double Cross = uix * ujy - uiy * ujx;
427: 		const double Denom = R[i] * R[j] + Dot;
428: 		TanHalf[i] = (FMath::Abs(Denom) > 1e-9) ? (Cross / Denom) : 0.0;
429: 	}
430: 	double SumW = 0.0, SumWH = 0.0;
431: 	double MinH = Data.VertexHeights[0], MaxH = Data.VertexHeights[0];
432: 	for (int32 i = 0; i < N; ++i)
433: 	{
434: 		const int32 iPrev = (i + N - 1) % N;
435: 		const double W = (TanHalf[iPrev] + TanHalf[i]) / R[i];
436: 		SumW += W;
437: 		SumWH += W * Data.VertexHeights[i];
438: 		MinH = FMath::Min(MinH, Data.VertexHeights[i]);
439: 		MaxH = FMath::Max(MaxH, Data.VertexHeights[i]);
440: 	}
441: 	const double Result = (FMath::Abs(SumW) > 1e-12) ? (SumWH / SumW) : Data.VertexHeights[BestSeg];
442: 	return FMath::Clamp(Result, MinH, MaxH);
443: }
444: 
445: void FProceduralPolygon_Simple::InsertCurbCutContours(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& RingPoints2D)
446: {
447: 	const FRoadPolygonData& Data = Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex];
448: 	if (Data.CurbCuts.Num() == 0 || RingArcs.Num() < 2)
449: 	{
450: 		return;
451: 	}
452: 
453: 	const double RingLength = RingArcs.Last();
454: 	const double Sign = MetaRoad::CurbCutRing::InwardSign(Data.Vertices);
455: 	const FPolygon2d RingPoly(RingPoints2D);
456: 
457: 	// Built once, outside the per-cut loop.
458: 	TArray<FPolygon2d> HolePolys;
459: 	HolePolys.Reserve(HolePoints2D.Num());
460: 	for (const TArray<FVector2D>& Hole : HolePoints2D)
461: 	{
462: 		HolePolys.Emplace(Hole);
463: 	}
464: 
465: 	// "Inside the SURFACE", not merely inside the ring. Inside a hole is OUTSIDE the surface: the fill claims
466: 	// nothing there, so a vertex placed in it would belong to no polygon and hard-fail the height pass -- exactly
467: 	// the failure the outside-the-ring rejection below was written for.
468: 	auto IsInSurface = [&RingPoly, &HolePolys](const FVector2D& P)
469: 	{
470: 		const FVector2d P2d(P.X, P.Y);
471: 		if (!RingPoly.Contains(P2d))
472: 		{
473: 			return false;
474: 		}
475: 		for (const FPolygon2d& Hole : HolePolys)
476: 		{
477: 			if (Hole.Contains(P2d))
478: 			{
479: 				return false;
480: 			}
481: 		}
482: 		return true;
483: 	};
484: 
485: 	auto ToWorld = [&](double S, double Inward)
486: 	{
487: 		return MetaRoad::CurbCutRing::SampleInward(Data.Vertices, RingArcs, S, Inward, Sign);
488: 	};
489: 
490: 	for (const FRoadCurbCutPlacement& Placement : Data.CurbCuts)
491: 	{
492: 		const FRoadCurbCut& Cut = Placement.Cut;
493: 		if (Cut.Depth <= UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
494: 		{
495: 			continue;
496: 		}
497: 
498: 		// FIRST, and unconditionally: the site is the HEIGHT field, the contour below is only the crease geometry
499: 		// that makes it crisp. Registering it at the end of this body (where it started) meant every early-out
500: 		// under it — above all the contour-outside-the-ring rejection — silently switched the whole ramp off
501: 		// instead of just leaving it coarse. A Depth slightly too large for the local curvature is an authoring
502: 		// slip, not a reason to ignore the cut.
503: 		RegisterCurbCutSite(Builder, Placement);
504: 
505: 		// Unwrapped so the chain stays monotonic; SampleInward wraps each S itself.
506: 		TArray<double> InnerS;
507: 		Cut.GetAdditionalOffsets(Placement.ArcOffset, 1.0, InnerS);
508: 		if (InnerS.Num() < 2)
509: 		{
510: 			continue;
511: 		}
512: 
513: 		// ---- Ring-side vertices: SPLIT the seam, never lay a second chain beside it -------------------------
514: 		// The ring the cut needs vertices on is a SEAM — the boundary this fill shares with whatever abuts it —
515: 		// and the two sides share it by sharing its arrangement edges. So a vertex the ramp needs there has to
516: 		// come from splitting one of those edges; SplitEdge copies GID and PID onto both halves, so both
517: 		// recovered boundaries pick it up and the joint stays vertex-for-vertex identical.
518: 		//
519: 		// An earlier version inserted a chain sampled from this fill's own Data.Vertices instead. On a MAGNETIZED
520: 		// span that is precisely what the design forbids: the span deliberately inserts nothing because those
521: 		// samples are only an approximation of the real edge, so re-inserting them crossed the seam, split it at
522: 		// arbitrary points and left each side holding vertices the other did not have.
523: 		//
524: 		// SnapRadius covers that approximation error without reaching a different edge.
525: 		constexpr double SnapRadius = 15.0;
---
428: 		TanHalf[i] = (FMath::Abs(Denom) > 1e-9) ? (Cross / Denom) : 0.0;
429: 	}
430: 	double SumW = 0.0, SumWH = 0.0;
431: 	double MinH = Data.VertexHeights[0], MaxH = Data.VertexHeights[0];
432: 	for (int32 i = 0; i < N; ++i)
433: 	{
434: 		const int32 iPrev = (i + N - 1) % N;
435: 		const double W = (TanHalf[iPrev] + TanHalf[i]) / R[i];
436: 		SumW += W;
437: 		SumWH += W * Data.VertexHeights[i];
438: 		MinH = FMath::Min(MinH, Data.VertexHeights[i]);
439: 		MaxH = FMath::Max(MaxH, Data.VertexHeights[i]);
440: 	}
441: 	const double Result = (FMath::Abs(SumW) > 1e-12) ? (SumWH / SumW) : Data.VertexHeights[BestSeg];
442: 	return FMath::Clamp(Result, MinH, MaxH);
443: }
444: 
445: void FProceduralPolygon_Simple::InsertCurbCutContours(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& RingPoints2D)
446: {
447: 	const FRoadPolygonData& Data = Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex];
448: 	if (Data.CurbCuts.Num() == 0 || RingArcs.Num() < 2)
449: 	{
450: 		return;
451: 	}
452: 
453: 	const double RingLength = RingArcs.Last();
454: 	const double Sign = MetaRoad::CurbCutRing::InwardSign(Data.Vertices);
455: 	const FPolygon2d RingPoly(RingPoints2D);
456: 
457: 	// Built once, outside the per-cut loop.
458: 	TArray<FPolygon2d> HolePolys;
459: 	HolePolys.Reserve(HolePoints2D.Num());
460: 	for (const TArray<FVector2D>& Hole : HolePoints2D)
461: 	{
462: 		HolePolys.Emplace(Hole);
463: 	}
464: 
465: 	// "Inside the SURFACE", not merely inside the ring. Inside a hole is OUTSIDE the surface: the fill claims
466: 	// nothing there, so a vertex placed in it would belong to no polygon and hard-fail the height pass -- exactly
467: 	// the failure the outside-the-ring rejection below was written for.
468: 	auto IsInSurface = [&RingPoly, &HolePolys](const FVector2D& P)
469: 	{
470: 		const FVector2d P2d(P.X, P.Y);
471: 		if (!RingPoly.Contains(P2d))
472: 		{
473: 			return false;
474: 		}
475: 		for (const FPolygon2d& Hole : HolePolys)
476: 		{
477: 			if (Hole.Contains(P2d))
478: 			{
479: 				return false;
480: 			}
481: 		}
482: 		return true;
483: 	};
484: 
485: 	auto ToWorld = [&](double S, double Inward)
486: 	{
487: 		return MetaRoad::CurbCutRing::SampleInward(Data.Vertices, RingArcs, S, Inward, Sign);
488: 	};
489: 
490: 	for (const FRoadCurbCutPlacement& Placement : Data.CurbCuts)
491: 	{
492: 		const FRoadCurbCut& Cut = Placement.Cut;
493: 		if (Cut.Depth <= UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
494: 		{
495: 			continue;
496: 		}
497: 
498: 		// FIRST, and unconditionally: the site is the HEIGHT field, the contour below is only the crease geometry
499: 		// that makes it crisp. Registering it at the end of this body (where it started) meant every early-out
500: 		// under it — above all the contour-outside-the-ring rejection — silently switched the whole ramp off
501: 		// instead of just leaving it coarse. A Depth slightly too large for the local curvature is an authoring
502: 		// slip, not a reason to ignore the cut.
503: 		RegisterCurbCutSite(Builder, Placement);
504: 
505: 		// Unwrapped so the chain stays monotonic; SampleInward wraps each S itself.
506: 		TArray<double> InnerS;
507: 		Cut.GetAdditionalOffsets(Placement.ArcOffset, 1.0, InnerS);
508: 		if (InnerS.Num() < 2)
509: 		{
510: 			continue;
511: 		}
512: 
513: 		// ---- Ring-side vertices: SPLIT the seam, never lay a second chain beside it -------------------------
514: 		// The ring the cut needs vertices on is a SEAM — the boundary this fill shares with whatever abuts it —
515: 		// and the two sides share it by sharing its arrangement edges. So a vertex the ramp needs there has to
516: 		// come from splitting one of those edges; SplitEdge copies GID and PID onto both halves, so both
517: 		// recovered boundaries pick it up and the joint stays vertex-for-vertex identical.
518: 		//
519: 		// An earlier version inserted a chain sampled from this fill's own Data.Vertices instead. On a MAGNETIZED
520: 		// span that is precisely what the design forbids: the span deliberately inserts nothing because those
521: 		// samples are only an approximation of the real edge, so re-inserting them crossed the seam, split it at
522: 		// arbitrary points and left each side holding vertices the other did not have.
523: 		//
524: 		// SnapRadius covers that approximation error without reaching a different edge.
525: 		constexpr double SnapRadius = 15.0;
526: 		{
527: 			TArray<double> OuterS;
528: 			Cut.GetAdditionalOffsets(Placement.ArcOffset, 0.0, OuterS);
---
434: 		const int32 iPrev = (i + N - 1) % N;
435: 		const double W = (TanHalf[iPrev] + TanHalf[i]) / R[i];
436: 		SumW += W;
437: 		SumWH += W * Data.VertexHeights[i];
438: 		MinH = FMath::Min(MinH, Data.VertexHeights[i]);
439: 		MaxH = FMath::Max(MaxH, Data.VertexHeights[i]);
440: 	}
441: 	const double Result = (FMath::Abs(SumW) > 1e-12) ? (SumWH / SumW) : Data.VertexHeights[BestSeg];
442: 	return FMath::Clamp(Result, MinH, MaxH);
443: }

445: void FProceduralPolygon_Simple::InsertCurbCutContours(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& RingPoints2D)
446: {
447: 	const FRoadPolygonData& Data = Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex];
448: 	if (Data.CurbCuts.Num() == 0 || RingArcs.Num() < 2)
449: 	{
450: 		return;
451: 	}
452:

454: 	const double Sign = MetaRoad::CurbCutRing::InwardSign(Data.Vertices);
455: 	const FPolygon2d RingPoly(RingPoints2D);

460: 	for (const TArray<FVector2D>& Hole : HolePoints2D)

467: 	// the failure the outside-the-ring rejection below was written for.

471: 		if (!RingPoly.Contains(P2d))
472: 		{
473: 			return false;
474: 		}
475: 		for (const FPolygon2d& Hole : HolePolys)
476: 		{
477: 			if (Hole.Contains(P2d))
478: 			{
479: 				return false;
480: 			}
481: 		}
482: 		return true;

487: 		return MetaRoad::CurbCutRing::SampleInward(Data.Vertices, RingArcs, S, Inward, Sign);

502: 		// slip, not a reason to ignore the cut.
503: 		RegisterCurbCutSite(Builder, Placement);
504:

506: 		TArray<double> InnerS;
507: 		Cut.GetAdditionalOffsets(Placement.ArcOffset, 1.0, InnerS);
508: 		if (InnerS.Num() < 2)
509: 		{
510: 			continue;
511: 		}
512: 
513: 		// ---- Ring-side vertices: SPLIT the seam, never lay a second chain beside it -------------------------

517: 		// recovered boundaries pick it up and the joint stays vertex-for-vertex identical.
518: 		//
519: 		// An earlier version inserted a chain sampled from this fill's own Data.Vertices instead. On a MAGNETIZED
520: 		// span that is precisely what the design forbids: the span deliberately inserts nothing because those
521: 		// samples are only an approximation of the real edge, so re-inserting them crossed the seam, split it at
522: 		// arbitrary points and left each side holding vertices the other did not have.
523: 		//
524: 		// SnapRadius covers that approximation error without reaching a different edge.
525: 		constexpr double SnapRadius = 15.0;

530: 			{

532: 				// to be connected afterwards: the seam edge itself is what joins the new vertices.

545: 		{
546: 			const FVector2D P = ToWorld(S, Cut.Depth);
547: 			if (!IsInSurface(P))
548: 			{
549: 				bInside = false;
550: 				break;
551: 			}
552: 			Chain.Add(P);
553: 		}
554: 		if (!bInside)
555: 		{
556: 			continue;
557: 		}
558: 
559: 		// PID = -1: FindPolyline recovers this fill's boundary by Dijkstra over PID-tagged edges, so a contour
560: 		// tagged with the fill's PID would let the recovered ring short-cut across the cut window.
561: 		for (int i = 0; i + 1 < Chain.Num(); ++i)
562: 		{
563: 			Builder.AddConstraintEdge(Chain[i], Chain[i + 1]);
564: 		}
565: 
566: 		// Rungs. The OUTER end is snapped onto the seam for the same reason as above — an unsnapped one would
567: 		// re-introduce the very crossing the block above exists to avoid, four times per cut. The inner end is
568: 		// strictly inside this fill, where no seam exists and a plain insert is correct.
569: 		const FInterpCurve<double> OuterCurve = Cut.GetCurveByProgress(Placement.ArcOffset, 0.0);
570: 		const FInterpCurve<double> InnerCurve = Cut.GetCurveByProgress(Placement.ArcOffset, 1.0);
571: 		if (OuterCurve.Points.Num() == InnerCurve.Points.Num())
572: 		{
---
483: 	};
484: 
485: 	auto ToWorld = [&](double S, double Inward)
486: 	{
487: 		return MetaRoad::CurbCutRing::SampleInward(Data.Vertices, RingArcs, S, Inward, Sign);
488: 	};
489: 
490: 	for (const FRoadCurbCutPlacement& Placement : Data.CurbCuts)
491: 	{
492: 		const FRoadCurbCut& Cut = Placement.Cut;
493: 		if (Cut.Depth <= UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
494: 		{
495: 			continue;
496: 		}
497: 
498: 		// FIRST, and unconditionally: the site is the HEIGHT field, the contour below is only the crease geometry
499: 		// that makes it crisp. Registering it at the end of this body (where it started) meant every early-out
500: 		// under it — above all the contour-outside-the-ring rejection — silently switched the whole ramp off
501: 		// instead of just leaving it coarse. A Depth slightly too large for the local curvature is an authoring
502: 		// slip, not a reason to ignore the cut.
503: 		RegisterCurbCutSite(Builder, Placement);
504: 
505: 		// Unwrapped so the chain stays monotonic; SampleInward wraps each S itself.
506: 		TArray<double> InnerS;
507: 		Cut.GetAdditionalOffsets(Placement.ArcOffset, 1.0, InnerS);
508: 		if (InnerS.Num() < 2)
509: 		{
510: 			continue;
511: 		}
512: 
513: 		// ---- Ring-side vertices: SPLIT the seam, never lay a second chain beside it -------------------------
514: 		// The ring the cut needs vertices on is a SEAM — the boundary this fill shares with whatever abuts it —
515: 		// and the two sides share it by sharing its arrangement edges. So a vertex the ramp needs there has to
516: 		// come from splitting one of those edges; SplitEdge copies GID and PID onto both halves, so both
517: 		// recovered boundaries pick it up and the joint stays vertex-for-vertex identical.
518: 		//
519: 		// An earlier version inserted a chain sampled from this fill's own Data.Vertices instead. On a MAGNETIZED
520: 		// span that is precisely what the design forbids: the span deliberately inserts nothing because those
521: 		// samples are only an approximation of the real edge, so re-inserting them crossed the seam, split it at
522: 		// arbitrary points and left each side holding vertices the other did not have.
523: 		//
524: 		// SnapRadius covers that approximation error without reaching a different edge.
525: 		constexpr double SnapRadius = 15.0;
526: 		{
527: 			TArray<double> OuterS;
528: 			Cut.GetAdditionalOffsets(Placement.ArcOffset, 0.0, OuterS);
529: 			for (double S : OuterS)
530: 			{
531: 				// The split IS the effect — it puts the vertex on the edge both sides already share. Nothing has
532: 				// to be connected afterwards: the seam edge itself is what joins the new vertices.
533: 				FVector2D Unused;
534: 				Builder.SplitConstraintNear(ToWorld(S, 0.0), SnapRadius, Unused);
535: 			}
536: 		}
537: 
538: 		// A contour point outside the SURFACE would belong to NO polygon and hard-fail the height pass, which is
539: 		// what a Depth larger than the local inner curvature radius produces — or, once this fill has holes, a
540: 		// Depth that reaches into one. Reject the whole contour then — a partial one strands a vertex just the same.
541: 		TArray<FVector2D> Chain;
542: 		Chain.Reserve(InnerS.Num());
543: 		bool bInside = true;
544: 		for (double S : InnerS)
545: 		{
546: 			const FVector2D P = ToWorld(S, Cut.Depth);
547: 			if (!IsInSurface(P))
548: 			{
549: 				bInside = false;
550: 				break;
551: 			}
552: 			Chain.Add(P);
553: 		}
554: 		if (!bInside)
555: 		{
556: 			continue;
557: 		}
558: 
559: 		// PID = -1: FindPolyline recovers this fill's boundary by Dijkstra over PID-tagged edges, so a contour
560: 		// tagged with the fill's PID would let the recovered ring short-cut across the cut window.
561: 		for (int i = 0; i + 1 < Chain.Num(); ++i)
562: 		{
563: 			Builder.AddConstraintEdge(Chain[i], Chain[i + 1]);
564: 		}
565: 
566: 		// Rungs. The OUTER end is snapped onto the seam for the same reason as above — an unsnapped one would
567: 		// re-introduce the very crossing the block above exists to avoid, four times per cut. The inner end is
568: 		// strictly inside this fill, where no seam exists and a plain insert is correct.
569: 		const FInterpCurve<double> OuterCurve = Cut.GetCurveByProgress(Placement.ArcOffset, 0.0);
570: 		const FInterpCurve<double> InnerCurve = Cut.GetCurveByProgress(Placement.ArcOffset, 1.0);
571: 		if (OuterCurve.Points.Num() == InnerCurve.Points.Num())
572: 		{
573: 			for (int i = 0; i < OuterCurve.Points.Num(); ++i)
574: 			{
575: 				FVector2D Outer;
576: 				if (!Builder.SplitConstraintNear(ToWorld(OuterCurve.Points[i].InVal, 0.0), SnapRadius, Outer))
577: 				{
578: 					continue;
579: 				}
580: 				const FVector2D Inner = ToWorld(InnerCurve.Points[i].InVal, Cut.Depth);
581: 
582: 				// The rungs sample S independently of InnerS above, so passing that check does not cover these
583: 				// points. Skipping ONE rung is safe where skipping one chain point is not: a rung is a single
---
576: 				if (!Builder.SplitConstraintNear(ToWorld(OuterCurve.Points[i].InVal, 0.0), SnapRadius, Outer))
577: 				{
578: 					continue;
579: 				}
580: 				const FVector2D Inner = ToWorld(InnerCurve.Points[i].InVal, Cut.Depth);
581: 
582: 				// The rungs sample S independently of InnerS above, so passing that check does not cover these
583: 				// points. Skipping ONE rung is safe where skipping one chain point is not: a rung is a single
584: 				// standalone edge, so it strands nothing.
585: 				if (!IsInSurface(Inner))
586: 				{
587: 					continue;
588: 				}
589: 
590: 				Builder.AddConstraintEdge(Outer, Inner);
591: 			}
592: 		}
593: 	}
594: }
595: 
596: void FProceduralPolygon_Simple::RegisterCurbCutSite(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const FRoadCurbCutPlacement& Placement) const
597: {
598: 	const FRoadPolygonData& Data = Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex];
599: 
600: 	FRoadCurbCutSite Site;
601: 	Site.ArcOffset = Placement.ArcOffset;
602: 	Site.RingLength = RingArcs.Last();
603: 	Site.Cut = Placement.Cut;
604: 	MetaRoad::CurbCutRing::ExtractWindow(Data.Vertices, RingArcs, Placement.ArcOffset,
605: 		Placement.Cut.GetSpan() * 0.5, Site.Chain, Site.Arcs);
606: 
607: 	Builder.AddCurbCutSite(MoveTemp(Site));
608: }
609: 
610: void FProceduralPolygon_Simple::FillVertexInfo(FRoadVertexInfo& VertexInfo, int VID, FVector2d V2d) const
611: {
612: 	FVector Point;
613: 	FVector Normal;
614: 	IntersectApproximation.Intersect(V2d, Point, Normal);
615: 
616: 	VertexInfo.Pos.Location.Z = Point.Z;
617: 	VertexInfo.Pos.Quat = FQuat::FindBetweenNormals(FVector::UpVector, Normal);
618: 	VertexInfo.Pos.ROffset = 0;
619: 	VertexInfo.Pos.SOffset = 0;
620: 
621: 	if (const FRoadZoneSidewalk* LaneSidewalk = GetRoadZone().GetPtr<FRoadZoneSidewalk>())
622: 	{
623: 		VertexInfo.HeightOffset = LaneSidewalk->DefaultHeight;
624: 
625: 		// Per-node height (Phase 3): if the Sidewalk spline supplied per-vertex heights, evaluate this vertex by
626: 		// projecting its 2D position onto the source ring. Empty array (crosswalks, pre-feature sidewalks) -> keep
627: 		// the flat DefaultHeight (bit-for-bit unchanged).
628: 		const FRoadPolygonData& Data = Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex];
629: 		if (Data.VertexHeights.Num() == Data.Vertices.Num() && Data.Vertices.Num() >= 2)
630: 		{
631: 			// Curb cuts are NOT applied here. They are registered as scope-wide sites and applied to every vertex
632: 			// in reach by BuildStages::ApplyCurbCuts — doing it from this polygon reached only the vertices it
633: 			// owns, which is exactly where the ramp used to stop at the seam with the road lane.
634: 			VertexInfo.HeightOffset = EvaluateHeightAt2D(V2d);
635: 		}
636: 	}
637: }
638: 
639: const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>& FProceduralPolygon_Simple::GetRoadZone() const
640: {
641: 	return Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex].RoadZone;
642: }
643: 
644: FText FProceduralPolygon_Simple::GetDescription() const
645: {
646: 	return LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_GetDescription", "SimplePoly");
647: }
648: 
649: bool FProceduralPolygon_Simple::SetUVLayers(FDynamicMesh3& Mesh, int TID) const
650: {
651: 	return SetPlanarDensityUVLayers(Mesh, TID, Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex].Texture);
652: }
653: 
654: double FProceduralPolygon_Simple::GetMaterialPriority() const
655: {
656: 	// Tie-break among equal zone priorities, following the road family's convention: earlier index wins, and
657: 	// the term stays inside [0, 1e-6) so it can never cross a zone boundary.
658: 	//
659: 	// The line was copied from FProceduralPolygon_RoadBase, where GetObjectIndex() returns SplineIndex and
660: 	// `Splines.Num() - GetObjectIndex() - 1` is consequently non-negative. Here GetObjectIndex() returns
661: 	// `Splines.Num() + SimplePolygonIndex`, so the Splines.Num() term CANCELS and what remained was
662: 	// -(SimplePolygonIndex + 1)/1e6 -- always negative. A fill could therefore never win a tie against a
663: 	// lane, and later fills sorted BELOW earlier ones: both backwards from the convention it was copied from.
664: 	// NORMALISED by the count, not a raw index over 1e6. The nudge has to stay strictly inside the band below
665: 	// the one above it: FProceduralPolygon_RoadBase adds MaterialPriority/1000, so anything reaching 1e-3 stops
666: 	// being a tie-break and starts reordering across material priorities. A raw index does reach it -- fills are
667: 	// per-marking (every crosswalk, island and arrow is one), and a city scope having a thousand of them is
668: 	// ordinary, whereas the road family's identical-looking expression is indexed by SPLINE and never got near.
669: 	const int32 NumSimple = FMath::Max(1, Owner.SurfacePolygons.Num());
670: 	const double Tie = double(NumSimple - SimplePolygonIndex - 1) / double(NumSimple); // [0, 1)
671: 	return ComputeZoneProfilePriority(GetRoadZone()) + Tie / 1000000.0;
672: }
673: 
674: int FProceduralPolygon_Simple::GetObjectIndex() const
675: {
676: 	return 	Owner.Inputs.Splines.Num() + SimplePolygonIndex;
---
580: 				const FVector2D Inner = ToWorld(InnerCurve.Points[i].InVal, Cut.Depth);
581: 
582: 				// The rungs sample S independently of InnerS above, so passing that check does not cover these
583: 				// points. Skipping ONE rung is safe where skipping one chain point is not: a rung is a single
584: 				// standalone edge, so it strands nothing.
585: 				if (!IsInSurface(Inner))
586: 				{
587: 					continue;
588: 				}
589: 
590: 				Builder.AddConstraintEdge(Outer, Inner);
591: 			}
592: 		}
593: 	}
594: }

599:

601: 	Site.ArcOffset = Placement.ArcOffset;
602: 	Site.RingLength = RingArcs.Last();
603: 	Site.Cut = Placement.Cut;
604: 	MetaRoad::CurbCutRing::ExtractWindow(Data.Vertices, RingArcs, Placement.ArcOffset,
605: 		Placement.Cut.GetSpan() * 0.5, Site.Chain, Site.Arcs);
606: 
607: 	Builder.AddCurbCutSite(MoveTemp(Site));
608: }

610: void FProceduralPolygon_Simple::FillVertexInfo(FRoadVertexInfo& VertexInfo, int VID, FVector2d V2d) const
611: {
612: 	FVector Point;
613: 	FVector Normal;
614: 	IntersectApproximation.Intersect(V2d, Point, Normal);
615: 
616: 	VertexInfo.Pos.Location.Z = Point.Z;
617: 	VertexInfo.Pos.Quat = FQuat::FindBetweenNormals(FVector::UpVector, Normal);
618: 	VertexInfo.Pos.ROffset = 0;
619: 	VertexInfo.Pos.SOffset = 0;
620: 
621: 	if (const FRoadZoneSidewalk* LaneSidewalk = GetRoadZone().GetPtr<FRoadZoneSidewalk>())
622: 	{
623: 		VertexInfo.HeightOffset = LaneSidewalk->DefaultHeight;
624: 
625: 		// Per-node height (Phase 3): if the Sidewalk spline supplied per-vertex heights, evaluate this vertex by
626: 		// projecting its 2D position onto the source ring. Empty array (crosswalks, pre-feature sidewalks) -> keep
627: 		// the flat DefaultHeight (bit-for-bit unchanged).
628: 		const FRoadPolygonData& Data = Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex];
629: 		if (Data.VertexHeights.Num() == Data.Vertices.Num() && Data.Vertices.Num() >= 2)
630: 		{
631: 			// Curb cuts are NOT applied here. They are registered as scope-wide sites and applied to every vertex
632: 			// in reach by BuildStages::ApplyCurbCuts — doing it from this polygon reached only the vertices it
633: 			// owns, which is exactly where the ramp used to stop at the seam with the road lane.
634: 			VertexInfo.HeightOffset = EvaluateHeightAt2D(V2d);
635: 		}
636: 	}
637: }
638: 
639: const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>& FProceduralPolygon_Simple::GetRoadZone() const
640: {
641: 	return Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex].RoadZone;
642: }
643: 
644: FText FProceduralPolygon_Simple::GetDescription() const
645: {
646: 	return LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_GetDescription", "SimplePoly");
647: }
648: 
649: bool FProceduralPolygon_Simple::SetUVLayers(FDynamicMesh3& Mesh, int TID) const
650: {
651: 	return SetPlanarDensityUVLayers(Mesh, TID, Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex].Texture);
652: }
653: 
654: double FProceduralPolygon_Simple::GetMaterialPriority() const
655: {
656: 	// Tie-break among equal zone priorities, following the road family's convention: earlier index wins, and
657: 	// the term stays inside [0, 1e-6) so it can never cross a zone boundary.
658: 	//
659: 	// The line was copied from FProceduralPolygon_RoadBase, where GetObjectIndex() returns SplineIndex and
660: 	// `Splines.Num() - GetObjectIndex() - 1` is consequently non-negative. Here GetObjectIndex() returns
661: 	// `Splines.Num() + SimplePolygonIndex`, so the Splines.Num() term CANCELS and what remained was
662: 	// -(SimplePolygonIndex + 1)/1e6 -- always negative. A fill could therefore never win a tie against a
663: 	// lane, and later fills sorted BELOW earlier ones: both backwards from the convention it was copied from.
664: 	// NORMALISED by the count, not a raw index over 1e6. The nudge has to stay strictly inside the band below
665: 	// the one above it: FProceduralPolygon_RoadBase adds MaterialPriority/1000, so anything reaching 1e-3 stops
666: 	// being a tie-break and starts reordering across material priorities. A raw index does reach it -- fills are
667: 	// per-marking (every crosswalk, island and arrow is one), and a city scope having a thousand of them is
668: 	// ordinary, whereas the road family's identical-looking expression is indexed by SPLINE and never got near.
669: 	const int32 NumSimple = FMath::Max(1, Owner.SurfacePolygons.Num());
670: 	const double Tie = double(NumSimple - SimplePolygonIndex - 1) / double(NumSimple); // [0, 1)
671: 	return ComputeZoneProfilePriority(GetRoadZone()) + Tie / 1000000.0;
672: }
673: 
674: int FProceduralPolygon_Simple::GetObjectIndex() const
675: {
676: 	return 	Owner.Inputs.Splines.Num() + SimplePolygonIndex;
677: }
678: 
679: 
680: #undef LOCTEXT_NAMESPACE
---
612: 	FVector Point;
613: 	FVector Normal;
614: 	IntersectApproximation.Intersect(V2d, Point, Normal);
615: 
616: 	VertexInfo.Pos.Location.Z = Point.Z;
617: 	VertexInfo.Pos.Quat = FQuat::FindBetweenNormals(FVector::UpVector, Normal);
618: 	VertexInfo.Pos.ROffset = 0;
619: 	VertexInfo.Pos.SOffset = 0;
620: 
621: 	if (const FRoadZoneSidewalk* LaneSidewalk = GetRoadZone().GetPtr<FRoadZoneSidewalk>())
622: 	{
623: 		VertexInfo.HeightOffset = LaneSidewalk->DefaultHeight;
624: 
625: 		// Per-node height (Phase 3): if the Sidewalk spline supplied per-vertex heights, evaluate this vertex by
626: 		// projecting its 2D position onto the source ring. Empty array (crosswalks, pre-feature sidewalks) -> keep
627: 		// the flat DefaultHeight (bit-for-bit unchanged).
628: 		const FRoadPolygonData& Data = Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex];
629: 		if (Data.VertexHeights.Num() == Data.Vertices.Num() && Data.Vertices.Num() >= 2)
630: 		{
631: 			// Curb cuts are NOT applied here. They are registered as scope-wide sites and applied to every vertex
632: 			// in reach by BuildStages::ApplyCurbCuts — doing it from this polygon reached only the vertices it
633: 			// owns, which is exactly where the ramp used to stop at the seam with the road lane.
634: 			VertexInfo.HeightOffset = EvaluateHeightAt2D(V2d);
635: 		}
636: 	}
637: }
638: 
639: const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>& FProceduralPolygon_Simple::GetRoadZone() const
640: {
641: 	return Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex].RoadZone;
642: }
643: 
644: FText FProceduralPolygon_Simple::GetDescription() const
645: {
646: 	return LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_Simple_GetDescription", "SimplePoly");
647: }
648: 
649: bool FProceduralPolygon_Simple::SetUVLayers(FDynamicMesh3& Mesh, int TID) const
650: {
651: 	return SetPlanarDensityUVLayers(Mesh, TID, Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex].Texture);
652: }

657: 	// the term stays inside [0, 1e-6) so it can never cross a zone boundary.
658: 	//
659: 	// The line was copied from FProceduralPolygon_RoadBase, where GetObjectIndex() returns SplineIndex and
660: 	// `Splines.Num() - GetObjectIndex() - 1` is consequently non-negative. Here GetObjectIndex() returns
661: 	// `Splines.Num() + SimplePolygonIndex`, so the Splines.Num() term CANCELS and what remained was
662: 	// -(SimplePolygonIndex + 1)/1e6 -- always negative. A fill could therefore never win a tie against a
663: 	// lane, and later fills sorted BELOW earlier ones: both backwards from the convention it was copied from.
664: 	// NORMALISED by the count, not a raw index over 1e6. The nudge has to stay strictly inside the band below
665: 	// the one above it: FProceduralPolygon_RoadBase adds MaterialPriority/1000, so anything reaching 1e-3 stops

31: 
32: static void RemovePointsFromBegin(TArray<FVector2D>& Vertices, double Distance)
33: {
34: 	if (Vertices.Num() && Distance >= 0)
35: 	{
36: 		double Dt = 0;
37: 		for (int i = 1; i < Vertices.Num(); ++i)
38: 		{
39: 			Dt += (Vertices[i] - Vertices[i - 1]).Length();
40: 			if (Dt >= Distance)
41: 			{
42: 				Vertices.RemoveAt(0, i);
43: 				return;
44: 			}
45: 		}
46: 		Vertices.Empty();
47: 	}
48: }
49: 
50: static void RemovePointsFromEnd(TArray<FVector2D>& Vertices, double Distance)
51: {
52: 	if (Vertices.Num() && Distance > 0)
53: 	{
54: 		double Dt = 0;
55: 		for (int i = Vertices.Num() - 2; i >= 0; --i)
56: 		{
57: 			Dt += (Vertices[i] - Vertices[i + 1]).Length();
58: 			if (Dt >= Distance)
59: 			{
60: 				Vertices.RemoveAt(i + 1, Vertices.Num() - i - 1);
61: 				return;
62: 			}
63: 		}
64: 		Vertices.Empty();
65: 	}
66: }
67: 
68: /**
69:  * Index of the polyline point coincident with Pt, or INDEX_NONE.
70:  *
71:  * Tolerance is 2 x the polyline snap distance: NormalizePolyline may have dropped our point in favour of one up
72:  * to DefSnapDistance away in S. Still far below any real vertex spacing, so it cannot grab a different vertex.
73:  *
74:  * Used by every cross edge a lane inserts (today: the zone seams). Such an edge must SNAP onto a vertex that is
75:  * already in the arrangement rather than re-sample GetRoadPosition -- see the comment in InsertZoneBoundaryEdges
76:  * for what re-sampling costs.
77:  */
78: static int32 FindPointOnLine2D(const TArray<FVector2D>& Line, const FVector2D& Pt)
79: {
80: 	const double SnapDistSq = FMath::Square(2.0 * GeomUtils::DefSnapDistance);
81: 
82: 	int32 Best = INDEX_NONE;
83: 	double BestDistSq = SnapDistSq;
84: 	for (int32 i = 0; i < Line.Num(); ++i)
85: 	{
86: 		const double DistSq = FVector2D::DistSquared(Line[i], Pt);
87: 		if (DistSq <= BestDistSq)
88: 		{
89: 			BestDistSq = DistSq;
90: 			Best = i;
91: 		}

242: {
243: 	ResultInfo = { EGeometryResultType::InProgress };
244: 
245: 	// Before anything else: the zone spans decide where the boundary polylines need a vertex and where a
246: 	// constrained edge has to cross the lane. The centre line has no lane zone to override.
247: 	if (LaneIndex != MetaRoad::ZeroLaneIndex)
248: 	{
249: 		MetaRoad::BuildRoadLaneZoneSpans(GetLane(), GetStartOffset(), GetEndOffset(), ZoneSpans);
250: 	}

253: 	// which is byte-observable downstream (it numbers the vertices); the split into three is what makes that
254: 	// order readable instead of implied by 200 lines of position in a constructor.
255: 	const FLaneContour Contour = BuildLaneContour(MaxSquareDistanceFromCap);
256:

258: 	{
259: 		return; // InsertLaneContour has already named which line failed in ResultInfo
260: 	}
261: 
262: 	RunLaneAttributeHooks(Builder);
263: 
264: 	ResultInfo.SetSuccess();
