81: 	}
82: 
83: 	// The cut's profile breakpoints, INSERTED rather than handed to the sampler as segment boundaries: without a
84: 	// vertex at each one the ramp has no topology to bend over, but a boundary would also make the sampler
85: 	// subdivide every interval between them and double the ring's density for the same MeshDensity. The identical
86: 	// list goes into lane +-1's copy of this same reference line -- see GatherCentreLineRingOffsets.
87: 	{
88: 		TArray<TArray<double>> RingCuts;
89: 		GatherCentreLineRingOffsets(GetRoadSpline(), RingLength, RingCuts);
90: 		InsertCentreLineRingCuts(GetRoadSpline(), 0, 0.0, RingLength, RingCuts, RoadPoints);
91: 	}
92: 
93: 	TArray<FVector2D> Points2D;
94: 	Points2D.Reserve(RoadPoints.Num());
95: 	for (auto& It : RoadPoints)
96: 	{
97: 		Points2D.Add(FVector2D{ It.Location });
98: 	}
99: 	GeomUtils::RemovedPolylineSelfIntersection(Points2D);
100: 
101: 	// Which R direction points into the island — from the ONE shared helper, which decides it by probing the ring
102: 	// rather than by reasoning about winding. Shared deliberately: the viewport preview of a cut resolves the same
103: 	// question through the same call, and two independent derivations of one sign is how a cut ends up drawn into
104: 	// the island and carved out of it (or the reverse).
105: 	InwardSignR = MetaRoad::GetLoopInwardSignR(GetRoadSpline().GetOriginSpline());
106: 
107: 	int GID = SelectSurfaceGID(GetRoadZone());
108: 
109: 	// KNOWN EXCEPTION, named on the builder rather than collapsed: the loop below runs i < Num-1 with no wrap
110: 	// (i.e. it assumes the last point duplicates the first), while the winding computation twenty lines above
111: 	// runs (i + 1) % Num (i.e. it assumes the opposite). Both cannot be right, and it also feeds no sample
112: 	// digest -- so collapsing it into InsertPolyline would move the fingerprint's `samples` column on every road
113: 	// with a looped zone, i.e. break the instrument that would check the collapse. See the header.
114: 	LineInfo = Builder.InsertRingAsPolyline(Points2D, GID);
115: 
116: 	if (!LineInfo.IsValid())
117: 	{
118: 		ResultInfo.SetFailed(FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop_OutsideLineFailed", "FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop: {0}: polygon failed "), GetDescription()));
119: 		return;
120: 	}
121: 
122: 	InsertRingCutContours(Builder, Points2D);
---
24: #include "RoadMeshBuild/PolylineRecovery.h" // FindPolyline (the phase-1 PID-chain recovery)
25: 
26: #define LOCTEXT_NAMESPACE "FProceduralPolygon"
27: 
28: using namespace MetaRoad;
29: #include "RoadMeshBuild/Polygon/RoadPolygonCommon.h"
30: 
31: void FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop::ForEachRingCustomization(TFunctionRef<void(const FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue&, double AbsS)> Fn) const
32: {
33: 	const auto& Sections = GetRoadSpline().RoadLayout.Sections;
34: 	for (const FRoadLaneSection& Section : Sections)
35: 	{
36: 		for (const auto& [Desc, Attribute] : Section.Attributes)
37: 		{
38: 			if (!Attribute.IsChildOf<FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue>())
39: 			{
40: 				continue;
41: 			}
42: 			for (const auto& Key : Attribute.Keys)
43: 			{
44: 				if (auto* Value = Key.GetValuePtr<FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue>())
45: 				{
46: 					// Centre-line keys are SECTION-relative (unlike the ring, which spans the whole spline).
47: 					Fn(*Value, Section.SOffset + Key.SOffset);
48: 				}
49: 			}
50: 		}
51: 	}
52: }
53: 
54: FVector2D FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop::SampleRingInward(double S, double InwardDistance) const
55: {
56: 	const auto& Spline = GetRoadSpline();
57: 	// The ring is NOT at R = 0: GetRoadPosition(_, ZeroLaneIndex, _) yields EvalLaneROffset(0) == 0 PLUS the
58: 	// layout's ROffset curve. Anchor the inward offset on that same R or the contour drifts off the boundary
59: 	// wherever ROffset has keys.
60: 	const double RingR = Spline.EvalROffset(S);
61: 	return FVector2D(Spline.GetRoadPosition(S, RingR + InwardSignR * InwardDistance, ESplineCoordinateSpace::World).Location);
62: }
63: 
64: FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop::FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop(const FRoadTriangulationData& Owner, FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, int SplineIndex, double MaxSquareDistanceFromSpline, double MinSegmentLength)
65: 	:FProceduralPolygon_RoadBase(Owner, SplineIndex)
66: {
67: 	ResultInfo = { EGeometryResultType::InProgress };
68: 
69: 	auto RoadPositionFunc = [this](double SOffset, ESplineCoordinateSpace::Type CoordinateSpace)
70: 	{
71: 		return GetRoadSpline().GetRoadPosition(0, MetaRoad::ZeroLaneIndex, 0.0, SOffset, CoordinateSpace);
72: 	};
73: 
74: 	RingLength = GetRoadSpline().SplineCurves.GetSplineLength();
75: 
76: 	TArray<FRoadPosition> RoadPoints;
77: 	if (!GetRoadSpline().ConvertSplineToPolyline(RoadPositionFunc, ESplineCoordinateSpace::World, MaxSquareDistanceFromSpline, MinSegmentLength, 0.0, RingLength, {}, true, RoadPoints))
78: 	{
79: 		ResultInfo.SetFailed(FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop_ConvertSplineToPolyline", "FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop: {0}: Can't ConvertSplineToPolyline"), GetDescription()));
80: 		return;
81: 	}
82: 
83: 	// The cut's profile breakpoints, INSERTED rather than handed to the sampler as segment boundaries: without a
84: 	// vertex at each one the ramp has no topology to bend over, but a boundary would also make the sampler
85: 	// subdivide every interval between them and double the ring's density for the same MeshDensity. The identical
86: 	// list goes into lane +-1's copy of this same reference line -- see GatherCentreLineRingOffsets.
87: 	{
88: 		TArray<TArray<double>> RingCuts;
89: 		GatherCentreLineRingOffsets(GetRoadSpline(), RingLength, RingCuts);
90: 		InsertCentreLineRingCuts(GetRoadSpline(), 0, 0.0, RingLength, RingCuts, RoadPoints);
91: 	}
92: 
93: 	TArray<FVector2D> Points2D;
94: 	Points2D.Reserve(RoadPoints.Num());
95: 	for (auto& It : RoadPoints)
96: 	{
97: 		Points2D.Add(FVector2D{ It.Location });
98: 	}
99: 	GeomUtils::RemovedPolylineSelfIntersection(Points2D);
100: 
101: 	// Which R direction points into the island — from the ONE shared helper, which decides it by probing the ring
102: 	// rather than by reasoning about winding. Shared deliberately: the viewport preview of a cut resolves the same
103: 	// question through the same call, and two independent derivations of one sign is how a cut ends up drawn into
104: 	// the island and carved out of it (or the reverse).
105: 	InwardSignR = MetaRoad::GetLoopInwardSignR(GetRoadSpline().GetOriginSpline());
106: 
107: 	int GID = SelectSurfaceGID(GetRoadZone());
108: 
109: 	// KNOWN EXCEPTION, named on the builder rather than collapsed: the loop below runs i < Num-1 with no wrap
110: 	// (i.e. it assumes the last point duplicates the first), while the winding computation twenty lines above
111: 	// runs (i + 1) % Num (i.e. it assumes the opposite). Both cannot be right, and it also feeds no sample
112: 	// digest -- so collapsing it into InsertPolyline would move the fingerprint's `samples` column on every road
113: 	// with a looped zone, i.e. break the instrument that would check the collapse. See the header.
114: 	LineInfo = Builder.InsertRingAsPolyline(Points2D, GID);
115: 
116: 	if (!LineInfo.IsValid())
117: 	{
118: 		ResultInfo.SetFailed(FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop_OutsideLineFailed", "FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop: {0}: polygon failed "), GetDescription()));
119: 		return;
120: 	}
121: 
122: 	InsertRingCutContours(Builder, Points2D);
123: 	RegisterCurbCutSites(Builder);
124: 
---
103: 	// question through the same call, and two independent derivations of one sign is how a cut ends up drawn into
104: 	// the island and carved out of it (or the reverse).
105: 	InwardSignR = MetaRoad::GetLoopInwardSignR(GetRoadSpline().GetOriginSpline());
106: 
107: 	int GID = SelectSurfaceGID(GetRoadZone());
108: 
109: 	// KNOWN EXCEPTION, named on the builder rather than collapsed: the loop below runs i < Num-1 with no wrap
110: 	// (i.e. it assumes the last point duplicates the first), while the winding computation twenty lines above
111: 	// runs (i + 1) % Num (i.e. it assumes the opposite). Both cannot be right, and it also feeds no sample
112: 	// digest -- so collapsing it into InsertPolyline would move the fingerprint's `samples` column on every road
113: 	// with a looped zone, i.e. break the instrument that would check the collapse. See the header.
114: 	LineInfo = Builder.InsertRingAsPolyline(Points2D, GID);
115: 
116: 	if (!LineInfo.IsValid())
117: 	{
118: 		ResultInfo.SetFailed(FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop_OutsideLineFailed", "FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop: {0}: polygon failed "), GetDescription()));
119: 		return;
120: 	}
121: 
122: 	InsertRingCutContours(Builder, Points2D);
123: 	RegisterCurbCutSites(Builder);
124: 
125: 	ResultInfo.SetSuccess();

127: 
128: void FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop::RegisterCurbCutSites(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder) const
129: {
130: 	ForEachRingCustomization([&](const FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue& Value, double AbsS)
131: 	{
132: 		FRoadCurbCut Cut;
133: 		if (!Value.GetRingCurbCut(Cut) || Cut.Depth <= UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
134: 		{
135: 			return;
136: 		}
137: 
138: 		FRoadCurbCutSite Site;
139: 		Site.ArcOffset = AbsS;
140: 		Site.RingLength = RingLength;
141: 		Site.Cut = Cut;
142: 
143: 		// This ring IS the spline reference line, so the site's arc coordinate is simply spline S and the chain is
144: 		// the line sampled across the cut's window. SampleRingInward wraps S itself, so a cut sitting on the seam
145: 		// needs no special case here, while the arcs stay unwrapped and monotonic — which is what ProjectOnChain
146: 		// needs and what lets the cut measure along the shortest arc.
147: 		constexpr int32 NumSteps = 32;
148: 		const double Half = Cut.GetSpan() * 0.5;
149: 		Site.Chain.Reserve(NumSteps + 1);
150: 		Site.Arcs.Reserve(NumSteps + 1);
151: 		for (int32 i = 0; i <= NumSteps; ++i)
152: 		{
153: 			const double S = AbsS - Half + (2.0 * Half) * i / NumSteps;
154: 			Site.Chain.Add(SampleRingInward(S, 0.0));
155: 			Site.Arcs.Add(S);
156: 		}
157: 
158: 		Builder.AddCurbCutSite(MoveTemp(Site));
159: 	});
160: }
161: 
162: void FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop::InsertRingCutContours(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& RingPoints2D)
163: {
164: 	// The ring counterpart of the lane hook's OnPolygonCreated: carve the cut's inner chain + rungs so the
165: 	// ramp has triangles to bend over. Inserted HERE, in the ctor, because Vertices3d is sized from
166: 	// Graph.MaxVertexID() once every insert is done (RoadBaseOp) — a later insert would break that 1:1 contract.
167: 	const FPolygon2d RingPoly(RingPoints2D);
168: 
169: 	// A contour point outside the ring would end up owned by NO polygon and hard-fail the build at the height
170: 	// pass ("Mesh is broken"), which is exactly what happens once Depth exceeds the local inner curvature
171: 	// radius on a tight island. Drop such a contour instead of poisoning the whole mesh.
172: 	auto ToWorld = [this](const FRoadRingCutPoint& Pt) { return SampleRingInward(Pt.S, Pt.InwardDistance); };
173: 	auto IsInside = [&RingPoly](const FVector2D& P) { return RingPoly.Contains(FVector2d(P.X, P.Y)); };
174: 
175: 	ForEachRingCustomization([&](const FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue& Value, double AbsS)
176: 	{
177: 		FRoadRingCutContour Contour;
178: 		Value.BuildRingCutContour(AbsS, RingLength, Contour);
179: 		if (Contour.IsEmpty())
180: 		{
181: 			return;
182: 		}
183: 
184: 		TArray<FVector2D> Chain;
185: 		Chain.Reserve(Contour.InnerChain.Num());
186: 		for (const FRoadRingCutPoint& Pt : Contour.InnerChain)
187: 		{
188: 			const FVector2D P = ToWorld(Pt);
189: 			if (!IsInside(P))
190: 			{
191: 				return; // whole contour rejected: a partial chain leaves a dangling vertex outside the fill
192: 			}
193: 			Chain.Add(P);
194: 		}
195: 
196: 		// A rung's OUTER end lies on the ring, which is a SEAM — so it comes from splitting the edge already
197: 		// there, never from an independently sampled point. ToWorld evaluates the true spline curve at that S,
198: 		// while the inserted ring is a CHORD approximation of it, so the two do not coincide: inserting the
199: 		// sample gives the ring a vertex a few millimetres off its own polyline AND splits it where the rung
200: 		// crosses. The curb, which sweeps the ring's recovered chain, then carries cross-sections the surface
201: 		// boundary has no vertices for -- one strand denser than the edge it is supposed to follow.
202: 		constexpr double SnapRadius = 15.0;
203: 		TArray<TPair<FVector2D, FVector2D>> Rungs;
---
108: 
109: 	// KNOWN EXCEPTION, named on the builder rather than collapsed: the loop below runs i < Num-1 with no wrap
110: 	// (i.e. it assumes the last point duplicates the first), while the winding computation twenty lines above
111: 	// runs (i + 1) % Num (i.e. it assumes the opposite). Both cannot be right, and it also feeds no sample
112: 	// digest -- so collapsing it into InsertPolyline would move the fingerprint's `samples` column on every road
113: 	// with a looped zone, i.e. break the instrument that would check the collapse. See the header.
114: 	LineInfo = Builder.InsertRingAsPolyline(Points2D, GID);
115: 
116: 	if (!LineInfo.IsValid())
117: 	{
118: 		ResultInfo.SetFailed(FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop_OutsideLineFailed", "FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop: {0}: polygon failed "), GetDescription()));
119: 		return;
120: 	}
121: 
122: 	InsertRingCutContours(Builder, Points2D);
123: 	RegisterCurbCutSites(Builder);
124: 
125: 	ResultInfo.SetSuccess();
126: }
127:

129: {
130: 	ForEachRingCustomization([&](const FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue& Value, double AbsS)
131: 	{
132: 		FRoadCurbCut Cut;
133: 		if (!Value.GetRingCurbCut(Cut) || Cut.Depth <= UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
134: 		{
135: 			return;
136: 		}
137: 
138: 		FRoadCurbCutSite Site;
139: 		Site.ArcOffset = AbsS;
140: 		Site.RingLength = RingLength;
141: 		Site.Cut = Cut;
142: 
143: 		// This ring IS the spline reference line, so the site's arc coordinate is simply spline S and the chain is
144: 		// the line sampled across the cut's window. SampleRingInward wraps S itself, so a cut sitting on the seam
145: 		// needs no special case here, while the arcs stay unwrapped and monotonic — which is what ProjectOnChain

149: 		Site.Chain.Reserve(NumSteps + 1);
150: 		Site.Arcs.Reserve(NumSteps + 1);
151: 		for (int32 i = 0; i <= NumSteps; ++i)
152: 		{

162: void FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop::InsertRingCutContours(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& RingPoints2D)
163: {

165: 	// ramp has triangles to bend over. Inserted HERE, in the ctor, because Vertices3d is sized from
166: 	// Graph.MaxVertexID() once every insert is done (RoadBaseOp) — a later insert would break that 1:1 contract.

179: 		if (Contour.IsEmpty())

182: 		}
183: 
184: 		TArray<FVector2D> Chain;
185: 		Chain.Reserve(Contour.InnerChain.Num());

187: 		{

189: 			if (!IsInside(P))
190: 			{

194: 		}

205: 		for (int i = 0; i + 1 < Contour.Rungs.Num(); i += 2)

---
112: 	// digest -- so collapsing it into InsertPolyline would move the fingerprint's `samples` column on every road

115: 
116: 	if (!LineInfo.IsValid())
117: 	{
118: 		ResultInfo.SetFailed(FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop_OutsideLineFailed", "FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop: {0}: polygon failed "), GetDescription()));
119: 		return;
120: 	}
121: 
122: 	InsertRingCutContours(Builder, Points2D);
123: 	RegisterCurbCutSites(Builder);
124: 
125: 	ResultInfo.SetSuccess();
126: }
127: 
128: void FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop::RegisterCurbCutSites(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder) const
129: {
130: 	ForEachRingCustomization([&](const FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue& Value, double AbsS)
131: 	{
132: 		FRoadCurbCut Cut;
133: 		if (!Value.GetRingCurbCut(Cut) || Cut.Depth <= UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
134: 		{
135: 			return;
136: 		}
137: 
138: 		FRoadCurbCutSite Site;
139: 		Site.ArcOffset = AbsS;
140: 		Site.RingLength = RingLength;
141: 		Site.Cut = Cut;
142: 
143: 		// This ring IS the spline reference line, so the site's arc coordinate is simply spline S and the chain is
144: 		// the line sampled across the cut's window. SampleRingInward wraps S itself, so a cut sitting on the seam
145: 		// needs no special case here, while the arcs stay unwrapped and monotonic — which is what ProjectOnChain
146: 		// needs and what lets the cut measure along the shortest arc.
147: 		constexpr int32 NumSteps = 32;
148: 		const double Half = Cut.GetSpan() * 0.5;
149: 		Site.Chain.Reserve(NumSteps + 1);
150: 		Site.Arcs.Reserve(NumSteps + 1);
151: 		for (int32 i = 0; i <= NumSteps; ++i)
152: 		{
153: 			const double S = AbsS - Half + (2.0 * Half) * i / NumSteps;
154: 			Site.Chain.Add(SampleRingInward(S, 0.0));
155: 			Site.Arcs.Add(S);
156: 		}
157: 
158: 		Builder.AddCurbCutSite(MoveTemp(Site));
159: 	});
160: }
161: 
162: void FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop::InsertRingCutContours(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& RingPoints2D)
163: {
164: 	// The ring counterpart of the lane hook's OnPolygonCreated: carve the cut's inner chain + rungs so the
165: 	// ramp has triangles to bend over. Inserted HERE, in the ctor, because Vertices3d is sized from
166: 	// Graph.MaxVertexID() once every insert is done (RoadBaseOp) — a later insert would break that 1:1 contract.
167: 	const FPolygon2d RingPoly(RingPoints2D);
168: 
169: 	// A contour point outside the ring would end up owned by NO polygon and hard-fail the build at the height
170: 	// pass ("Mesh is broken"), which is exactly what happens once Depth exceeds the local inner curvature
171: 	// radius on a tight island. Drop such a contour instead of poisoning the whole mesh.
172: 	auto ToWorld = [this](const FRoadRingCutPoint& Pt) { return SampleRingInward(Pt.S, Pt.InwardDistance); };
173: 	auto IsInside = [&RingPoly](const FVector2D& P) { return RingPoly.Contains(FVector2d(P.X, P.Y)); };
174: 
175: 	ForEachRingCustomization([&](const FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue& Value, double AbsS)
176: 	{
177: 		FRoadRingCutContour Contour;
178: 		Value.BuildRingCutContour(AbsS, RingLength, Contour);
179: 		if (Contour.IsEmpty())
180: 		{
181: 			return;
182: 		}
183: 
184: 		TArray<FVector2D> Chain;
185: 		Chain.Reserve(Contour.InnerChain.Num());
186: 		for (const FRoadRingCutPoint& Pt : Contour.InnerChain)
187: 		{
188: 			const FVector2D P = ToWorld(Pt);
189: 			if (!IsInside(P))
190: 			{
191: 				return; // whole contour rejected: a partial chain leaves a dangling vertex outside the fill
192: 			}
193: 			Chain.Add(P);
194: 		}
195: 
196: 		// A rung's OUTER end lies on the ring, which is a SEAM — so it comes from splitting the edge already
197: 		// there, never from an independently sampled point. ToWorld evaluates the true spline curve at that S,
198: 		// while the inserted ring is a CHORD approximation of it, so the two do not coincide: inserting the
199: 		// sample gives the ring a vertex a few millimetres off its own polyline AND splits it where the rung
200: 		// crosses. The curb, which sweeps the ring's recovered chain, then carries cross-sections the surface
201: 		// boundary has no vertices for -- one strand denser than the edge it is supposed to follow.
202: 		constexpr double SnapRadius = 15.0;
203: 		TArray<TPair<FVector2D, FVector2D>> Rungs;
204: 		Rungs.Reserve(Contour.Rungs.Num() / 2);
205: 		for (int i = 0; i + 1 < Contour.Rungs.Num(); i += 2)
206: 		{
207: 			FVector2D Outer;
208: 			if (!Builder.SplitConstraintNear(ToWorld(Contour.Rungs[i]), SnapRadius, Outer))
209: 			{
210: 				continue;
211: 			}
212: 			const FVector2D Inner = ToWorld(Contour.Rungs[i + 1]);
---
113: 	// with a looped zone, i.e. break the instrument that would check the collapse. See the header.
114: 	LineInfo = Builder.InsertRingAsPolyline(Points2D, GID);
115: 
116: 	if (!LineInfo.IsValid())
117: 	{
118: 		ResultInfo.SetFailed(FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop_OutsideLineFailed", "FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop: {0}: polygon failed "), GetDescription()));
119: 		return;
120: 	}
121: 
122: 	InsertRingCutContours(Builder, Points2D);
123: 	RegisterCurbCutSites(Builder);
124: 
125: 	ResultInfo.SetSuccess();
126: }
127:

129: {
130: 	ForEachRingCustomization([&](const FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue& Value, double AbsS)
131: 	{
132: 		FRoadCurbCut Cut;
133: 		if (!Value.GetRingCurbCut(Cut) || Cut.Depth <= UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER)
134: 		{
135: 			return;
136: 		}
137: 
138: 		FRoadCurbCutSite Site;
139: 		Site.ArcOffset = AbsS;
140: 		Site.RingLength = RingLength;
141: 		Site.Cut = Cut;
142: 
143: 		// This ring IS the spline reference line, so the site's arc coordinate is simply spline S and the chain is
144: 		// the line sampled across the cut's window. SampleRingInward wraps S itself, so a cut sitting on the seam
145: 		// needs no special case here, while the arcs stay unwrapped and monotonic — which is what ProjectOnChain
146: 		// needs and what lets the cut measure along the shortest arc.

152: 		{

154: 			Site.Chain.Add(SampleRingInward(S, 0.0));
155: 			Site.Arcs.Add(S);
156: 		}
157:

159: 	});

162: void FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop::InsertRingCutContours(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& RingPoints2D)
163: {
164: 	// The ring counterpart of the lane hook's OnPolygonCreated: carve the cut's inner chain + rungs so the
165: 	// ramp has triangles to bend over. Inserted HERE, in the ctor, because Vertices3d is sized from
166: 	// Graph.MaxVertexID() once every insert is done (RoadBaseOp) — a later insert would break that 1:1 contract.
167: 	const FPolygon2d RingPoly(RingPoints2D);
168: 
169: 	// A contour point outside the ring would end up owned by NO polygon and hard-fail the build at the height
170: 	// pass ("Mesh is broken"), which is exactly what happens once Depth exceeds the local inner curvature
171: 	// radius on a tight island. Drop such a contour instead of poisoning the whole mesh.
172: 	auto ToWorld = [this](const FRoadRingCutPoint& Pt) { return SampleRingInward(Pt.S, Pt.InwardDistance); };
173: 	auto IsInside = [&RingPoly](const FVector2D& P) { return RingPoly.Contains(FVector2d(P.X, P.Y)); };
174: 
175: 	ForEachRingCustomization([&](const FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue& Value, double AbsS)
176: 	{
177: 		FRoadRingCutContour Contour;
178: 		Value.BuildRingCutContour(AbsS, RingLength, Contour);
179: 		if (Contour.IsEmpty())
180: 		{
181: 			return;
182: 		}
183: 
184: 		TArray<FVector2D> Chain;
185: 		Chain.Reserve(Contour.InnerChain.Num());
186: 		for (const FRoadRingCutPoint& Pt : Contour.InnerChain)
187: 		{
188: 			const FVector2D P = ToWorld(Pt);
189: 			if (!IsInside(P))
190: 			{
191: 				return; // whole contour rejected: a partial chain leaves a dangling vertex outside the fill
192: 			}
193: 			Chain.Add(P);
194: 		}
195: 
196: 		// A rung's OUTER end lies on the ring, which is a SEAM — so it comes from splitting the edge already
197: 		// there, never from an independently sampled point. ToWorld evaluates the true spline curve at that S,
198: 		// while the inserted ring is a CHORD approximation of it, so the two do not coincide: inserting the
199: 		// sample gives the ring a vertex a few millimetres off its own polyline AND splits it where the rung
200: 		// crosses. The curb, which sweeps the ring's recovered chain, then carries cross-sections the surface
201: 		// boundary has no vertices for -- one strand denser than the edge it is supposed to follow.
202: 		constexpr double SnapRadius = 15.0;
203: 		TArray<TPair<FVector2D, FVector2D>> Rungs;
204: 		Rungs.Reserve(Contour.Rungs.Num() / 2);
205: 		for (int i = 0; i + 1 < Contour.Rungs.Num(); i += 2)
206: 		{
207: 			FVector2D Outer;
208: 			if (!Builder.SplitConstraintNear(ToWorld(Contour.Rungs[i]), SnapRadius, Outer))
209: 			{
210: 				continue;
211: 			}
212: 			const FVector2D Inner = ToWorld(Contour.Rungs[i + 1]);
213: 			if (!IsInside(Inner))
214: 			{
215: 				return;
216: 			}
217: 			Rungs.Emplace(Outer, Inner);
218: 		}
219: 
220: 		// PID = -1 is load-bearing, not laziness: FindPolyline recovers the ring by Dijkstra over PID-tagged
221: 		// edges, so tagging the contour with the ring's PID would let the recovered boundary short-cut straight
222: 		// across the cut window.
223: 		for (int i = 0; i + 1 < Chain.Num(); ++i)
224: 		{
225: 			Builder.AddConstraintEdge(Chain[i], Chain[i + 1]);
226: 		}
227: 		for (const auto& [Outer, Inner] : Rungs)
228: 		{
229: 			Builder.AddConstraintEdge(Outer, Inner);
230: 		}
231: 	});
232: }

159: 	});

227: 		for (const auto& [Outer, Inner] : Rungs)
228: 		{
229: 			Builder.AddConstraintEdge(Outer, Inner);
230: 		}
231: 	});
232: }
233: 
234: bool FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop::OnCompleteArrangement(FRoadCompletionSink& Sink)
235: {
236: 	if (ResultInfo.HasFailed())
237: 	{
238: 		return false;
239: 	}
240: 
241: 	if (!FindPolyline(Owner.GetGraph(), LineInfo, LineVertices))
242: 	{
243: 		ResultInfo.SetFailed(FText::Format(LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop_InsideLineNotFound", "FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop: {0}: line not found after arrangement"), GetDescription()));
244: 		return false;
245: 	}
246: 
247: 	BuildClosedRingBoundary(Sink.Vertices);
248: 
249: 	return true;
250: }
251: 
252: void FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop::FillVertexInfo(FRoadVertexInfo& VertexInfo, int VID, FVector2d V2d) const
253: {
254: 	VertexInfo.Pos = Owner.Inputs.Splines[GetSplineIndex()]->UpRayIntersection(V2d);
255: 
---
248: 
249: 	return true;
250: }
251: 
252: void FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop::FillVertexInfo(FRoadVertexInfo& VertexInfo, int VID, FVector2d V2d) const
253: {
254: 	VertexInfo.Pos = Owner.Inputs.Splines[GetSplineIndex()]->UpRayIntersection(V2d);
255: 
256: 	const FRoadZoneSidewalk* LaneSidewalk = GetRoadZone().GetPtr<FRoadZoneSidewalk>();
257: 	if (!LaneSidewalk)
258: 	{
259: 		return;
260: 	}
261: 
262: 	VertexInfo.HeightOffset = LaneSidewalk->DefaultHeight;
263: 
264: 	const auto& Spline = GetRoadSpline();
265: 	const double S = VertexInfo.Pos.SOffset;
266: 
267: 	// ---- Sidewalk height: the centre-line key of the section this vertex falls in ----------------------------
268: 	// Keys are section-relative, so each section evaluates its own attribute over its own S window; the ring, by
269: 	// contrast, spans the whole spline.
270: 	{
271: 		const FRoadLaneAttributeSidewalkHeightValue DefHeightValue(VertexInfo.HeightOffset);
272: 		const auto& Sections = Spline.RoadLayout.Sections;
273: 		for (int i = 0; i < Sections.Num(); ++i)
274: 		{
275: 			const FRoadLaneSection& Section = Sections[i];
276: 			const bool bIsLast = (i == Sections.Num() - 1);
277: 			if (S < Section.SOffset || (!bIsLast && S >= Section.SOffsetEnd_Cached))
278: 			{
279: 				continue;
280: 			}
281: 			for (const auto& [Desc, Attribute] : Section.Attributes)
282: 			{
283: 				if (Attribute.IsChildOf<FRoadLaneAttributeSidewalkHeightValue>())
284: 				{
285: 					VertexInfo.HeightOffset = Attribute.Evaluate(S - Section.SOffset, DefHeightValue).Height;
286: 				}
287: 			}
288: 			break;
289: 		}
290: 	}
291: 
292: 	// Curb cuts are NOT applied here — they are registered as scope-wide sites (RegisterCurbCutSites) and applied
293: 	// by BuildStages::ApplyCurbCuts to every vertex in reach. Doing it from this polygon reached only the vertices
294: 	// it owns, which is where the ramp used to stop dead at the seam with an abutting sidewalk lane.
295: }
296: 
297: const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>& FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop::GetRoadZone() const
298: {
299: 	return GetRoadSpline().RoadLayout.LoopedRoadZone;
300: }
301: 
302: FText FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop::GetDescription() const
303: {
304: 	return LOCTEXT("FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop_GetDescription", "LoopPoly");
305: }
306:

308: bool FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop::SetUVLayers(FDynamicMesh3& Mesh, int TID) const
309: {
310: 	return SetPlanarDensityUVLayers(Mesh, TID, GetRoadSpline().RoadLayout.LoopedTexture);
311: }

155: 	{
156: 		return false;
157: 	}
158: 
159: 	// Landing on a vertex that already exists: take it. Splitting there would write a coincident pair, and
160: 	// FDelaunay2 drops one of them -- taking every constrained edge that referenced it out of the triangulation.
161: 	const double MergeTol = FMath::Max(Arrangement.VertexSnapTol * 10.0, 1.0);
162: 	const int32 Existing = Arrangement.FindNearestVertex(BestPoint, MergeTol);
163: 	if (Existing >= 0)
164: 	{
165: 		const FVector2d V = Graph.GetVertex(Existing);
166: 		OutPoint = FVector2D(V.X, V.Y);
167: 		return true;
168: 	}
169: 
170: 	Arrangement.SplitEdgeAtPoint(BestEID, BestPoint);
171: 	OutPoint = FVector2D(BestPoint.X, BestPoint.Y);
172: 	return true;
173: }
174: 
175: void FRoadArrangementBuilder::AddCurbCutSite(FRoadCurbCutSite&& Site)
176: {
177: 	check(!bClosed);
178: 	if (!Site.IsValid())
179: 	{
180: 		return;
181: 	}
182: 
183: 	// The reject box the consumer tests against: the reachable stretch of ring, widened by how far the ramp
184: 	// travels away from it. Built here so ApplyCurbCuts never has to look at the chain to know it can skip.
185: 	Site.Influence = FBox2D(ForceInit);
186: 	for (const FVector2D& P : Site.Chain)
187: 	{
188: 		Site.Influence += P;
189: 	}
190: 	Site.Influence = Site.Influence.ExpandBy(Site.Cut.Depth);
191: 
192: 	Data.CurbCutSites.Add(MoveTemp(Site));
193: }
194: 
195: int32 FRoadArrangementBuilder::ConditionBoundaries(double MaxEdgeLength, int32 MaxNewVertices)
196: {
197: 	check(!bClosed);
198: 	return BoundaryConditioner::Run(*Data.Arrangement, MaxEdgeLength, MaxNewVertices);
199: }
200: 
201: void FRoadArrangementBuilder::Close()
202: {
203: 	if (bClosed)
204: 	{
205: 		return;
206: 	}
207: 	bClosed = true;
208: 
209: 	// THE HUG CONTRACT. Vertices3d is a PARALLEL 3D array keyed 1:1 by arrangement vertex-ID (VID): index i is the
210: 	// 3D lift of graph vertex i. Every downstream reader relies on this identity -- MakeDelaunay2's
211: 	// check(MaxVertexID == VertexCount), every Info/VID lookup, FSpatialQuery::Build, and every Pattern-A op's
212: 	// check(NewVID == VID). Sizing it is what ENDS the open phase, which is why it lives here and not in a stage.
213: 	Data.Vertices3d.SyncToGraph(Data.Arrangement->Graph);
214: }

218: FRoadCompletionSink::FRoadCompletionSink(FRoadTriangulationData& InData)
219: 	: Vertices(InData.Vertices3d)
220: 	, FeatureLinePaths(InData.FeatureLineWeldedPaths)
221: 	, Data(InData)
222: {
223: }
224: 
225: bool FRoadCompletionSink::TagMatchedPath(TArrayView<const FVector2d> Approx, const ArrangementMatch::FParams& Params,
226:                                          TArray<int>& InOutPath, int32 PID)
227: {
228: 	FArrangement2d& Arrangement = *Data.Arrangement;
229: 
230: 	// The postcondition is the whole reason this is a named operation rather than a handle. Vertices3d was sized
231: 	// by Close() one phase ago and every boundary chain in this loop was recovered against THIS graph; a vertex or
232: 	// an edge appearing here would invalidate both, and would do it silently.
233: 	const int32 VertsBefore = Arrangement.Graph.MaxVertexID();
234: 	const int32 EdgesBefore = Arrangement.Graph.EdgeCount();
235: 
236: 	const bool bTagged = ArrangementMatch::RevalidateAndTagPath(Arrangement, Approx, Params, InOutPath, PID);
237: 
238: 	checkf(Arrangement.Graph.MaxVertexID() == VertsBefore && Arrangement.Graph.EdgeCount() == EdgesBefore,
239: 		TEXT("TagMatchedPath changed the graph (%d verts / %d edges -> %d / %d). Tagging must only stamp edges "
240: 		     "that already exist -- Vertices3d was sized by Close() and every recovered VID chain predates this."),
241: 		VertsBefore, EdgesBefore, Arrangement.Graph.MaxVertexID(), Arrangement.Graph.EdgeCount());
242: 
243: 	return bTagged;
244: }
---
164: 	{
165: 		const FVector2d V = Graph.GetVertex(Existing);
166: 		OutPoint = FVector2D(V.X, V.Y);
167: 		return true;
168: 	}
169: 
170: 	Arrangement.SplitEdgeAtPoint(BestEID, BestPoint);
171: 	OutPoint = FVector2D(BestPoint.X, BestPoint.Y);
172: 	return true;
173: }

208: 
209: 	// THE HUG CONTRACT. Vertices3d is a PARALLEL 3D array keyed 1:1 by arrangement vertex-ID (VID): index i is the
210: 	// 3D lift of graph vertex i. Every downstream reader relies on this identity -- MakeDelaunay2's
211: 	// check(MaxVertexID == VertexCount), every Info/VID lookup, FSpatialQuery::Build, and every Pattern-A op's
212: 	// check(NewVID == VID). Sizing it is what ENDS the open phase, which is why it lives here and not in a stage.
213: 	Data.Vertices3d.SyncToGraph(Data.Arrangement->Graph);

218: FRoadCompletionSink::FRoadCompletionSink(FRoadTriangulationData& InData)
219: 	: Vertices(InData.Vertices3d)
220: 	, FeatureLinePaths(InData.FeatureLineWeldedPaths)
221: 	, Data(InData)

224: 
225: bool FRoadCompletionSink::TagMatchedPath(TArrayView<const FVector2d> Approx, const ArrangementMatch::FParams& Params,
226:                                          TArray<int>& InOutPath, int32 PID)
227: {
228: 	FArrangement2d& Arrangement = *Data.Arrangement;
229: 
230: 	// The postcondition is the whole reason this is a named operation rather than a handle. Vertices3d was sized
231: 	// by Close() one phase ago and every boundary chain in this loop was recovered against THIS graph; a vertex or
232: 	// an edge appearing here would invalidate both, and would do it silently.
233: 	const int32 VertsBefore = Arrangement.Graph.MaxVertexID();
234: 	const int32 EdgesBefore = Arrangement.Graph.EdgeCount();
235: 
236: 	const bool bTagged = ArrangementMatch::RevalidateAndTagPath(Arrangement, Approx, Params, InOutPath, PID);
237: 
238: 	checkf(Arrangement.Graph.MaxVertexID() == VertsBefore && Arrangement.Graph.EdgeCount() == EdgesBefore,
239: 		TEXT("TagMatchedPath changed the graph (%d verts / %d edges -> %d / %d). Tagging must only stamp edges "
240: 		     "that already exist -- Vertices3d was sized by Close() and every recovered VID chain predates this."),
241: 		VertsBefore, EdgesBefore, Arrangement.Graph.MaxVertexID(), Arrangement.Graph.EdgeCount());
242: 
243: 	return bTagged;
244: }
---
172: 	return true;
173: }
174: 
175: void FRoadArrangementBuilder::AddCurbCutSite(FRoadCurbCutSite&& Site)
176: {
177: 	check(!bClosed);
178: 	if (!Site.IsValid())
179: 	{
180: 		return;
181: 	}
182: 
183: 	// The reject box the consumer tests against: the reachable stretch of ring, widened by how far the ramp
184: 	// travels away from it. Built here so ApplyCurbCuts never has to look at the chain to know it can skip.
185: 	Site.Influence = FBox2D(ForceInit);
186: 	for (const FVector2D& P : Site.Chain)
187: 	{
188: 		Site.Influence += P;
189: 	}
190: 	Site.Influence = Site.Influence.ExpandBy(Site.Cut.Depth);
191: 
192: 	Data.CurbCutSites.Add(MoveTemp(Site));
193: }
194: 
195: int32 FRoadArrangementBuilder::ConditionBoundaries(double MaxEdgeLength, int32 MaxNewVertices)
196: {
197: 	check(!bClosed);
198: 	return BoundaryConditioner::Run(*Data.Arrangement, MaxEdgeLength, MaxNewVertices);
199: }
200: 
201: void FRoadArrangementBuilder::Close()
202: {
203: 	if (bClosed)
204: 	{
205: 		return;
206: 	}
207: 	bClosed = true;
208: 
209: 	// THE HUG CONTRACT. Vertices3d is a PARALLEL 3D array keyed 1:1 by arrangement vertex-ID (VID): index i is the
210: 	// 3D lift of graph vertex i. Every downstream reader relies on this identity -- MakeDelaunay2's
211: 	// check(MaxVertexID == VertexCount), every Info/VID lookup, FSpatialQuery::Build, and every Pattern-A op's
212: 	// check(NewVID == VID). Sizing it is what ENDS the open phase, which is why it lives here and not in a stage.
213: 	Data.Vertices3d.SyncToGraph(Data.Arrangement->Graph);
214: }
215: 
216: // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
217: 
218: FRoadCompletionSink::FRoadCompletionSink(FRoadTriangulationData& InData)
219: 	: Vertices(InData.Vertices3d)
220: 	, FeatureLinePaths(InData.FeatureLineWeldedPaths)
221: 	, Data(InData)
222: {
223: }
224: 
225: bool FRoadCompletionSink::TagMatchedPath(TArrayView<const FVector2d> Approx, const ArrangementMatch::FParams& Params,
226:                                          TArray<int>& InOutPath, int32 PID)
227: {
228: 	FArrangement2d& Arrangement = *Data.Arrangement;
229: 
230: 	// The postcondition is the whole reason this is a named operation rather than a handle. Vertices3d was sized
231: 	// by Close() one phase ago and every boundary chain in this loop was recovered against THIS graph; a vertex or
232: 	// an edge appearing here would invalidate both, and would do it silently.
233: 	const int32 VertsBefore = Arrangement.Graph.MaxVertexID();
234: 	const int32 EdgesBefore = Arrangement.Graph.EdgeCount();
235: 
236: 	const bool bTagged = ArrangementMatch::RevalidateAndTagPath(Arrangement, Approx, Params, InOutPath, PID);
237: 
238: 	checkf(Arrangement.Graph.MaxVertexID() == VertsBefore && Arrangement.Graph.EdgeCount() == EdgesBefore,
239: 		TEXT("TagMatchedPath changed the graph (%d verts / %d edges -> %d / %d). Tagging must only stamp edges "
240: 		     "that already exist -- Vertices3d was sized by Close() and every recovered VID chain predates this."),
241: 		VertsBefore, EdgesBefore, Arrangement.Graph.MaxVertexID(), Arrangement.Graph.EdgeCount());
242: 
243: 	return bTagged;
244: }

=== Source\MetaRoadEditor\Public\Assets\RoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomization.h ===
1: /*
2:  * Copyright (c) 2026 Ivan Zhukov. All Rights Reserved.
3:  * Email: ivzhuk7@gmail.com
4:  */
5: 
6: #pragma once
7: 
8: #include "Assets/RoadLaneAttributeDescriptor.h"
9: #include "RoadMeshBuild/RoadConstraintSink.h" // the ONLY way a hook may write to the arrangement
10: #include "RoadCurbCut.h"                      // FRoadCurbCut (GetRingCurbCut -- the scope-wide site payload)
11: #include "RoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomization.generated.h"
12: 
13: class URoadSplineComponent;
14: 
15: namespace MetaRoad
16: {
17: 	struct FProceduralPolygon_RoadLane;
18: 
19: 	/**
20: 	 * True when this spline's CENTRE LINE is the boundary of a sidewalk ring â€” a closed spline whose
21: 	 * FRoadLayout::LoopedRoadZone is a FRoadZoneSidewalk. That ring (FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop) is the
22: 	 * closed-ring host for sidewalk-height / curb-cut keys authored on FRoadLaneSection::Attributes, so it is
23: 	 * the single condition under which those descriptors accept MetaRoad::ZeroLaneIndex.
24: 	 */
25: 	METAROADEDITOR_API bool IsSidewalkLoopHost(const URoadSplineComponent* Spline);
26: 
27: 	/**
28: 	 * Which R direction points INTO a closed spline's island: -1 when the ring runs counter-clockwise in XY
29: 	 * (interior to the LEFT of travel, and +R is to the right), +1 when clockwise. Same answer as the sign
30: 	 * FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop caches from its sampled ring, but from a coarse sampling so the editor can
31: 	 * draw the cut without waiting for a build.
32: 	 */
33: 	METAROADEDITOR_API double GetLoopInwardSignR(const URoadSplineComponent* Spline);
34: }
35: 
36: /** One point of a ring-cut contour, in the ring's own parametric frame. */
37: struct FRoadRingCutPoint
38: {
39: 	/** Position along the ring [cm] â€” spline S for the looped road zone, arc length for a plain ring. */
40: 	double S = 0.0;
41: 	/** Distance toward the ring's INTERIOR [cm]; 0 = on the ring. */
42: 	double InwardDistance = 0.0;
43: };
44: 
45: /**
46:  * The extra geometry a closed-ring host must carve so a curb cut has topology: the inner chain at the cut's
47:  * full depth plus the rungs joining it back to the ring â€” the ring counterpart of what
48:  * FRoadLaneAttributeCurbCutValue::OnPolygonCreated inserts across a lane.
49:  *
50:  * Deliberately PARAMETRIC (S + inward distance) rather than world-space: only the host knows how to turn that
51:  * into XY (the looped road zone reads its spline frame; a plain ring offsets its polyline), and only the host
52:  * can tell whether a point still lies inside its own boundary. A contour point that escapes the ring ends up
53:  * with no polygon Info at all and hard-fails the build, so REJECTING such points is the host's job.
54:  */
55: struct FRoadRingCutContour
56: {
57: 	/** Densified chain at the cut's inward extent. */
58: 	TArray<FRoadRingCutPoint> InnerChain;
59: 
60: 	/** Rungs ring -> inner chain, as consecutive PAIRS (outer, inner). */
61: 	TArray<FRoadRingCutPoint> Rungs;
62: 
63: 	bool IsEmpty() const { return InnerChain.Num() < 2 && Rungs.Num() < 2; }
64: };
65: 
66: /**
67:  * FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue
68:  *
69:  * Extension point for attribute values that reshape the TRIANGULATION rather than adding their own mesh:
70:  * they add sampling positions, insert constraint edges, and modulate the sidewalk lift.
71:  *
72:  * Two host families, one per geometry model:
73:  *   - LANE (FProceduralPolygon_RoadLane) â€” the original: parameterized by (SOffset, lane-relative Alpha).
74:  *     The lift is modulated IN PLACE through GetHeightOffset.
75:  *   - RING â€” a closed fill boundary: the looped road zone (FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop) and sidewalk-spline
76:  *     fills (FProceduralPolygon_Simple). Parameterized by (S along the ring, distance inward). The lift is
77:  *     modulated through the per-vertex FArrangementVertex3d::HeightScale instead, because the surface takes
78:  *     the MAX HeightOffset over polygons and a ring routinely shares its vertices with another sidewalk.
79:  */
80: USTRUCT(BlueprintType, Blueprintable)
81: struct METAROADEDITOR_API FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue : public FRoadLaneAttributeValue
82: {
83: 	GENERATED_USTRUCT_BODY()
84: 
85: 
86: 	virtual ~FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue() {}
87: 
88: 	//~ Lane host
---

76:  *     fills (FProceduralPolygon_Simple). Parameterized by (S along the ring, distance inward). The lift is
77:  *     modulated through the per-vertex FArrangementVertex3d::HeightScale instead, because the surface takes
78:  *     the MAX HeightOffset over polygons and a ring routinely shares its vertices with another sidewalk.
79:  */
80: USTRUCT(BlueprintType, Blueprintable)
81: struct METAROADEDITOR_API FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue : public FRoadLaneAttributeValue
82: {
83: 	GENERATED_USTRUCT_BODY()
84: 
85: 
86: 	virtual ~FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue() {}
87: 
88: 	//~ Lane host
89: 
90: 	/**
91: 	 * Called once per attribute key, on a COMPLETE polygon, at the end of its construction.
92: 	 *
93: 	 * The polygon is const and the only way to write is Sink: the implementation used to reach
94: 	 * Polygon.Owner.Arrangement and insert directly, which is what kept FProceduralPolygon::Owner a public
95: 	 * non-const reference to the entire build.
96: 	 */
97: 	virtual void OnPolygonCreated(const MetaRoad::FProceduralPolygon_RoadLane& Polygon,
98: 	                              MetaRoad::IRoadConstraintSink& Sink, double SKeyOffset) const {}
99: 
100: 	virtual void GetAdditionalSplinePoints(const MetaRoad::FProceduralPolygon_RoadLane& Polygon, double SKeyOffset, double LaneAlpha, TArray<double>& OutSegments) const {}
101: 
102: 	virtual double GetHeightOffset(const MetaRoad::FProceduralPolygon_RoadLane& Polygon, double SKeyOffset, double SOffset, double LaneAlpha, double InHeightOffset) const { return InHeightOffset; }
---
28: 	 * Which R direction points INTO a closed spline's island: -1 when the ring runs counter-clockwise in XY
29: 	 * (interior to the LEFT of travel, and +R is to the right), +1 when clockwise. Same answer as the sign
30: 	 * FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop caches from its sampled ring, but from a coarse sampling so the editor can
31: 	 * draw the cut without waiting for a build.
32: 	 */
33: 	METAROADEDITOR_API double GetLoopInwardSignR(const URoadSplineComponent* Spline);
34: }
35: 
36: /** One point of a ring-cut contour, in the ring's own parametric frame. */
37: struct FRoadRingCutPoint
38: {
39: 	/** Position along the ring [cm] â€” spline S for the looped road zone, arc length for a plain ring. */
40: 	double S = 0.0;
41: 	/** Distance toward the ring's INTERIOR [cm]; 0 = on the ring. */
42: 	double InwardDistance = 0.0;
43: };
44: 
45: /**
46:  * The extra geometry a closed-ring host must carve so a curb cut has topology: the inner chain at the cut's
47:  * full depth plus the rungs joining it back to the ring â€” the ring counterpart of what
48:  * FRoadLaneAttributeCurbCutValue::OnPolygonCreated inserts across a lane.
49:  *
50:  * Deliberately PARAMETRIC (S + inward distance) rather than world-space: only the host knows how to turn that
51:  * into XY (the looped road zone reads its spline frame; a plain ring offsets its polyline), and only the host
52:  * can tell whether a point still lies inside its own boundary. A contour point that escapes the ring ends up
53:  * with no polygon Info at all and hard-fails the build, so REJECTING such points is the host's job.
54:  */
55: struct FRoadRingCutContour
56: {
57: 	/** Densified chain at the cut's inward extent. */
58: 	TArray<FRoadRingCutPoint> InnerChain;
59: 
60: 	/** Rungs ring -> inner chain, as consecutive PAIRS (outer, inner). */
61: 	TArray<FRoadRingCutPoint> Rungs;
62: 
63: 	bool IsEmpty() const { return InnerChain.Num() < 2 && Rungs.Num() < 2; }
64: };
65: 
66: /**
67:  * FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue
68:  *
69:  * Extension point for attribute values that reshape the TRIANGULATION rather than adding their own mesh:
70:  * they add sampling positions, insert constraint edges, and modulate the sidewalk lift.
71:  *
72:  * Two host families, one per geometry model:
73:  *   - LANE (FProceduralPolygon_RoadLane) â€” the original: parameterized by (SOffset, lane-relative Alpha).
74:  *     The lift is modulated IN PLACE through GetHeightOffset.
75:  *   - RING â€” a closed fill boundary: the looped road zone (FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop) and sidewalk-spline
76:  *     fills (FProceduralPolygon_Simple). Parameterized by (S along the ring, distance inward). The lift is
77:  *     modulated through the per-vertex FArrangementVertex3d::HeightScale instead, because the surface takes
78:  *     the MAX HeightOffset over polygons and a ring routinely shares its vertices with another sidewalk.
79:  */
80: USTRUCT(BlueprintType, Blueprintable)
81: struct METAROADEDITOR_API FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue : public FRoadLaneAttributeValue
82: {
83: 	GENERATED_USTRUCT_BODY()
84: 
85: 
86: 	virtual ~FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue() {}
87: 
88: 	//~ Lane host
89: 
90: 	/**
91: 	 * Called once per attribute key, on a COMPLETE polygon, at the end of its construction.
92: 	 *
93: 	 * The polygon is const and the only way to write is Sink: the implementation used to reach
94: 	 * Polygon.Owner.Arrangement and insert directly, which is what kept FProceduralPolygon::Owner a public
95: 	 * non-const reference to the entire build.
96: 	 */
97: 	virtual void OnPolygonCreated(const MetaRoad::FProceduralPolygon_RoadLane& Polygon,
98: 	                              MetaRoad::IRoadConstraintSink& Sink, double SKeyOffset) const {}
99: 
100: 	virtual void GetAdditionalSplinePoints(const MetaRoad::FProceduralPolygon_RoadLane& Polygon, double SKeyOffset, double LaneAlpha, TArray<double>& OutSegments) const {}
101: 
102: 	virtual double GetHeightOffset(const MetaRoad::FProceduralPolygon_RoadLane& Polygon, double SKeyOffset, double SOffset, double LaneAlpha, double InHeightOffset) const { return InHeightOffset; }
103: 
104: 	//~ Ring host. Default: contributes nothing, so an attribute that only makes sense on a lane needs no opt-out.
105: 
106: 	/** S positions the host must sample along the RING itself so the profile's breakpoints become vertices.
107: 	 *  Appends. `RingLength > 0` => values are wrapped into [0, RingLength). */
108: 	virtual void GetAdditionalRingOffsets(double SCenter, double RingLength, TArray<double>& OutS) const {}
109: 
110: 	/** Inner chain + rungs to carve inside the ring (see FRoadRingCutContour). */
111: 	virtual void BuildRingCutContour(double SCenter, double RingLength, FRoadRingCutContour& Out) const {}
112: 
113: 	/**
114: 	 * The cut this value represents, so a ring host can register it as a SCOPE-WIDE site
115: 	 * (FRoadTriangulationData::CurbCutSites) instead of applying it to its own vertices only. False = this
116: 	 * attribute is not a curb cut and contributes no site.
117: 	 */
118: 	virtual bool GetRingCurbCut(FRoadCurbCut& Out) const { return false; }
119: };

104: 	//~ Ring host. Default: contributes nothing, so an attribute that only makes sense on a lane needs no opt-out.
105: 
106: 	/** S positions the host must sample along the RING itself so the profile's breakpoints become vertices.
107: 	 *  Appends. `RingLength > 0` => values are wrapped into [0, RingLength). */
108: 	virtual void GetAdditionalRingOffsets(double SCenter, double RingLength, TArray<double>& OutS) const {}
109: 
110: 	/** Inner chain + rungs to carve inside the ring (see FRoadRingCutContour). */
111: 	virtual void BuildRingCutContour(double SCenter, double RingLength, FRoadRingCutContour& Out) const {}
112: 
113: 	/**
114: 	 * The cut this value represents, so a ring host can register it as a SCOPE-WIDE site
115: 	 * (FRoadTriangulationData::CurbCutSites) instead of applying it to its own vertices only. False = this
116: 	 * attribute is not a curb cut and contributes no site.
117: 	 */
118: 	virtual bool GetRingCurbCut(FRoadCurbCut& Out) const { return false; }
119: };
---
95: 	 * non-const reference to the entire build.
96: 	 */
97: 	virtual void OnPolygonCreated(const MetaRoad::FProceduralPolygon_RoadLane& Polygon,
98: 	                              MetaRoad::IRoadConstraintSink& Sink, double SKeyOffset) const {}
99: 
100: 	virtual void GetAdditionalSplinePoints(const MetaRoad::FProceduralPolygon_RoadLane& Polygon, double SKeyOffset, double LaneAlpha, TArray<double>& OutSegments) const {}
101: 
102: 	virtual double GetHeightOffset(const MetaRoad::FProceduralPolygon_RoadLane& Polygon, double SKeyOffset, double SOffset, double LaneAlpha, double InHeightOffset) const { return InHeightOffset; }
103: 
104: 	//~ Ring host. Default: contributes nothing, so an attribute that only makes sense on a lane needs no opt-out.
105: 
106: 	/** S positions the host must sample along the RING itself so the profile's breakpoints become vertices.
107: 	 *  Appends. `RingLength > 0` => values are wrapped into [0, RingLength). */
108: 	virtual void GetAdditionalRingOffsets(double SCenter, double RingLength, TArray<double>& OutS) const {}
109: 
110: 	/** Inner chain + rungs to carve inside the ring (see FRoadRingCutContour). */
111: 	virtual void BuildRingCutContour(double SCenter, double RingLength, FRoadRingCutContour& Out) const {}
112: 
113: 	/**
114: 	 * The cut this value represents, so a ring host can register it as a SCOPE-WIDE site
115: 	 * (FRoadTriangulationData::CurbCutSites) instead of applying it to its own vertices only. False = this
116: 	 * attribute is not a curb cut and contributes no site.
117: 	 */
118: 	virtual bool GetRingCurbCut(FRoadCurbCut& Out) const { return false; }
119: };
---
96: 	 */
97: 	virtual void OnPolygonCreated(const MetaRoad::FProceduralPolygon_RoadLane& Polygon,
98: 	                              MetaRoad::IRoadConstraintSink& Sink, double SKeyOffset) const {}
99: 
100: 	virtual void GetAdditionalSplinePoints(const MetaRoad::FProceduralPolygon_RoadLane& Polygon, double SKeyOffset, double LaneAlpha, TArray<double>& OutSegments) const {}
101: 
102: 	virtual double GetHeightOffset(const MetaRoad::FProceduralPolygon_RoadLane& Polygon, double SKeyOffset, double SOffset, double LaneAlpha, double InHeightOffset) const { return InHeightOffset; }
103: 
104: 	//~ Ring host. Default: contributes nothing, so an attribute that only makes sense on a lane needs no opt-out.
105: 
106: 	/** S positions the host must sample along the RING itself so the profile's breakpoints become vertices.
107: 	 *  Appends. `RingLength > 0` => values are wrapped into [0, RingLength). */
108: 	virtual void GetAdditionalRingOffsets(double SCenter, double RingLength, TArray<double>& OutS) const {}

116: 	 * attribute is not a curb cut and contributes no site.
117: 	 */

98: 	                              MetaRoad::IRoadConstraintSink& Sink, double SKeyOffset) const {}

9: #include "RoadMeshBuild/RoadSplineEditorComponent.h"
10: #include "RoadFeature.h"                 // FMagneticSpan (magnetized-fill span matching)
11: #include "Assets/RoadLaneAttributeZone.h" // MetaRoad::FRoadLaneZoneSpan (the RoadZone lane attribute)
12: #include "Geometry/Arrangement2d.h"      // GUIFlags::WasOverlaped — the other half of the flag space (static_assert below)
13: #include "Polygon2.h"
14: #include "CompGeom/Delaunay2.h"
15: #include "DynamicMesh/DynamicMesh3.h"
16: #include "Util/ProgressCancel.h"
17: #include "Utils/GeomUtils.h"
18: #include "RoadMeshBuild/RoadConstraintSink.h"
19: 
20: using namespace UE::Geometry;
21: 
22: struct FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue;
23: 
24: namespace MetaRoad
25: {
26: 	struct FProceduralPolygon;
27: 	struct FRoadTriangulationData;
28: 	class FRoadVertexTable;
29: 
30: 	//~ The two write channels of the build, one per phase (RoadMeshBuild/RoadArrangementBuilder.h). A polygon
31: 	//~ holds a CONST Owner and receives these as parameters, so what it may write is decided by which phase it
32: 	//~ is in, not by what it can reach.
33: 	class FRoadArrangementBuilder;  // open phase: inserts
34: 	struct FRoadCompletionSink;     // after Close(): the 3D lift + the published feature-line chains
35: 
36: 	/**
37: 	 * Which road zone an arrangement edge came from. OR-combined when inserts overlap, so a GID can only ever be used
38: 	 * to EXCLUDE ("is CenterLine set?"), never to identify ("is this the driving surface?") — and note 0 is a valid
39: 	 * value, meaning a zone that is neither Driving nor Sidewalk (Marking/Median/Parking/Tram, ProceduralPolygon.cpp).
40: 	 *
41: 	 * uint8 on purpose: Geometry/Arrangement2d.h reopens this namespace for GUIFlags::WasOverlaped, which sits at bit
42: 	 * 8 precisely so it cannot collide with these. Keep zone bits inside 0-7; see the note there for the bug that
43: 	 * arose when the two headers each guessed at free bits.
44: 	 */
45: 	namespace GUIFlags
46: 	{
47: 		static uint8 DrivingSurface = 1 << 0;
48: 		static uint8 SidewalksSoft  = 1 << 1;
49: 		static uint8 SidewalksHard  = 1 << 2;
50: 		static uint8 CenterLine     = 1 << 3;
51: 
52: 		static uint8 Other          = 1 << 7;
53: 
54: 		// The two headers cannot merge (generic geometry must not depend on road zones), so the bit reservation is
55: 		// enforced here instead of trusted: this is the one place that sees BOTH halves of the namespace.
56: 		static_assert(WasOverlaped > (int32)MAX_uint8,
57: 			"GUIFlags::WasOverlaped must stay above the uint8 zone bits (Geometry/Arrangement2d.h). Overlapping them "
58: 			"silently mislabels edges rather than failing -- which is exactly how the '1 < 31' aliasing bug survived.");
59: 	}
60: 
61: 
62: 	/**
63: 	 * FRoadVertexInfo
64: 	 */
65: 	struct FRoadVertexInfo
66: 	{
67: 		const FProceduralPolygon * Poly;
68: 		FRoadPosition Pos;
69: 		double Alpha0;
70: 		double Alpha1;
71: 		int VID;
72: 		/**
73: 		 * This vertex sits on the SEAM of a closed-loop lane, where S is both 0 and the spline length.
74: 		 *
75: 		 * It was one value of a seven-value ERoadVertexInfoFlags (Inside / Outside / BeginCap / EndCap /
76: 		 * LoopSeam / OverlapPoly / LoopPoly). The other six were written at every call site and READ NOWHERE --
77: 		 * the whole channel had exactly four readers and all four tested LoopSeam. The argument was still
78: 		 * mandatory, so every caller had to name a value that meant nothing, and the enum read as though the
79: 		 * build tracked where a vertex came from. It does not.
80: 		 *
81: 		 * OR-combined across polygons, like the flags were: a vertex reached first as a cap and then as a seam
82: 		 * is a seam. */
83: 		bool bLoopSeam = false;
84: 		double HeightOffset;
85: 	};
86: 
87: 	/**
88: 	 * FArrangementVertex3d
89: 	 */
90: 	struct METAROADEDITOR_API FArrangementVertex3d
91: 	{

2:  * Copyright (c) 2026 Ivan Zhukov. All Rights Reserved.
3:  * Email: ivzhuk7@gmail.com
4:  */

37: 	 * Which road zone an arrangement edge came from. OR-combined when inserts overlap, so a GID can only ever be used
38: 	 * to EXCLUDE ("is CenterLine set?"), never to identify ("is this the driving surface?") — and note 0 is a valid
39: 	 * value, meaning a zone that is neither Driving nor Sidewalk (Marking/Median/Parking/Tram, ProceduralPolygon.cpp).
40: 	 *
41: 	 * uint8 on purpose: Geometry/Arrangement2d.h reopens this namespace for GUIFlags::WasOverlaped, which sits at bit
42: 	 * 8 precisely so it cannot collide with these. Keep zone bits inside 0-7; see the note there for the bug that
43: 	 * arose when the two headers each guessed at free bits.
44: 	 */
45: 	namespace GUIFlags
46: 	{
47: 		static uint8 DrivingSurface = 1 << 0;
48: 		static uint8 SidewalksSoft  = 1 << 1;
49: 		static uint8 SidewalksHard  = 1 << 2;
50: 		static uint8 CenterLine     = 1 << 3;
51: 
52: 		static uint8 Other          = 1 << 7;
53: 
54: 		// The two headers cannot merge (generic geometry must not depend on road zones), so the bit reservation is
55: 		// enforced here instead of trusted: this is the one place that sees BOTH halves of the namespace.
56: 		static_assert(WasOverlaped > (int32)MAX_uint8,
57: 			"GUIFlags::WasOverlaped must stay above the uint8 zone bits (Geometry/Arrangement2d.h). Overlapping them "
58: 			"silently mislabels edges rather than failing -- which is exactly how the '1 < 31' aliasing bug survived.");

62: 	/**
63: 	 * FRoadVertexInfo
64: 	 */
65: 	struct FRoadVertexInfo
66: 	{
67: 		const FProceduralPolygon * Poly;
68: 		FRoadPosition Pos;
69: 		double Alpha0;
70: 		double Alpha1;
71: 		int VID;
72: 		/**
73: 		 * This vertex sits on the SEAM of a closed-loop lane, where S is both 0 and the spline length.
74: 		 *
75: 		 * It was one value of a seven-value ERoadVertexInfoFlags (Inside / Outside / BeginCap / EndCap /
76: 		 * LoopSeam / OverlapPoly / LoopPoly). The other six were written at every call site and READ NOWHERE --
77: 		 * the whole channel had exactly four readers and all four tested LoopSeam. The argument was still
78: 		 * mandatory, so every caller had to name a value that meant nothing, and the enum read as though the
79: 		 * build tracked where a vertex came from. It does not.
80: 		 *
81: 		 * OR-combined across polygons, like the flags were: a vertex reached first as a cap and then as a seam
82: 		 * is a seam. */
83: 		bool bLoopSeam = false;
84: 		double HeightOffset;
85: 	};

87: 	/**
88: 	 * FArrangementVertex3d
89: 	 */
90: 	struct METAROADEDITOR_API FArrangementVertex3d
91: 	{
92: 		TMap<const FProceduralPolygon*, FRoadVertexInfo> Infos;
93: 		FVector Vertex;
94: 		FVector Normal;
95: 
96: 		/**
97: 		 * Per-VERTEX multiplier on the resolved sidewalk lift [0..1], MIN-combined across polygons — the shared
98: 		 * "this vertex is cut down" channel used by the CLOSED-RING curb-cut hosts (looped road zone, sidewalk
99: 		 * spline fill). 1.0 = untouched.
100: 		 *
101: 		 * It exists because the two consumers of the lift disagree by design: the sidewalk SURFACE takes the MAX
102: 		 * of every polygon's HeightOffset at the vertex (FSidewalksOp), while a CURB takes the OWNING polygon's
---
87: 	/**
88: 	 * FArrangementVertex3d
89: 	 */
90: 	struct METAROADEDITOR_API FArrangementVertex3d
91: 	{
92: 		TMap<const FProceduralPolygon*, FRoadVertexInfo> Infos;
93: 		FVector Vertex;
94: 		FVector Normal;
95: 
96: 		/**
97: 		 * Per-VERTEX multiplier on the resolved sidewalk lift [0..1], MIN-combined across polygons — the shared
98: 		 * "this vertex is cut down" channel used by the CLOSED-RING curb-cut hosts (looped road zone, sidewalk
99: 		 * spline fill). 1.0 = untouched.
100: 		 *
101: 		 * It exists because the two consumers of the lift disagree by design: the sidewalk SURFACE takes the MAX
102: 		 * of every polygon's HeightOffset at the vertex (FSidewalksOp), while a CURB takes the OWNING polygon's
103: 		 * (RoadPolygonUtils::MakePolyline). A cut can only ever lower one polygon's HeightOffset, so wherever a
104: 		 * ring coincides with another sidewalk polygon at full height the MAX cancelled the cut on the surface
105: 		 * while the curb still dropped. A min-combined scale applied at BOTH sites cannot be cancelled.
106: 		 *
107: 		 * WRITTEN BY ONE STAGE, NEVER BY A POLYGON — BuildStages::ApplyCurbCuts, from the scope-wide
108: 		 * FRoadTriangulationData::CurbCutSites. A ramp is a property of a PLACE: applied from the authoring
109: 		 * polygon's FillVertexInfo (as it first was) it reached only that polygon's own vertices and stopped dead
110: 		 * at the seam with the neighbouring lane, which is the bug this channel is now free of.
111: 		 *
112: 		 * The LANE host deliberately does NOT use it: FRoadLaneAttributeCurbCutValue::GetHeightOffset keeps
113: 		 * baking its cut straight into FRoadVertexInfo::HeightOffset, so the lane path stays bit-identical (its
114: 		 * scale simply remains 1.0).
115: 		 */
116: 		double HeightScale = 1.0;
117: 
118: 		/** MIN-combine a cut factor into HeightScale (cuts compose multiplicatively only through the strongest). */
119: 		void ApplyHeightScale(double InScale) { HeightScale = FMath::Min(HeightScale, InScale); }
120: 
121: 		/**
122: 		 * How far this vertex is lowered by the EDGE PROFILE (gutter), in cm, >= 0. Subtracted from Vertex.Z once,
123: 		 * after the OverlapRadius kernel and before FinalizeVertexGeometry.
124: 		 *
125: 		 * Kept SEPARATE from Vertex.Z rather than folded straight into it, because Snap to Ground overwrites Z
126: 		 * outright on the game thread -- ApplyGroundSnap re-applies this so the profile survives, and traces from
127: 		 * Vertex.Z + ProfileDrop so a deep drop cannot walk the trace window out of SnapTraceHeightAbove.
128: 		 */
129: 		double ProfileDrop = 0.0;
130: 	};
131: 
132: 	/**
133: 	 * ERoadPolygonType
134: 	 */
135: 	enum ERoadPolygonType
136: 	{
137: 		RoadLane,
138: 		SplineLoop,
139: 		Simple,
140: 		FeatureLine
141: 	};
142: 
143: 	/**
144: 	 * FLineInfo 
145: 	 * Notes:
146: 	 * If VID_B == -1 means the line contain only one point (VID_A)
147: 	 * If VID_A == VID_B means the line is loop
148: 	 * if VID_A == -1 means line is not valid
149: 	 */
150: 	struct FLineInfo
151: 	{
152: 		int PID = -1;
153: 		int VID_A = -1;
154: 		int VID_B = -1;
155: 
156: 		inline bool IsValid() const { return VID_A != -1; }
157: 		inline bool IsLoop() const { return IsValid() && VID_A == VID_B; }
158: 	};
159: 
160: 	/**
161: 	 * FTriInfo -- one polygon's three per-vertex Infos for one triangle, as a READ-ONLY view.
162: 	 *
163: 	 * const because FindTri() is const and reaches them through the polygon's const Owner. Nothing has ever
164: 	 * written through it: every user (UV layers, GetSOffset, per-triangle zone resolution) reads. The one place
165: 	 * an Info is written is AddVertexInfo, which takes the vertex table explicitly.
166: 	 */
167: 	struct FTriInfo
168: 	{
169: 		const FRoadVertexInfo* A;
170: 		const FRoadVertexInfo* B;
171: 		const FRoadVertexInfo* C;
172: 
173: 		inline bool IsValid() const { return A && B && C; }
174: 		inline const FRoadVertexInfo& GetVertexInfo(int VID) const
175: 		{
176: 			check(IsValid());
177: 			auto& Ret = A->VID == VID ? *A : (B->VID == VID ? *B : *C);
178: 			check(Ret.VID == VID);
179: 			return Ret;
180: 		}
181: 	};
182:

184: 	 * FProceduralPolygon
185: 	 */

93: 		FVector Vertex;
94: 		FVector Normal;
95: 
96: 		/**
97: 		 * Per-VERTEX multiplier on the resolved sidewalk lift [0..1], MIN-combined across polygons — the shared
98: 		 * "this vertex is cut down" channel used by the CLOSED-RING curb-cut hosts (looped road zone, sidewalk
99: 		 * spline fill). 1.0 = untouched.
100: 		 *
101: 		 * It exists because the two consumers of the lift disagree by design: the sidewalk SURFACE takes the MAX
102: 		 * of every polygon's HeightOffset at the vertex (FSidewalksOp), while a CURB takes the OWNING polygon's
103: 		 * (RoadPolygonUtils::MakePolyline). A cut can only ever lower one polygon's HeightOffset, so wherever a
104: 		 * ring coincides with another sidewalk polygon at full height the MAX cancelled the cut on the surface
105: 		 * while the curb still dropped. A min-combined scale applied at BOTH sites cannot be cancelled.
106: 		 *
107: 		 * WRITTEN BY ONE STAGE, NEVER BY A POLYGON — BuildStages::ApplyCurbCuts, from the scope-wide
108: 		 * FRoadTriangulationData::CurbCutSites. A ramp is a property of a PLACE: applied from the authoring
109: 		 * polygon's FillVertexInfo (as it first was) it reached only that polygon's own vertices and stopped dead
110: 		 * at the seam with the neighbouring lane, which is the bug this channel is now free of.
111: 		 *
112: 		 * The LANE host deliberately does NOT use it: FRoadLaneAttributeCurbCutValue::GetHeightOffset keeps
113: 		 * baking its cut straight into FRoadVertexInfo::HeightOffset, so the lane path stays bit-identical (its
114: 		 * scale simply remains 1.0).
115: 		 */
116: 		double HeightScale = 1.0;
117: 
118: 		/** MIN-combine a cut factor into HeightScale (cuts compose multiplicatively only through the strongest). */
119: 		void ApplyHeightScale(double InScale) { HeightScale = FMath::Min(HeightScale, InScale); }
120: 
121: 		/**
122: 		 * How far this vertex is lowered by the EDGE PROFILE (gutter), in cm, >= 0. Subtracted from Vertex.Z once,
123: 		 * after the OverlapRadius kernel and before FinalizeVertexGeometry.
124: 		 *
125: 		 * Kept SEPARATE from Vertex.Z rather than folded straight into it, because Snap to Ground overwrites Z
126: 		 * outright on the game thread -- ApplyGroundSnap re-applies this so the profile survives, and traces from
127: 		 * Vertex.Z + ProfileDrop so a deep drop cannot walk the trace window out of SnapTraceHeightAbove.
128: 		 */
129: 		double ProfileDrop = 0.0;
130: 	};
