92: 		TMap<const FProceduralPolygon*, FRoadVertexInfo> Infos;
93: 		FVector Vertex;
94: 		FVector Normal;
95: 
96: 		/**
97: 		 * Per-VERTEX multiplier on the resolved sidewalk lift [0..1], MIN-combined across polygons — the shared
98: 		 * "this vertex is cut down" channel used by the CLOSED-RING curb-cut hosts (looped road zone, sidewalk
99: 		 * spline fill). 1.0 = untouched.
100: 		 *
101: 		 * It exists because the two consumers of the lift disagree by design: the sidewalk SURFACE takes the MAX
102: 		 * of every polygon's HeightOffset at the vertex (FSidewalksOp), while a CURB takes the OWNING polygon's
103: 		 * (RoadPolygonUtils::MakePolyline). A cut can only ever lower one polygon's HeightOffset, so wherever a
104: 		 * ring coincides with another sidewalk polygon at full height the MAX cancelled the cut on the surface
105: 		 * while the curb still dropped. A min-combined scale applied at BOTH sites cannot be cancelled.
106: 		 *
107: 		 * WRITTEN BY ONE STAGE, NEVER BY A POLYGON — BuildStages::ApplyCurbCuts, from the scope-wide
108: 		 * FRoadTriangulationData::CurbCutSites. A ramp is a property of a PLACE: applied from the authoring
109: 		 * polygon's FillVertexInfo (as it first was) it reached only that polygon's own vertices and stopped dead
110: 		 * at the seam with the neighbouring lane, which is the bug this channel is now free of.
111: 		 *
112: 		 * The LANE host deliberately does NOT use it: FRoadLaneAttributeCurbCutValue::GetHeightOffset keeps
113: 		 * baking its cut straight into FRoadVertexInfo::HeightOffset, so the lane path stays bit-identical (its
114: 		 * scale simply remains 1.0).
115: 		 */
116: 		double HeightScale = 1.0;
117: 
118: 		/** MIN-combine a cut factor into HeightScale (cuts compose multiplicatively only through the strongest). */
119: 		void ApplyHeightScale(double InScale) { HeightScale = FMath::Min(HeightScale, InScale); }
120: 
121: 		/**
122: 		 * How far this vertex is lowered by the EDGE PROFILE (gutter), in cm, >= 0. Subtracted from Vertex.Z once,
123: 		 * after the OverlapRadius kernel and before FinalizeVertexGeometry.
124: 		 *
125: 		 * Kept SEPARATE from Vertex.Z rather than folded straight into it, because Snap to Ground overwrites Z
126: 		 * outright on the game thread -- ApplyGroundSnap re-applies this so the profile survives, and traces from
127: 		 * Vertex.Z + ProfileDrop so a deep drop cannot walk the trace window out of SnapTraceHeightAbove.
128: 		 */
129: 		double ProfileDrop = 0.0;
130: 	};
131: 
132: 	/**
133: 	 * ERoadPolygonType
134: 	 */
135: 	enum ERoadPolygonType
136: 	{
137: 		RoadLane,
138: 		SplineLoop,
139: 		Simple,
140: 		FeatureLine
141: 	};
142: 
143: 	/**
144: 	 * FLineInfo 
145: 	 * Notes:
146: 	 * If VID_B == -1 means the line contain only one point (VID_A)
147: 	 * If VID_A == VID_B means the line is loop
148: 	 * if VID_A == -1 means line is not valid
149: 	 */
150: 	struct FLineInfo
151: 	{
152: 		int PID = -1;
153: 		int VID_A = -1;
154: 		int VID_B = -1;
155: 
156: 		inline bool IsValid() const { return VID_A != -1; }
157: 		inline bool IsLoop() const { return IsValid() && VID_A == VID_B; }
158: 	};
159: 
160: 	/**
161: 	 * FTriInfo -- one polygon's three per-vertex Infos for one triangle, as a READ-ONLY view.
162: 	 *
163: 	 * const because FindTri() is const and reaches them through the polygon's const Owner. Nothing has ever
164: 	 * written through it: every user (UV layers, GetSOffset, per-triangle zone resolution) reads. The one place
165: 	 * an Info is written is AddVertexInfo, which takes the vertex table explicitly.
166: 	 */
167: 	struct FTriInfo
168: 	{
169: 		const FRoadVertexInfo* A;
170: 		const FRoadVertexInfo* B;
171: 		const FRoadVertexInfo* C;
172: 
173: 		inline bool IsValid() const { return A && B && C; }
174: 		inline const FRoadVertexInfo& GetVertexInfo(int VID) const
175: 		{
176: 			check(IsValid());
177: 			auto& Ret = A->VID == VID ? *A : (B->VID == VID ? *B : *C);
178: 			check(Ret.VID == VID);
179: 			return Ret;
180: 		}
181: 	};
182: 
183: 	/**
184: 	 * FProceduralPolygon
185: 	 */
186: 	struct METAROADEDITOR_API FProceduralPolygon
187: 	{
188: 
189: 
190: 		FProceduralPolygon(const FRoadTriangulationData& Owner)
191: 			: Owner(Owner)
192: 		{}
---
203: 		/** Fill Vertices[VID].Infos[this]. Base impl: find-or-add, OR-combine the seam flag on an existing entry, else
204: 		 *  create one with the common fields + XY from the graph, delegating the subtype lift (Pos Z/Quat/ROffset/SOffset
205: 		 *  + HeightOffset) to FillVertexInfo. RoadLane overrides this whole method (it needs a UV-parameterized 2-overload
206: 		 *  variant); the other subtypes override only FillVertexInfo.
207: 		 *
208: 		 *  The table is a PARAMETER, not Owner.Vertices3d: this is the polygon layer's one write into the build, it
209: 		 *  happens in two different phases (completion and the triangle claim), and passing it is what states at every
210: 		 *  call site that the polygon computes while the table is written. */
211: 		virtual void AddVertexInfo(FRoadVertexTable& Vertices, int VID, bool bLoopSeam) const;
212: 
213: 		/** The subtype lift of ONE vertex: Pos Z/Quat/ROffset/SOffset, and optionally HeightOffset.
214: 		 *
215: 		 *  Info is the entry just created in the vertex's Infos, and it is the ONLY thing an override may write:
216: 		 *  anything shared by several polygons at one vertex belongs to a stage, not to whichever polygon happened
217: 		 *  to reach it first (FArrangementVertex3d::HeightScale is the worked example). */
218: 		virtual void FillVertexInfo(FRoadVertexInfo& Info, int VID, FVector2d V2d) const {}
219: 
220: 		virtual const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>& GetRoadZone() const = 0;
221: 
222: 		//~ S-varying zone. Only FProceduralPolygon_RoadLane ever answers differently (the RoadZone lane
223: 		//  attribute overrides the BASE FRoadZone fields over a sub-range); every other polygon keeps one
224: 		//  zone for its whole surface, so the defaults below ARE the old behaviour.
225: 		//  Note what deliberately does NOT go through these: the concrete zone SUBTYPE, which decides which
226: 		//  op a polygon feeds (drive surface / sidewalk / curbs) and is resolved once per polygon.
227: 
228: 		/** Zone in effect at absolute arc length SOffset. Default: the polygon-wide zone (S ignored). */
229: 		virtual const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>& GetRoadZoneAt(double SOffset) const { return GetRoadZone(); }
230: 
231: 		/** Zone in effect on ONE claimed triangle. Default: the polygon-wide zone (TID ignored). */
232: 		virtual const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>& GetRoadZoneForTri(int TID) const { return GetRoadZone(); }
233: 
234: 		/** Every distinct zone this polygon can present along S -- for op-level "does this polygon take part
235: 		 *  at all?" gates that must not miss a zone only some of the surface has. Resets Out; never empty. */
236: 		virtual void GetDistinctRoadZones(TArray<const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>*>& Out) const;
237: 
238: 		virtual FText GetDescription() const = 0;
239: 		/** Write this polygon's UV layers for one claimed triangle. Every scale it needs lives in Owner.Params
240: 		 *  (DefaultUVDensity / UV0ScaleFactor / UV1ScaleFactor / UVMaxSize), so nothing is threaded through the
241: 		 *  call sites -- adding a UV param to FRoadTriangulationParams needs no signature or op change. */
242: 		virtual bool SetUVLayers(FDynamicMesh3& Mesh, int TID) const = 0;
243: 		virtual double GetMaterialPriority() const = 0;
244: 		virtual int GetObjectIndex() const = 0;
245: 
246: 		const UE::Geometry::FGeometryResult& GetResult() const { return ResultInfo; }
247: 
248: 		/**
249: 		 * Planar world-DENSITY UV for one claimed triangle, replicated into every UV layer the mesh has.
250: 		 *
251: 		 * The shared mapping of the two FILL polygons (looped road zone and simple fill). Their two overrides
252: 		 * were ~50 lines each and differed in exactly ONE expression -- which FRoadTextureTransform the shape
253: 		 * carries -- so that expression is the parameter and the rest is here.
254: 		 */
255: 		bool SetPlanarDensityUVLayers(FDynamicMesh3& Mesh, int TID, const FRoadTextureTransform& Tex) const;
256: 
257: 		FTriInfo FindTri(int TID) const;
258: 		/** "Do not fill me": an OPEN chain, or no chain at all.
259: 		 *
260: 		 *  The empty case is why this is not simply `Boundary.Num() && ...`. Every failure path in
261: 		 *  OnCompleteArrangement returns before building Boundary, and CompleteArrangement ignores the bool it
262: 		 *  returns -- so a polygon that failed to recover stayed in Data.Polygons with an empty Boundary and
263: 		 *  answered "no, I am a closed fillable polygon". ClaimTriangles then ran a NonZeroWinding fill over an
264: 		 *  empty edge set, claiming an arbitrary region and painting it with this polygon's material, silently.
265: 		 *  Answering "not fillable" makes a failed polygon claim nothing, which is the only safe reading. */
266: 		inline bool IsPolyline() const { return Boundary.Num() == 0 || Boundary[0].A != Boundary.Last().B; }
267: 
268: 		//~ There are no insertion methods here any more. Inserting requires holding a FRoadArrangementBuilder, which
269: 		//~ a polygon only ever receives as a constructor PARAMETER -- so a polygon cannot touch the arrangement once
270: 		//~ it exists, and the "everything is inserted before the vertex table is sized" rule is a lifetime rather
271: 		//~ than a comment. See RoadMeshBuild/RoadArrangementBuilder.h.
272: 
273: 		/** Shared recovery tail for a CLOSED filling polygon: after LineVertices is recovered, AddVertexInfo each
274: 		 *  vertex, build the directed Boundary, and force clockwise winding (reversing LineVertices/Boundary/Poly2d together).
275: 		 *  Used by RoadLoop and Simple; RoadLane (Holes side-effects) and FeatureLine (open boundary) build their own. */
276: 		void BuildClosedRingBoundary(FRoadVertexTable& Vertices);
277: 
278: 		/** Recover ONE inner ring from its own PID and APPEND it to Holes as a CW FIndex2i chain -- the inner-ring
279: 		 *  counterpart of BuildClosedRingBoundary, deliberately the same three steps: AddVertexInfo every vertex (a
280: 		 *  hole ring is THIS polygon's own geometry, so the ">= 1 Info per graph vertex" rule is ours to satisfy),
281: 		 *  build the directed chain, force CW so OpUtils::MergeBoundaries' reversal cancels the outer winding.
282: 		 *
283: 		 *  Returns false and APPENDS NOTHING when the chain cannot be recovered or is degenerate. That is the point
284: 		 *  of the return value: an EMPTY entry in Holes is byte-identical to no hole at all at MergeBoundaries time,
285: 		 *  so a caller that pushed one would be silently reporting a hole it did not make.
286: 		 *
287: 		 *  Poly2d is deliberately NOT touched -- it stays the OUTER ring, which is what the UV centring and every
288: 		 *  Contains() test read. Used by Simple today; on the base so a second host is a three-line change. */
289: 		bool BuildClosedRingHole(FRoadVertexTable& Vertices, const FLineInfo& HoleLineInfo);
290: 
291: 		//~ ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
292: 		//~ Surface refinement (TargetEdgeLength). A polygon does NOT decide where extra vertices go -- density
293: 		//~ is a property of the SURFACE, not of one shape, which is why the whole decision lives in
294: 		//~ MetaRoad::SurfaceRefine (RoadSurfaceRefine.h) and only these two hooks remain here: "what area do I
295: 		//~ cover" (Poly2d + Holes) and "which vertices did the refiner attribute to me".
296: 
297: 		/** VIDs of the isolated interior vertices the refiner attributed to this polygon. Kept so the claim stage
298: 		 *  can guarantee the ">=1 FRoadVertexInfo per graph vertex" invariant even if one of them somehow escapes
299: 		 *  the per-polygon triangle claim. */
300: 		TArray<int> InteriorSampleVIDs;
301: 
302: 		/** Holes are stored as FIndex2i VID edge lists; rebuild them as polygons for containment tests.
303: 		 *  Call once per pass, not per candidate. Empty for every polygon except a closed-loop RoadLane today. */
---
275: 		 *  Used by RoadLoop and Simple; RoadLane (Holes side-effects) and FeatureLine (open boundary) build their own. */
276: 		void BuildClosedRingBoundary(FRoadVertexTable& Vertices);
277: 
278: 		/** Recover ONE inner ring from its own PID and APPEND it to Holes as a CW FIndex2i chain -- the inner-ring
279: 		 *  counterpart of BuildClosedRingBoundary, deliberately the same three steps: AddVertexInfo every vertex (a
280: 		 *  hole ring is THIS polygon's own geometry, so the ">= 1 Info per graph vertex" rule is ours to satisfy),
281: 		 *  build the directed chain, force CW so OpUtils::MergeBoundaries' reversal cancels the outer winding.
282: 		 *
283: 		 *  Returns false and APPENDS NOTHING when the chain cannot be recovered or is degenerate. That is the point
284: 		 *  of the return value: an EMPTY entry in Holes is byte-identical to no hole at all at MergeBoundaries time,
285: 		 *  so a caller that pushed one would be silently reporting a hole it did not make.
286: 		 *
287: 		 *  Poly2d is deliberately NOT touched -- it stays the OUTER ring, which is what the UV centring and every
288: 		 *  Contains() test read. Used by Simple today; on the base so a second host is a three-line change. */
289: 		bool BuildClosedRingHole(FRoadVertexTable& Vertices, const FLineInfo& HoleLineInfo);
290: 
291: 		//~ ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
292: 		//~ Surface refinement (TargetEdgeLength). A polygon does NOT decide where extra vertices go -- density
293: 		//~ is a property of the SURFACE, not of one shape, which is why the whole decision lives in
294: 		//~ MetaRoad::SurfaceRefine (RoadSurfaceRefine.h) and only these two hooks remain here: "what area do I
295: 		//~ cover" (Poly2d + Holes) and "which vertices did the refiner attribute to me".
296: 
297: 		/** VIDs of the isolated interior vertices the refiner attributed to this polygon. Kept so the claim stage
298: 		 *  can guarantee the ">=1 FRoadVertexInfo per graph vertex" invariant even if one of them somehow escapes
299: 		 *  the per-polygon triangle claim. */
300: 		TArray<int> InteriorSampleVIDs;
301: 
302: 		/** Holes are stored as FIndex2i VID edge lists; rebuild them as polygons for containment tests.
303: 		 *  Call once per pass, not per candidate. Empty for every polygon except a closed-loop RoadLane today. */
304: 		void BuildHolePolygons(TArray<UE::Geometry::FPolygon2d>& Out) const;
305: 
306: 		/**
307: 		 * The whole build, READ-ONLY.
308: 		 *
309: 		 * It used to be a public NON-const reference, and that was not an oversight to be tidied away -- it was a
310: 		 * dependency: const-methods wrote into Owner.Vertices3d, a lane constructor grew the input list, and the
311: 		 * curb-cut attribute (a file outside RoadMeshBuild entirely) reached through it into the arrangement. Each
312: 		 * of those now has a phase-scoped channel instead (FRoadArrangementBuilder, FRoadCompletionSink,
313: 		 * IRoadConstraintSink), which is what makes const possible at all.
314: 		 *
315: 		 * The arrangement is no longer part of that: it is private on FRoadTriangulationData, behind GetArrangement()
316: 		 * / GetGraph(), with three friends -- one per phase. What still launders const here is `Polygons`, a
317: 		 * TArray<TUniquePtr<FProceduralPolygon>>: the same TUniquePtr trick, the same fix, not yet done.
318: 		 */
319: 		const FRoadTriangulationData& Owner;
320: 
321: 		TArray<FIndex2i> Boundary; // ClockWise
322: 		TArray<TArray<FIndex2i>> Holes; // ClockWise
323: 		TArray<int> TrianglesIDs;
324: 		TArray<int> LineVertices;        // ordered boundary vertex list (loop / simple polygons)
325: 		UE::Geometry::FPolygon2d Poly2d; // 2D polygon, built on arrangement completion
326: 		UE::Geometry::FGeometryResult ResultInfo;
327: 
328: 
329: 
330: 	};

291: 		//~ ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
292: 		//~ Surface refinement (TargetEdgeLength). A polygon does NOT decide where extra vertices go -- density
293: 		//~ is a property of the SURFACE, not of one shape, which is why the whole decision lives in
294: 		//~ MetaRoad::SurfaceRefine (RoadSurfaceRefine.h) and only these two hooks remain here: "what area do I
295: 		//~ cover" (Poly2d + Holes) and "which vertices did the refiner attribute to me".
296: 
297: 		/** VIDs of the isolated interior vertices the refiner attributed to this polygon. Kept so the claim stage
298: 		 *  can guarantee the ">=1 FRoadVertexInfo per graph vertex" invariant even if one of them somehow escapes
299: 		 *  the per-polygon triangle claim. */
300: 		TArray<int> InteriorSampleVIDs;
301: 
302: 		/** Holes are stored as FIndex2i VID edge lists; rebuild them as polygons for containment tests.
303: 		 *  Call once per pass, not per candidate. Empty for every polygon except a closed-loop RoadLane today. */
304: 		void BuildHolePolygons(TArray<UE::Geometry::FPolygon2d>& Out) const;
305: 
306: 		/**
307: 		 * The whole build, READ-ONLY.
308: 		 *
309: 		 * It used to be a public NON-const reference, and that was not an oversight to be tidied away -- it was a
310: 		 * dependency: const-methods wrote into Owner.Vertices3d, a lane constructor grew the input list, and the
311: 		 * curb-cut attribute (a file outside RoadMeshBuild entirely) reached through it into the arrangement. Each
312: 		 * of those now has a phase-scoped channel instead (FRoadArrangementBuilder, FRoadCompletionSink,
313: 		 * IRoadConstraintSink), which is what makes const possible at all.
314: 		 *
315: 		 * The arrangement is no longer part of that: it is private on FRoadTriangulationData, behind GetArrangement()
316: 		 * / GetGraph(), with three friends -- one per phase. What still launders const here is `Polygons`, a
317: 		 * TArray<TUniquePtr<FProceduralPolygon>>: the same TUniquePtr trick, the same fix, not yet done.
318: 		 */
319: 		const FRoadTriangulationData& Owner;
320: 
321: 		TArray<FIndex2i> Boundary; // ClockWise
322: 		TArray<TArray<FIndex2i>> Holes; // ClockWise
323: 		TArray<int> TrianglesIDs;
324: 		TArray<int> LineVertices;        // ordered boundary vertex list (loop / simple polygons)
325: 		UE::Geometry::FPolygon2d Poly2d; // 2D polygon, built on arrangement completion
326: 		UE::Geometry::FGeometryResult ResultInfo;
327: 
328: 
329: 
330: 	};

383: 		double GetEndOffset() const;

388: 		{
389: 			Inner,
390: 			Outer
391: 		};

398: 		int GetLaneIndex() const { return LaneIndex; }

400: 		TArray<int> InsideLineVertices;
401: 		TArray<int> EndCapVertices;
402: 		TArray<int> OutsideLineVertices;
403: 		TArray<int> BeginCapVertices;

407: 
408: 	private:
409: 		void AddVertexInfo(FRoadVertexTable& Vertices, int VID, bool bLoopSeam, bool bWithUV1) const;
410: 		bool ProcessPolyline(FRoadVertexTable& Vertices, const FLineInfo& LineInfo, TArray<int>& VIDs, bool bLoopSeam);
411: 
412: 		//~ -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
413: 		//~ The three seams of construction. They were one 203-line constructor, which is what a phase marker looks
414: 		//~ like when the language is not asked to express it: "insert before the table is sized" was true only
415: 		//~ because the code sat in a ctor. With FRoadArrangementBuilder saying that instead, the seams that were
416: 		//~ always there can be named -- and exactly one of the three touches the graph.
417: 		//~
418: 		//~ ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING and byte-observable: inside -> outside -> endcap -> begincap -> zone seams ->
419: 		//~ attribute hooks. Any permutation renumbers the vertices and every index derived from them.
420: 
421: 		/** What the lane's shape is, before anything is inserted: the two boundary polylines, both caps, and the
422: 		 *  zone GID. PURE -- it samples the spline and touches neither the graph nor Owner. */
423: 		struct FLaneContour
424: 		{
425: 			TArray<FVector2D> Inside;
426: 			TArray<FVector2D> Outside;
427: 			TArray<FVector2D> BeginCap;
428: 			TArray<FVector2D> EndCap;
429: 			int GID = 0;
430: 		};
431: 		FLaneContour BuildLaneContour(double MaxSquareDistanceFromCap) const;
432: 
433: 		/** THE ONLY PHASE THAT TOUCHES THE OPEN ARRANGEMENT. Sets bIsLoop and the four FLineInfos; returns false
434: 		 *  having named which line failed in ResultInfo. */
435: 		bool InsertLaneContour(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const FLaneContour& Contour);
436: 
437: 		/** Last, on a COMPLETE polygon: the polygon-customization hooks (curb cuts, through the narrow sink), then
438: 		 *  the PRO Polygon attribute's derived fills. */
439: 		void RunLaneAttributeHooks(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder);
440: 
441: 		/** Carve one constrained edge across the lane at each zone boundary (see GetZoneSpans). Ctor-time only,
442: 		 *  and only AFTER the two boundary polylines are final: a boundary whose vertex did not survive into
443: 		 *  them (a sidewalk cap truncates the lane ends) must not get an edge, or its endpoint would be a
444: 		 *  vertex no polygon owns and the build would fail "Mesh is broken". */
445: 		void InsertZoneBoundaryEdges(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& InsideLine2D, const TArray<FVector2D>& OutsideLine2D);
446: 
447: 	private:
448: 		/** Resolved RoadZone-attribute spans over this lane, sorted and contiguous. EMPTY on the (vastly more
449: 		 *  common) lane without the attribute -- every GetRoadZone* override early-outs on that, so the
450: 		 *  no-attribute path costs nothing and stays bit-identical to before the feature.
451: 		 *
452: 		 *  Each entry owns a COPY of the merged zone, whose OverrideMaterial / OverrideDecal are bare
453: 		 *  UObject pointers for GC (FRoadTriangulationData is plain C++ behind a TSharedPtr and is never
454: 		 *  walked by the reference collector -- same situation as FRoadPolygonData::RoadZone, see
455: 		 *  RoadTriangulationData.h). They stay alive because the ORIGINALS live in the cloned
456: 		 *  URoadSplineEditorComponent's lane attribute, which FRoadBuildInputs::Splines roots with a
457: 		 *  TStrongObjectPtr for the whole build.
458: 		 *
459: 		 *  Private, with no accessor: the public GetZoneSpans() had no callers at all. Note that
460: 		 *  LaneProxy.cpp re-derives its own spans through MetaRoad::BuildRoadLaneZoneSpans rather than
461: 		 *  reading these -- that duplication is now cemented, and is worth collapsing separately. */
---

403: 		TArray<int> BeginCapVertices;

405: 		FAxisAlignedBox2d LocalSplineBounds; // X - SOffset, Y - ROffset

491: 		 *  section, so this is the one place that bridges the two. */

477: 	struct METAROADEDITOR_API FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop : public FProceduralPolygon_RoadBase
478: 	{
479: 		FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop(const FRoadTriangulationData& Owner, FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, int SplineIndex, double MaxSquareDistanceFromSpline, double MinSegmentLength);
480: 
481: 		virtual ERoadPolygonType GetType() const override { return ERoadPolygonType::SplineLoop; }
482: 		virtual bool OnCompleteArrangement(FRoadCompletionSink& Sink) override;
483: 		virtual void FillVertexInfo(FRoadVertexInfo& Info, int VID, FVector2d V2d) const override;
484: 		virtual const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>& GetRoadZone() const override;
485: 		virtual FText GetDescription() const override;

488: 	private:
489: 		/** Run Fn for every CLOSED-RING customization key on a section centre line, with the key's ABSOLUTE S
490: 		 *  (Section.SOffset + Key.SOffset). The ring spans the whole spline while the keys are stored per
491: 		 *  section, so this is the one place that bridges the two. */
492: 		void ForEachRingCustomization(TFunctionRef<void(const FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue&, double AbsS)> Fn) const;
493: 
494: 		/** World XY of the ring at S, pushed InwardDistance cm toward the island interior. */
495: 		FVector2D SampleRingInward(double S, double InwardDistance) const;

498: 		void InsertRingCutContours(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& RingPoints2D);

501: 		void RegisterCurbCutSites(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder) const;
502: 
503: 		FLineInfo LineInfo;
504: 
505: 		/** Which R direction points INTO the island: -1 when the sampled ring runs counter-clockwise in XY
506: 		 *  (interior to the LEFT of travel, i.e. decreasing R), +1 when clockwise.
507: 		 *
508: 		 *  Cached in the ctor and never re-derived: BuildClosedRingBoundary forces its own CW winding onto
509: 		 *  Poly2d/LineVertices, and it does so AFTER FillVertexInfo has already run for the boundary vertices —
510: 		 *  so reading the winding back later would be both too late and a different answer. */
511: 		double InwardSignR = -1.0;
512: 
513: 		/** Ring length [cm] (= spline length). S wraps here, so a cut may straddle the seam. */
514: 		double RingLength = 0.0;
515: 	};

458: 		 *
459: 		 *  Private, with no accessor: the public GetZoneSpans() had no callers at all. Note that
460: 		 *  LaneProxy.cpp re-derives its own spans through MetaRoad::BuildRoadLaneZoneSpans rather than
461: 		 *  reading these -- that duplication is now cemented, and is worth collapsing separately. */
462: 		TArray<FRoadLaneZoneSpan> ZoneSpans;
463: 		int SectionIndex;
464: 		int LaneIndex;
465: 		double MaxSquareDistanceFromSpline;
466: 		double MinSegmentLength;
467: 		FLineInfo InsideLineInfo;
468: 		FLineInfo EndCapInfo;
469: 		FLineInfo OutsideLineInfo;
470: 		FLineInfo BeginCapInfo;
471: 		bool bIsLoop;
472: 	};
473: 
474: 	/**
475: 	 * FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop
476: 	 */
477: 	struct METAROADEDITOR_API FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop : public FProceduralPolygon_RoadBase
478: 	{
479: 		FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop(const FRoadTriangulationData& Owner, FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, int SplineIndex, double MaxSquareDistanceFromSpline, double MinSegmentLength);
480: 
481: 		virtual ERoadPolygonType GetType() const override { return ERoadPolygonType::SplineLoop; }
482: 		virtual bool OnCompleteArrangement(FRoadCompletionSink& Sink) override;
483: 		virtual void FillVertexInfo(FRoadVertexInfo& Info, int VID, FVector2d V2d) const override;
484: 		virtual const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>& GetRoadZone() const override;
485: 		virtual FText GetDescription() const override;
486: 		virtual bool SetUVLayers(FDynamicMesh3& Mesh, int TID) const override;
487: 
488: 	private:
489: 		/** Run Fn for every CLOSED-RING customization key on a section centre line, with the key's ABSOLUTE S
490: 		 *  (Section.SOffset + Key.SOffset). The ring spans the whole spline while the keys are stored per
491: 		 *  section, so this is the one place that bridges the two. */
492: 		void ForEachRingCustomization(TFunctionRef<void(const FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue&, double AbsS)> Fn) const;
493: 
494: 		/** World XY of the ring at S, pushed InwardDistance cm toward the island interior. */
495: 		FVector2D SampleRingInward(double S, double InwardDistance) const;
496: 
497: 		/** Carve every ring-cut contour (inner chain + rungs) into the arrangement. Ctor-time only. */
498: 		void InsertRingCutContours(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& RingPoints2D);
499: 
500: 		/** Hand every centre-line curb cut to FRoadTriangulationData::CurbCutSites. Ctor-time only. */
501: 		void RegisterCurbCutSites(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder) const;
502: 
503: 		FLineInfo LineInfo;
504: 
505: 		/** Which R direction points INTO the island: -1 when the sampled ring runs counter-clockwise in XY
506: 		 *  (interior to the LEFT of travel, i.e. decreasing R), +1 when clockwise.
507: 		 *
508: 		 *  Cached in the ctor and never re-derived: BuildClosedRingBoundary forces its own CW winding onto
509: 		 *  Poly2d/LineVertices, and it does so AFTER FillVertexInfo has already run for the boundary vertices —
510: 		 *  so reading the winding back later would be both too late and a different answer. */
511: 		double InwardSignR = -1.0;
512: 
513: 		/** Ring length [cm] (= spline length). S wraps here, so a cut may straddle the seam. */
514: 		double RingLength = 0.0;
515: 	};
516: 
517: 	/**
518: 	 * FProceduralPolygon_Simple
519: 	 */
---
428: 			TArray<FVector2D> EndCap;
429: 			int GID = 0;
430: 		};
431: 		FLaneContour BuildLaneContour(double MaxSquareDistanceFromCap) const;
432: 
433: 		/** THE ONLY PHASE THAT TOUCHES THE OPEN ARRANGEMENT. Sets bIsLoop and the four FLineInfos; returns false
434: 		 *  having named which line failed in ResultInfo. */
435: 		bool InsertLaneContour(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const FLaneContour& Contour);
436: 
437: 		/** Last, on a COMPLETE polygon: the polygon-customization hooks (curb cuts, through the narrow sink), then
438: 		 *  the PRO Polygon attribute's derived fills. */
439: 		void RunLaneAttributeHooks(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder);
440: 
441: 		/** Carve one constrained edge across the lane at each zone boundary (see GetZoneSpans). Ctor-time only,
442: 		 *  and only AFTER the two boundary polylines are final: a boundary whose vertex did not survive into
443: 		 *  them (a sidewalk cap truncates the lane ends) must not get an edge, or its endpoint would be a
444: 		 *  vertex no polygon owns and the build would fail "Mesh is broken". */
445: 		void InsertZoneBoundaryEdges(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& InsideLine2D, const TArray<FVector2D>& OutsideLine2D);
446: 
447: 	private:
448: 		/** Resolved RoadZone-attribute spans over this lane, sorted and contiguous. EMPTY on the (vastly more
449: 		 *  common) lane without the attribute -- every GetRoadZone* override early-outs on that, so the
450: 		 *  no-attribute path costs nothing and stays bit-identical to before the feature.
451: 		 *
452: 		 *  Each entry owns a COPY of the merged zone, whose OverrideMaterial / OverrideDecal are bare
453: 		 *  UObject pointers for GC (FRoadTriangulationData is plain C++ behind a TSharedPtr and is never
454: 		 *  walked by the reference collector -- same situation as FRoadPolygonData::RoadZone, see
455: 		 *  RoadTriangulationData.h). They stay alive because the ORIGINALS live in the cloned
456: 		 *  URoadSplineEditorComponent's lane attribute, which FRoadBuildInputs::Splines roots with a
457: 		 *  TStrongObjectPtr for the whole build.
458: 		 *
459: 		 *  Private, with no accessor: the public GetZoneSpans() had no callers at all. Note that
460: 		 *  LaneProxy.cpp re-derives its own spans through MetaRoad::BuildRoadLaneZoneSpans rather than
461: 		 *  reading these -- that duplication is now cemented, and is worth collapsing separately. */
462: 		TArray<FRoadLaneZoneSpan> ZoneSpans;
463: 		int SectionIndex;
464: 		int LaneIndex;
465: 		double MaxSquareDistanceFromSpline;
466: 		double MinSegmentLength;
467: 		FLineInfo InsideLineInfo;
468: 		FLineInfo EndCapInfo;
469: 		FLineInfo OutsideLineInfo;
470: 		FLineInfo BeginCapInfo;
471: 		bool bIsLoop;
472: 	};
473: 
474: 	/**
475: 	 * FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop

482: 		virtual bool OnCompleteArrangement(FRoadCompletionSink& Sink) override;
483: 		virtual void FillVertexInfo(FRoadVertexInfo& Info, int VID, FVector2d V2d) const override;
484: 		virtual const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>& GetRoadZone() const override;
485: 		virtual FText GetDescription() const override;
486: 		virtual bool SetUVLayers(FDynamicMesh3& Mesh, int TID) const override;
487: 
488: 	private:
489: 		/** Run Fn for every CLOSED-RING customization key on a section centre line, with the key's ABSOLUTE S
490: 		 *  (Section.SOffset + Key.SOffset). The ring spans the whole spline while the keys are stored per
491: 		 *  section, so this is the one place that bridges the two. */
492: 		void ForEachRingCustomization(TFunctionRef<void(const FRoadLaneAttributePolygonCustomizationValue&, double AbsS)> Fn) const;
493: 
494: 		/** World XY of the ring at S, pushed InwardDistance cm toward the island interior. */
495: 		FVector2D SampleRingInward(double S, double InwardDistance) const;
496: 
497: 		/** Carve every ring-cut contour (inner chain + rungs) into the arrangement. Ctor-time only. */
498: 		void InsertRingCutContours(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& RingPoints2D);
499: 
500: 		/** Hand every centre-line curb cut to FRoadTriangulationData::CurbCutSites. Ctor-time only. */
501: 		void RegisterCurbCutSites(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder) const;
502: 
503: 		FLineInfo LineInfo;
504: 
505: 		/** Which R direction points INTO the island: -1 when the sampled ring runs counter-clockwise in XY
506: 		 *  (interior to the LEFT of travel, i.e. decreasing R), +1 when clockwise.
507: 		 *
508: 		 *  Cached in the ctor and never re-derived: BuildClosedRingBoundary forces its own CW winding onto
509: 		 *  Poly2d/LineVertices, and it does so AFTER FillVertexInfo has already run for the boundary vertices —
510: 		 *  so reading the winding back later would be both too late and a different answer. */
511: 		double InwardSignR = -1.0;
512: 
513: 		/** Ring length [cm] (= spline length). S wraps here, so a cut may straddle the seam. */
514: 		double RingLength = 0.0;
515: 	};

536: 		 *  ensure VertexHeights is sized 1:1 with Vertices (>= 2). */

520: 	struct METAROADEDITOR_API FProceduralPolygon_Simple : public FProceduralPolygon
521: 	{
522: 		FProceduralPolygon_Simple(const FRoadTriangulationData& Owner, FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, int SimplePolygonIndex);
523: 
524: 		virtual ERoadPolygonType GetType() const override  { return ERoadPolygonType::Simple; }
525: 		virtual bool OnCompleteArrangement(FRoadCompletionSink& Sink) override;
526: 		virtual void FillVertexInfo(FRoadVertexInfo& Info, int VID, FVector2d V2d) const override;
527: 		virtual const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>& GetRoadZone() const override;
528: 		virtual FText GetDescription() const override;
529: 		virtual bool SetUVLayers(FDynamicMesh3& Mesh, int TID) const override;
530: 		virtual double GetMaterialPriority() const override;
531: 		virtual int GetObjectIndex() const override;
532: 
533: 	protected:
534: 		/** Per-node sidewalk height at a boundary vertex's 2D position: project onto the source ring
535: 		 *  (Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex].Vertices) and lerp the bracketing VertexHeights. Caller must
536: 		 *  ensure VertexHeights is sized 1:1 with Vertices (>= 2). */
537: 		double EvaluateHeightAt2D(const FVector2d& V2d) const;
538: 
539: 		/** Validate and insert every inner contour of this fill, each with its OWN PID so OnCompleteArrangement can
540: 		 *  recover it independently of the boundary. A ring that is degenerate, self-intersecting, not STRICTLY
541: 		 *  inside OuterPoints2D, or overlapping an already-accepted hole is dropped with a warning -- the fill then
542: 		 *  comes out solid there, which is wrong but valid, where letting it through is a hard build failure.
543: 		 *  Ctor-time only, after the boundary insert and before InsertCurbCutContours. */
544: 		void InsertHoleRings(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& OuterPoints2D, int GID);
545: 
546: 		/** Carve every curb cut's inner chain + rungs into the arrangement, and register each cut as a scope-wide
547: 		 *  site. Ctor-time only. */
548: 		void InsertCurbCutContours(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& RingPoints2D);
549: 
550: 		/** Hand one cut to FRoadTriangulationData::CurbCutSites, windowed to the stretch of ring it can reach. */
551: 		void RegisterCurbCutSite(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const FRoadCurbCutPlacement& Placement) const;
552: 
553: 		/** Cumulative arc length of the source ring (Num + 1 entries; last = total). Built in the ctor only when
554: 		 *  the fill actually carries curb cuts — nothing else needs it. */
555: 		TArray<double> RingArcs;
556: 
557: 		/** A magnetized boundary span that MATCHED a real arrangement path at ctor time, so its own (approximate)
558: 		 *  segments were skipped instead of inserted. OnCompleteArrangement tags Path with this polygon's PID and
559: 		 *  FindPolyline then recovers the boundary along the real road-lane geometry.
560: 		 *  A span that does NOT match is never recorded here -- its segments are inserted like any plain fill's, which
561: 		 *  is what keeps every vertex owned. (Skipping a span we cannot tag would strand the fill's other inserted
562: 		 *  vertices with no Infos and fail the whole build at the height pass.) */
563: 		struct FMagneticSpanRec
564: 		{
565: 			int32 VidA = INDEX_NONE;
566: 			int32 VidB = INDEX_NONE;
567: 			FMagneticSpan Span;
568: 			TArray<int> Path; // matched at ctor time; re-validated (and re-matched if a later insert split it) on complete
569: 		};
570: 		TArray<FMagneticSpanRec> MagneticSpanRecs;
571: 
572: 		/** Resolve each span to a real arrangement path, recording the ones that matched into MagneticSpanRecs and
---

553: 		/** Cumulative arc length of the source ring (Num + 1 entries; last = total). Built in the ctor only when
554: 		 *  the fill actually carries curb cuts — nothing else needs it. */
555: 		TArray<double> RingArcs;
556: 
557: 		/** A magnetized boundary span that MATCHED a real arrangement path at ctor time, so its own (approximate)
558: 		 *  segments were skipped instead of inserted. OnCompleteArrangement tags Path with this polygon's PID and
559: 		 *  FindPolyline then recovers the boundary along the real road-lane geometry.
560: 		 *  A span that does NOT match is never recorded here -- its segments are inserted like any plain fill's, which
561: 		 *  is what keeps every vertex owned. (Skipping a span we cannot tag would strand the fill's other inserted
562: 		 *  vertices with no Infos and fail the whole build at the height pass.) */
563: 		struct FMagneticSpanRec
564: 		{
565: 			int32 VidA = INDEX_NONE;
566: 			int32 VidB = INDEX_NONE;
567: 			FMagneticSpan Span;
568: 			TArray<int> Path; // matched at ctor time; re-validated (and re-matched if a later insert split it) on complete
569: 		};
570: 		TArray<FMagneticSpanRec> MagneticSpanRecs;
571: 
572: 		/** Resolve each span to a real arrangement path, recording the ones that matched into MagneticSpanRecs and
573: 		 *  flagging their segments in OutSkipSeg (indexed like Points2D) so the caller inserts everything else. */
574: 		//~ Takes no builder: matching only READS the arrangement (FindPinnedVertex / MatchPolyline), and reads go
575: 		//~ through the const Owner like every other read.
576: 		void MatchMagneticSpans(const TArray<FVector2D>& Points2D, const TArray<FMagneticSpan>& Spans, TBitArray<>& OutSkipSeg);
577: 
578: 		FLineInfo LineInfo;
579: 
580: 		/** One FLineInfo per hole ring that PASSED validation and was inserted, in insertion order. NOT 1:1 with
581: 		 *  Owner.SurfacePolygons[i].Holes -- a rejected hole gets no entry, so index arithmetic between the two is
582: 		 *  invalid by construction. */
583: 		TArray<FLineInfo> HoleLineInfos;
584: 
585: 		/** The cleaned 2D hole rings, parallel to HoleLineInfos. Kept from the ctor because InsertCurbCutContours
586: 		 *  must reject a cut contour that reaches INTO a hole: such a point is a graph vertex this fill will not
587: 		 *  claim, i.e. a vertex no polygon owns, which hard-fails the height pass with "Mesh is broken". */
588: 		TArray<TArray<FVector2D>> HolePoints2D;
589: 
590: 		int SimplePolygonIndex;
591: 		GeomUtils::FPolygonIntersectApproximation IntersectApproximation;
592: 	};
593: 
594: 	/**
595: 	 * FProceduralPolygon_FeatureLine — a FRoadFeatureLine turned into an EXACT arrangement VID chain (published to
596: 	 * FeatureLineWeldedPaths) that the sweep ops walk so the mark/curb hugs the mesh edges. Two ways in, one recovery:
597: 	 *   - HugMode == Weld:      INSERT the line as CenterLine-GID constraint edges (splitting whatever it crosses).
598: 	 *   - HugMode == Magnetize: INSERT NOTHING -- pin the endpoints, MatchPolyline the line's approximation onto the
599: 	 *                           real geometry underneath it, and TAG that existing-edge path (RevalidateAndTagPath) at
600: 	 *                           completion. Open lines only (RoadBaseOp gates closed magnetize out -> drape).
601: 	 * Both then recover the chain via FindPolyline over LineInfo.PID.
602: 	 *
603: 	 * An OPEN polyline: it builds an open Boundary (first.A != last.B) so IsPolyline()==true -> skipped by
604: 	 * FindBoundaries (its GID is CenterLine) and by the triangle-claim loop; it never fills. Fills Infos in
605: 	 * OnCompleteArrangement by projecting each arrangement vertex onto the line's own 3D vertices (Z + optional
606: 	 * per-vertex height), enough to satisfy the >=1-Infos invariant. Its RoadZone is INVALID on purpose (treated as a
607: 	 * decal), so it does NOT influence surface Z wherever a real lane/loop/simple vertex coexists -- no crease under
608: 	 * the marking. (Magnetize tags EXISTING lane vertices, which already own a real surface Info, so the added decal
609: 	 * Info is inert there.)
610: 	 */
611: 	struct METAROADEDITOR_API FProceduralPolygon_FeatureLine : public FProceduralPolygon
612: 	{
613: 		FProceduralPolygon_FeatureLine(const FRoadTriangulationData& Owner, FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, int FeatureLineIndex);
614: 
615: 		virtual ERoadPolygonType GetType() const override { return ERoadPolygonType::FeatureLine; }
616: 		virtual bool OnCompleteArrangement(FRoadCompletionSink& Sink) override;
617: 		virtual void FillVertexInfo(FRoadVertexInfo& Info, int VID, FVector2d V2d) const override;
618: 		virtual const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>& GetRoadZone() const override;
619: 		virtual FText GetDescription() const override;
620: 		virtual bool SetUVLayers(FDynamicMesh3& Mesh, int TID) const override { return false; } // never fills -> never UV-mapped
621: 		virtual double GetMaterialPriority() const override { return 0.0; }
622: 		virtual int GetObjectIndex() const override;
623: 
---
528: 		virtual FText GetDescription() const override;
529: 		virtual bool SetUVLayers(FDynamicMesh3& Mesh, int TID) const override;
530: 		virtual double GetMaterialPriority() const override;
531: 		virtual int GetObjectIndex() const override;
532: 
533: 	protected:
534: 		/** Per-node sidewalk height at a boundary vertex's 2D position: project onto the source ring
535: 		 *  (Owner.SurfacePolygons[SimplePolygonIndex].Vertices) and lerp the bracketing VertexHeights. Caller must
536: 		 *  ensure VertexHeights is sized 1:1 with Vertices (>= 2). */
537: 		double EvaluateHeightAt2D(const FVector2d& V2d) const;
538: 
539: 		/** Validate and insert every inner contour of this fill, each with its OWN PID so OnCompleteArrangement can
540: 		 *  recover it independently of the boundary. A ring that is degenerate, self-intersecting, not STRICTLY
541: 		 *  inside OuterPoints2D, or overlapping an already-accepted hole is dropped with a warning -- the fill then
542: 		 *  comes out solid there, which is wrong but valid, where letting it through is a hard build failure.
543: 		 *  Ctor-time only, after the boundary insert and before InsertCurbCutContours. */
544: 		void InsertHoleRings(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& OuterPoints2D, int GID);
545: 
546: 		/** Carve every curb cut's inner chain + rungs into the arrangement, and register each cut as a scope-wide
547: 		 *  site. Ctor-time only. */
548: 		void InsertCurbCutContours(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const TArray<FVector2D>& RingPoints2D);
549: 
550: 		/** Hand one cut to FRoadTriangulationData::CurbCutSites, windowed to the stretch of ring it can reach. */
551: 		void RegisterCurbCutSite(FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, const FRoadCurbCutPlacement& Placement) const;
552: 
553: 		/** Cumulative arc length of the source ring (Num + 1 entries; last = total). Built in the ctor only when
554: 		 *  the fill actually carries curb cuts — nothing else needs it. */
555: 		TArray<double> RingArcs;
556: 
557: 		/** A magnetized boundary span that MATCHED a real arrangement path at ctor time, so its own (approximate)
558: 		 *  segments were skipped instead of inserted. OnCompleteArrangement tags Path with this polygon's PID and
559: 		 *  FindPolyline then recovers the boundary along the real road-lane geometry.
560: 		 *  A span that does NOT match is never recorded here -- its segments are inserted like any plain fill's, which
561: 		 *  is what keeps every vertex owned. (Skipping a span we cannot tag would strand the fill's other inserted
562: 		 *  vertices with no Infos and fail the whole build at the height pass.) */
563: 		struct FMagneticSpanRec
564: 		{
565: 			int32 VidA = INDEX_NONE;
566: 			int32 VidB = INDEX_NONE;
567: 			FMagneticSpan Span;
568: 			TArray<int> Path; // matched at ctor time; re-validated (and re-matched if a later insert split it) on complete
569: 		};
570: 		TArray<FMagneticSpanRec> MagneticSpanRecs;
571: 
572: 		/** Resolve each span to a real arrangement path, recording the ones that matched into MagneticSpanRecs and
573: 		 *  flagging their segments in OutSkipSeg (indexed like Points2D) so the caller inserts everything else. */
574: 		//~ Takes no builder: matching only READS the arrangement (FindPinnedVertex / MatchPolyline), and reads go
575: 		//~ through the const Owner like every other read.
576: 		void MatchMagneticSpans(const TArray<FVector2D>& Points2D, const TArray<FMagneticSpan>& Spans, TBitArray<>& OutSkipSeg);
577: 
578: 		FLineInfo LineInfo;
579: 
580: 		/** One FLineInfo per hole ring that PASSED validation and was inserted, in insertion order. NOT 1:1 with
581: 		 *  Owner.SurfacePolygons[i].Holes -- a rejected hole gets no entry, so index arithmetic between the two is
582: 		 *  invalid by construction. */
583: 		TArray<FLineInfo> HoleLineInfos;
584: 
585: 		/** The cleaned 2D hole rings, parallel to HoleLineInfos. Kept from the ctor because InsertCurbCutContours
586: 		 *  must reject a cut contour that reaches INTO a hole: such a point is a graph vertex this fill will not
587: 		 *  claim, i.e. a vertex no polygon owns, which hard-fails the height pass with "Mesh is broken". */
588: 		TArray<TArray<FVector2D>> HolePoints2D;
589: 
590: 		int SimplePolygonIndex;
591: 		GeomUtils::FPolygonIntersectApproximation IntersectApproximation;
592: 	};
593: 
594: 	/**
595: 	 * FProceduralPolygon_FeatureLine — a FRoadFeatureLine turned into an EXACT arrangement VID chain (published to
596: 	 * FeatureLineWeldedPaths) that the sweep ops walk so the mark/curb hugs the mesh edges. Two ways in, one recovery:
597: 	 *   - HugMode == Weld:      INSERT the line as CenterLine-GID constraint edges (splitting whatever it crosses).
598: 	 *   - HugMode == Magnetize: INSERT NOTHING -- pin the endpoints, MatchPolyline the line's approximation onto the
599: 	 *                           real geometry underneath it, and TAG that existing-edge path (RevalidateAndTagPath) at
600: 	 *                           completion. Open lines only (RoadBaseOp gates closed magnetize out -> drape).
601: 	 * Both then recover the chain via FindPolyline over LineInfo.PID.
602: 	 *
603: 	 * An OPEN polyline: it builds an open Boundary (first.A != last.B) so IsPolyline()==true -> skipped by
604: 	 * FindBoundaries (its GID is CenterLine) and by the triangle-claim loop; it never fills. Fills Infos in
605: 	 * OnCompleteArrangement by projecting each arrangement vertex onto the line's own 3D vertices (Z + optional
606: 	 * per-vertex height), enough to satisfy the >=1-Infos invariant. Its RoadZone is INVALID on purpose (treated as a
607: 	 * decal), so it does NOT influence surface Z wherever a real lane/loop/simple vertex coexists -- no crease under
608: 	 * the marking. (Magnetize tags EXISTING lane vertices, which already own a real surface Info, so the added decal
609: 	 * Info is inert there.)
610: 	 */

616: 		virtual bool OnCompleteArrangement(FRoadCompletionSink& Sink) override;

623: 
624: 	private:
625: 		/** Z at a 2D point ON the line: project onto Points2D, lerp bracketing PointsZ. Every inserted/crossing
626: 		 *  vertex is collinear on some segment, so the projection is exact. */
627: 		double EvaluateZAt2D(const FVector2d& V2d) const;
628: 
629: 		int FeatureLineIndex;
630: 		bool bClosedLine = false;   // a closed contour (e.g. the MarkProfile border of a box junction / island)
631: 		FLineInfo LineInfo;
---
565: 			int32 VidA = INDEX_NONE;
566: 			int32 VidB = INDEX_NONE;
567: 			FMagneticSpan Span;
568: 			TArray<int> Path; // matched at ctor time; re-validated (and re-matched if a later insert split it) on complete
569: 		};
570: 		TArray<FMagneticSpanRec> MagneticSpanRecs;
571: 
572: 		/** Resolve each span to a real arrangement path, recording the ones that matched into MagneticSpanRecs and
573: 		 *  flagging their segments in OutSkipSeg (indexed like Points2D) so the caller inserts everything else. */
574: 		//~ Takes no builder: matching only READS the arrangement (FindPinnedVertex / MatchPolyline), and reads go
575: 		//~ through the const Owner like every other read.
576: 		void MatchMagneticSpans(const TArray<FVector2D>& Points2D, const TArray<FMagneticSpan>& Spans, TBitArray<>& OutSkipSeg);
577: 
578: 		FLineInfo LineInfo;
579: 
580: 		/** One FLineInfo per hole ring that PASSED validation and was inserted, in insertion order. NOT 1:1 with
581: 		 *  Owner.SurfacePolygons[i].Holes -- a rejected hole gets no entry, so index arithmetic between the two is
582: 		 *  invalid by construction. */
583: 		TArray<FLineInfo> HoleLineInfos;
584: 
585: 		/** The cleaned 2D hole rings, parallel to HoleLineInfos. Kept from the ctor because InsertCurbCutContours
586: 		 *  must reject a cut contour that reaches INTO a hole: such a point is a graph vertex this fill will not
587: 		 *  claim, i.e. a vertex no polygon owns, which hard-fails the height pass with "Mesh is broken". */
588: 		TArray<TArray<FVector2D>> HolePoints2D;
589: 
590: 		int SimplePolygonIndex;
591: 		GeomUtils::FPolygonIntersectApproximation IntersectApproximation;
592: 	};
593: 
594: 	/**
595: 	 * FProceduralPolygon_FeatureLine — a FRoadFeatureLine turned into an EXACT arrangement VID chain (published to
596: 	 * FeatureLineWeldedPaths) that the sweep ops walk so the mark/curb hugs the mesh edges. Two ways in, one recovery:
597: 	 *   - HugMode == Weld:      INSERT the line as CenterLine-GID constraint edges (splitting whatever it crosses).
598: 	 *   - HugMode == Magnetize: INSERT NOTHING -- pin the endpoints, MatchPolyline the line's approximation onto the
599: 	 *                           real geometry underneath it, and TAG that existing-edge path (RevalidateAndTagPath) at
600: 	 *                           completion. Open lines only (RoadBaseOp gates closed magnetize out -> drape).
601: 	 * Both then recover the chain via FindPolyline over LineInfo.PID.
602: 	 *
603: 	 * An OPEN polyline: it builds an open Boundary (first.A != last.B) so IsPolyline()==true -> skipped by
604: 	 * FindBoundaries (its GID is CenterLine) and by the triangle-claim loop; it never fills. Fills Infos in
605: 	 * OnCompleteArrangement by projecting each arrangement vertex onto the line's own 3D vertices (Z + optional
606: 	 * per-vertex height), enough to satisfy the >=1-Infos invariant. Its RoadZone is INVALID on purpose (treated as a
607: 	 * decal), so it does NOT influence surface Z wherever a real lane/loop/simple vertex coexists -- no crease under
608: 	 * the marking. (Magnetize tags EXISTING lane vertices, which already own a real surface Info, so the added decal
609: 	 * Info is inert there.)
610: 	 */
611: 	struct METAROADEDITOR_API FProceduralPolygon_FeatureLine : public FProceduralPolygon
612: 	{
613: 		FProceduralPolygon_FeatureLine(const FRoadTriangulationData& Owner, FRoadArrangementBuilder& Builder, int FeatureLineIndex);
614: 
615: 		virtual ERoadPolygonType GetType() const override { return ERoadPolygonType::FeatureLine; }
616: 		virtual bool OnCompleteArrangement(FRoadCompletionSink& Sink) override;
617: 		virtual void FillVertexInfo(FRoadVertexInfo& Info, int VID, FVector2d V2d) const override;
618: 		virtual const TInstancedStruct<FRoadZone>& GetRoadZone() const override;
619: 		virtual FText GetDescription() const override;
620: 		virtual bool SetUVLayers(FDynamicMesh3& Mesh, int TID) const override { return false; } // never fills -> never UV-mapped
621: 		virtual double GetMaterialPriority() const override { return 0.0; }
622: 		virtual int GetObjectIndex() const override;
623: 
624: 	private:
625: 		/** Z at a 2D point ON the line: project onto Points2D, lerp bracketing PointsZ. Every inserted/crossing
626: 		 *  vertex is collinear on some segment, so the projection is exact. */
627: 		double EvaluateZAt2D(const FVector2d& V2d) const;
628: 
629: 		int FeatureLineIndex;
630: 		bool bClosedLine = false;   // a closed contour (e.g. the MarkProfile border of a box junction / island)
631: 		FLineInfo LineInfo;
632: 		TArray<FVector2D> Points2D; // XY of the line's own vertices (closing duplicate stripped for closed lines)
633: 		TArray<double>    PointsZ;  // Location.Z (+ VertexHeights if present), 1:1 with Points2D
634: 
635: 		// Magnetize path only (HugMode == Magnetize): the line inserts nothing; instead its endpoints are pinned and its
636: 		// approximation is matched onto existing edges at ctor time, then tagged in OnCompleteArrangement.
637: 		bool bMagnetize = false;
638: 		int32 VidA = INDEX_NONE;    // pinned endpoint vertices (the matched path's ends)
639: 		int32 VidB = INDEX_NONE;
640: 		TArray<int> MatchedPath;    // VID path matched at ctor; re-validated + tagged on complete (see RevalidateAndTagPath)
641: 	};
642: 
643: 	namespace RoadPolygonUtils
644: 	{
645: 		TArray<FRoadPosition> MakePolyline(TConstArrayView<FArrangementVertex3d> Vertexes, const TArray<int>& VerticesIDs, const FProceduralPolygon* PolyFilter);
646: 	}
647: 
648: 
649: 
650: } // MetaRoad

=== Source\MetaRoadEditor\Public\RoadMeshBuild\RoadArrangementBuilder.h ===
1: /*
2:  * Copyright (c) 2026 Ivan Zhukov. All Rights Reserved.
3:  * Email: ivzhuk7@gmail.com
4:  */
5: 
6: #pragma once
7: 
8: #include "CoreMinimal.h"
9: #include "RoadMeshBuild/ProceduralPolygon.h" // FLineInfo, IRoadConstraintSink
10: #include "RoadMeshBuild/ArrangementMatch.h"  // ArrangementMatch::FParams (FRoadCompletionSink::TagMatchedPath)
11: 
12: struct FRoadPolygonData;
13: 
14: namespace MetaRoad
15: {
16: 	struct FRoadTriangulationData;
17: 	struct FRoadCurbCutSite;
18: 	class FRoadVertexTable;
19: 
20: /**
21:  * FRoadArrangementBuilder -- the OPEN phase of the build, expressed as a lifetime.
22:  *
23:  * WHAT IT REPLACES. The rule "everything is inserted before Vertices3d is sized" was expressed by the fact
24:  * that the code lived in a polygon CONSTRUCTOR -- which is how a 203-line constructor came about: the
25:  * constructor was standing in for a phase marker. It could not be moved out, because a polygon reached the
26:  * arrangement through a public non-const reference to the whole build (FProceduralPolygon::Owner), and there
27:  * was nowhere else for the write to go.
28:  *
29:  * Now there is. Inserting requires holding a builder; a builder exists only inside the arrange stages of
30:  * FRoadTriangulationOp::CalculateResult, as a local in a lexical scope; and Close() -- which is what sizes the
31:  * vertex table -- ends it. After Close() there is nowhere to insert: the restriction is not documented, it is
32:  * inexpressible. That is what lets FProceduralPolygon::Owner finally be `const`.
33:  *
34:  * A polygon receives the builder as a PARAMETER, never as a member, so it cannot insert after its own
35:  * construction has finished.
36:  *
37:  * The reach-through this type could not originally close -- FRoadTriangulationData::Arrangement was a public
38:  * TUniquePtr, whose const operator-> hands back a NON-const FArrangement2d* -- is closed now: the field is
39:  * private and this class is one of the three friends allowed to write it, one per phase. See the friend list at
40:  * the bottom of FRoadTriangulationData for what that makes impossible.
41:  */
42: class METAROADEDITOR_API FRoadArrangementBuilder : public IRoadConstraintSink
43: {
44: public:
45: 	/**
46: 	 * CREATES the arrangement (bounds hint from Data.Bounds, which Initialize has finished by now) and sets
47: 	 * VertexSnapTol -- both moved verbatim out of BuildStages::Initialize, where they ran because no phase type
48: 	 * existed yet. The lifetime is total now: before this constructor there is nothing to write to, and after
49: 	 * Close() there is nowhere to write.
50: 	 *
51: 	 * VertexSnapTol arrives as an argument rather than being poked into the field afterwards, because every
52: 	 * later use of it is a read.
53: 	 */
54: 	FRoadArrangementBuilder(FRoadTriangulationData& InData, double VertexSnapTol);
55: 
56: 	/** Closes the phase if a stage bailed out before doing so. Close() is idempotent, so the explicit call at
57: 	 *  the end of the arrange scope stays the one that reads as intentional. */
58: 	virtual ~FRoadArrangementBuilder();
59: 
60: 	FRoadArrangementBuilder(const FRoadArrangementBuilder&) = delete;
61: 	FRoadArrangementBuilder& operator=(const FRoadArrangementBuilder&) = delete;
62: 
63: 	//~ ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
64: 	//~ The three insertion primitives. Moved here verbatim from FProceduralPolygon, the sample-digest feed
65: 	//~ included -- that feed is part of the build fingerprint's `samples` column, so it must not drift.
66: 
67: 	/** Insert an OPEN polyline (segments [i,i+1]); returns {PID, VID_A=first, VID_B=last} (VID_B=-1 for a 2-point loop). */
68: 	FLineInfo InsertPolyline(const TArray<FVector2D>& Points, int GID);
69: 
70: 	/** Insert a CLOSED ring (segments [i,(i+1)%N], so the wrap is included); returns a loop FLineInfo (VID_A==VID_B).
71: 	 *  Optional SkipSeg (indexed like Points) omits segments whose start index is set -- used by a magnetic fill whose
72: 	 *  matched spans are tagged later instead of inserted. */
73: 	FLineInfo InsertClosedRing(const TArray<FVector2D>& Points, int GID, const TBitArray<>* SkipSeg = nullptr);
74: 
75: 	/** One untracked constrained edge (GID = Other, PID = -1) -- the "rung" idiom, and the whole of
76: 	 *  IRoadConstraintSink. A TRACKED edge would get a PID, and FindPolyline recovers a polygon's boundary by
77: 	 *  walking PID chains, so a hook that could insert one would silently divert its host's boundary. */
78: 	virtual void AddConstraintEdge(const FVector2D& A, const FVector2D& B) override;
79: 
80: 	//~ ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
81: 	//~ Two named exceptions. Neither is equivalent to a primitive above, and both are named here rather than
82: 	//~ left as a raw reach-through so that the polygon layer's contact with the arrangement stays countable.
83: 
84: 	/**
85: 	 * A ring inserted as N-1 segments with NO wrap, reported as a loop unconditionally (VID_A == VID_B).
86: 	 * FProceduralPolygon_RoadLoop's only insert.
87: 	 *
88: 	 * NOT InsertPolyline / InsertClosedRing, and deliberately not collapsed into either:
89: 	 *   - the loop runs `i < Num-1` (i.e. it assumes the last point DUPLICATES the first) while the ring's own
90: 	 *     winding computation twenty lines earlier runs `(i + 1) % Num` (i.e. it assumes the opposite). Both
91: 	 *     cannot be right. If the point IS duplicated this is exactly InsertPolyline; if it is not, this misses
92: 	 *     the closing segment while still reporting IsLoop(). Establishing which is a separate job.
93: 	 *   - InsertClosedRing is wrong either way: its %-wrap would add a degenerate segment to a duplicated-point
94: 	 *     array.
95: 	 *   - it does NOT feed the sample digest, so collapsing it would move the fingerprint's `samples` column on
96: 	 *     every road carrying a looped zone -- i.e. destroy the very instrument that would check the collapse.
97: 	 */
---
114: 	 * THIS IS THE ONLY WAY TO ADD A VERTEX TO A SEAM. Two polygons share a boundary by sharing its arrangement
115: 	 * edges, so a vertex that both sides must see has to come from splitting one of those edges: SplitEdge copies
116: 	 * the GID and the PID onto both halves, so every chain recovered through that PID picks the new vertex up, on
117: 	 * both sides, by construction.
118: 	 *
119: 	 * Inserting a near-duplicate instead is the failure this exists to prevent, and it is not hypothetical: a
120: 	 * magnetized fill deliberately inserts NOTHING along a matched span (its own sampled ring is only an
121: 	 * approximation of the real edge), so laying that approximation back over the seam crosses it, splits it at
122: 	 * arbitrary points and leaves each side with vertices the other does not have. `Radius` therefore wants to be
123: 	 * a few centimetres â€” enough to cover an approximation error, small enough not to reach a different edge.
124: 	 *
125: 	 * A projection landing on an existing vertex returns that vertex and splits nothing: SplitEdgeAtPoint writes
126: 	 * the position blindly, and FDelaunay2 runs with bAutomaticallyFixEdgesToDuplicateVertices = false, so a
127: 	 * coincident pair costs a dropped constraint and a leaking winding fill.
128: 	 */
129: 	bool SplitConstraintNear(const FVector2D& Near, double Radius, FVector2D& OutPoint);
130: 
131: 	//~ ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
132: 
133: 	/**
134: 	 * Surface polygons a lane's PRO Polygon attribute derives while its constructor runs.
135: 	 *
136: 	 * The one mutation of the open phase that is not an arrangement write, and it belongs to the same disease:
137: 	 * the build's working list of fills GROWS during construction. It is here so that FProceduralPolygon::Owner
138: 	 * can be const, and so the growth is visible as a phase operation rather than as `Owner.SurfacePolygons.Append`
139: 	 * buried in a polygon. Order is load-bearing -- gathered fills first, then derived ones in lane order, which
140: 	 * is what fixes FProceduralPolygon_Simple's index and GetObjectIndex().
141: 	 */
142: 	void AddSurfacePolygons(TArray<FRoadPolygonData>&& Fills);
143: 
144: 	/**
145: 	 * Register a curb cut as a SCOPE-WIDE site (FRoadTriangulationData::CurbCutSites).
146: 	 *
147: 	 * Here, and not on the completion sink, because the ring a cut is anchored to is the polygon's own authored
148: 	 * geometry â€” known while it inserts, and the same numbers it carves its contour from. The site is pure data;
149: 	 * nothing reads it until BuildStages::ApplyCurbCuts, long after this phase has closed. Invalid sites (a
150: 	 * degenerate window, zero Depth) are dropped here so the consumer has no error case.
151: 	 */
152: 	void AddCurbCutSite(FRoadCurbCutSite&& Site);
153: 
154: 	/** Split constrained edges longer than MaxEdgeLength (MetaRoad::BoundaryConditioner). THE LAST OPERATION
155: 	 *  THAT MAY CHANGE AN EDGE -- which is precisely why it is a method here and not a free call: it has to
156: 	 *  happen while the phase is still open. Returns the number of vertices inserted. */
157: 	int32 ConditionBoundaries(double MaxEdgeLength, int32 MaxNewVertices);
158: 
159: 	/**
160: 	 * End the open phase: size Vertices3d from the graph (FRoadVertexTable::SyncToGraph).
161: 	 *
162: 	 * THE HUG CONTRACT is established by this call -- index i of the table is the 3D lift of graph vertex i --
163: 	 * and it is why the phase has to end somewhere nameable. Idempotent.
164: 	 */
165: 	void Close();
166: 
167: 	/** For the arrange stages' own diagnostics; a polygon reads the arrangement through its const Owner. */
168: 	const FRoadTriangulationData& GetData() const { return Data; }
169: 
170: private:
171: 	FRoadTriangulationData& Data;
172: 	bool bClosed = false;
173: };
174: 
175: /**
176:  * FRoadCompletionSink -- what may still be written once the graph is final.
177:  *
178:  * The symmetric counterpart of FRoadArrangementBuilder: that one is the open phase, this is what is left after
179:  * Close(). A polygon's OnCompleteArrangement recovers its boundary from the now-final graph and lifts the
180:  * vertices it owns into 3D; those are writes, and with a const Owner they need a channel. Reads of the graph
181:  * need none -- they go through Owner like everything else.
182:  *
183:  * Deliberately a pair of references rather than a class: it grants exactly two things and hides nothing, so
184:  * there is no behaviour for it to own.
185:  */
186: struct METAROADEDITOR_API FRoadCompletionSink
187: {
188: 	explicit FRoadCompletionSink(FRoadTriangulationData& InData);
189: 
190: 	/** The 3D lift, sized by FRoadArrangementBuilder::Close(). Written through FProceduralPolygon::AddVertexInfo. */
191: 	FRoadVertexTable& Vertices;
192: 
193: 	/** Indexed 1:1 with FRoadBuildInputs::FeatureLines; only FProceduralPolygon_FeatureLine publishes into it. */
194: 	TArray<TArray<int32>>& FeatureLinePaths;
195: 
196: 	/**
197: 	 * Re-validate a construction-time VID path against the final graph and stamp its edges with PID
198: 	 * (ArrangementMatch::RevalidateAndTagPath). THE COMPLETION PHASE'S ONLY WRITE TO THE ARRANGEMENT.
199: 	 *
200: 	 * A NAMED OPERATION rather than an FArrangement2d& member, because the postcondition IS the licence: it adds
201: 	 * no vertex and no edge -- AppendEdgePolylinesID only stamps an edge that already exists -- and that is what
202: 	 * keeps Vertices3d, sized by Close() one phase earlier, exactly right. Asserted across the call rather than
203: 	 * believed. Handing out a mutable arrangement here would have granted SplitEdgeAtPoint as well, and a split
204: 	 * in this phase silently invalidates the very chains the surrounding loop is recovering.
205: 	 */
206: 	bool TagMatchedPath(TArrayView<const FVector2d> Approx, const ArrangementMatch::FParams& Params,
207: 	                    TArray<int>& InOutPath, int32 PID);
208: 
209: private:
210: 	FRoadTriangulationData& Data;
211: };
212: 
213: } // namespace MetaRoad
---

126: 	 * the position blindly, and FDelaunay2 runs with bAutomaticallyFixEdgesToDuplicateVertices = false, so a
127: 	 * coincident pair costs a dropped constraint and a leaking winding fill.
128: 	 */

164: 	 */
165: 	void Close();
166: 
167: 	/** For the arrange stages' own diagnostics; a polygon reads the arrangement through its const Owner. */
168: 	const FRoadTriangulationData& GetData() const { return Data; }
169: 
170: private:
171: 	FRoadTriangulationData& Data;
172: 	bool bClosed = false;
173: };

175: /**
176:  * FRoadCompletionSink -- what may still be written once the graph is final.
177:  *
178:  * The symmetric counterpart of FRoadArrangementBuilder: that one is the open phase, this is what is left after
179:  * Close(). A polygon's OnCompleteArrangement recovers its boundary from the now-final graph and lifts the
180:  * vertices it owns into 3D; those are writes, and with a const Owner they need a channel. Reads of the graph
181:  * need none -- they go through Owner like everything else.
182:  *
183:  * Deliberately a pair of references rather than a class: it grants exactly two things and hides nothing, so
184:  * there is no behaviour for it to own.
185:  */
186: struct METAROADEDITOR_API FRoadCompletionSink
187: {
188: 	explicit FRoadCompletionSink(FRoadTriangulationData& InData);
189: 
190: 	/** The 3D lift, sized by FRoadArrangementBuilder::Close(). Written through FProceduralPolygon::AddVertexInfo. */
191: 	FRoadVertexTable& Vertices;
192: 
193: 	/** Indexed 1:1 with FRoadBuildInputs::FeatureLines; only FProceduralPolygon_FeatureLine publishes into it. */
194: 	TArray<TArray<int32>>& FeatureLinePaths;
195: 
196: 	/**
197: 	 * Re-validate a construction-time VID path against the final graph and stamp its edges with PID
198: 	 * (ArrangementMatch::RevalidateAndTagPath). THE COMPLETION PHASE'S ONLY WRITE TO THE ARRANGEMENT.
199: 	 *
200: 	 * A NAMED OPERATION rather than an FArrangement2d& member, because the postcondition IS the licence: it adds
201: 	 * no vertex and no edge -- AppendEdgePolylinesID only stamps an edge that already exists -- and that is what
202: 	 * keeps Vertices3d, sized by Close() one phase earlier, exactly right. Asserted across the call rather than
203: 	 * believed. Handing out a mutable arrangement here would have granted SplitEdgeAtPoint as well, and a split
204: 	 * in this phase silently invalidates the very chains the surrounding loop is recovering.
205: 	 */
206: 	bool TagMatchedPath(TArrayView<const FVector2d> Approx, const ArrangementMatch::FParams& Params,
207: 	                    TArray<int>& InOutPath, int32 PID);
208: 
209: private:
210: 	FRoadTriangulationData& Data;
211: };
212: 
213: } // namespace MetaRoad
---
132: 
133: 	/**
134: 	 * Surface polygons a lane's PRO Polygon attribute derives while its constructor runs.
135: 	 *
136: 	 * The one mutation of the open phase that is not an arrangement write, and it belongs to the same disease:
137: 	 * the build's working list of fills GROWS during construction. It is here so that FProceduralPolygon::Owner
138: 	 * can be const, and so the growth is visible as a phase operation rather than as `Owner.SurfacePolygons.Append`
139: 	 * buried in a polygon. Order is load-bearing -- gathered fills first, then derived ones in lane order, which
140: 	 * is what fixes FProceduralPolygon_Simple's index and GetObjectIndex().
141: 	 */
142: 	void AddSurfacePolygons(TArray<FRoadPolygonData>&& Fills);
143: 
144: 	/**
145: 	 * Register a curb cut as a SCOPE-WIDE site (FRoadTriangulationData::CurbCutSites).
146: 	 *
147: 	 * Here, and not on the completion sink, because the ring a cut is anchored to is the polygon's own authored
148: 	 * geometry â€” known while it inserts, and the same numbers it carves its contour from. The site is pure data;
149: 	 * nothing reads it until BuildStages::ApplyCurbCuts, long after this phase has closed. Invalid sites (a
150: 	 * degenerate window, zero Depth) are dropped here so the consumer has no error case.
151: 	 */
152: 	void AddCurbCutSite(FRoadCurbCutSite&& Site);
153: 
154: 	/** Split constrained edges longer than MaxEdgeLength (MetaRoad::BoundaryConditioner). THE LAST OPERATION
155: 	 *  THAT MAY CHANGE AN EDGE -- which is precisely why it is a method here and not a free call: it has to
156: 	 *  happen while the phase is still open. Returns the number of vertices inserted. */
157: 	int32 ConditionBoundaries(double MaxEdgeLength, int32 MaxNewVertices);
158: 
159: 	/**
160: 	 * End the open phase: size Vertices3d from the graph (FRoadVertexTable::SyncToGraph).
161: 	 *
162: 	 * THE HUG CONTRACT is established by this call -- index i of the table is the 3D lift of graph vertex i --
163: 	 * and it is why the phase has to end somewhere nameable. Idempotent.
164: 	 */
165: 	void Close();
166: 
167: 	/** For the arrange stages' own diagnostics; a polygon reads the arrangement through its const Owner. */
168: 	const FRoadTriangulationData& GetData() const { return Data; }
169: 
170: private:
171: 	FRoadTriangulationData& Data;
172: 	bool bClosed = false;
173: };
174: 
175: /**
176:  * FRoadCompletionSink -- what may still be written once the graph is final.
177:  *
178:  * The symmetric counterpart of FRoadArrangementBuilder: that one is the open phase, this is what is left after
179:  * Close(). A polygon's OnCompleteArrangement recovers its boundary from the now-final graph and lifts the
180:  * vertices it owns into 3D; those are writes, and with a const Owner they need a channel. Reads of the graph
181:  * need none -- they go through Owner like everything else.
182:  *
183:  * Deliberately a pair of references rather than a class: it grants exactly two things and hides nothing, so
184:  * there is no behaviour for it to own.
185:  */

191: 	FRoadVertexTable& Vertices;

194: 	TArray<TArray<int32>>& FeatureLinePaths;

199: 	 *
200: 	 * A NAMED OPERATION rather than an FArrangement2d& member, because the postcondition IS the licence: it adds
201: 	 * no vertex and no edge -- AppendEdgePolylinesID only stamps an edge that already exists -- and that is what
202: 	 * keeps Vertices3d, sized by Close() one phase earlier, exactly right. Asserted across the call rather than

85: 	/**
86: 	 * FDebugLineBuffer
87: 	 *
88: 	 * Thread-safe collection of debug line batches. Written by operators on background threads
89: 	 * (e.g. FSplineMeshOp when bDrawRefSplines), drained by UMetaRoadPreviewManager::RenderDebugLines()
90: 	 * on the game thread. Encapsulates the lock so callers never touch a raw FCriticalSection.
91: 	 */
92: 	struct FDebugLineBuffer
93: 	{
94: 		struct FBatch
95: 		{
96: 			TArray<TPair<FVector, FVector>> Lines;
97: 			FColor Color;
98: 			float Thickness;
99: 		};
100: 
101: 		void Add(FBatch Batch)
102: 		{
103: 			FScopeLock Lock(&Mutex);
104: 			Batches.Add(MoveTemp(Batch));
105: 		}
106: 
107: 		template <typename FFunc>
108: 		void ForEach(FFunc&& Func) const
109: 		{
110: 			FScopeLock Lock(&Mutex);
111: 			for (const FBatch& Batch : Batches)
112: 			{
113: 				Func(Batch);
114: 			}
115: 		}
116: 
117: 	private:
118: 		TArray<FBatch> Batches;
119: 		mutable FCriticalSection Mutex;
120: 	};

152: 	 *
153: 	 * What FRoadTriangulationOp::SetActorWithRoads gathered from one (AMetaRoad, SubGroup) scope. Grouped into its
154: 	 * own struct (a member of FRoadTriangulationData) so the input/output split is explicit rather than a banner
155: 	 * comment: layer ops receive the whole triangulation data by const&, and read inputs via `Data.Inputs.X`.
156: 	 * (FRoadTriangulationParams stays top-level on FRoadTriangulationData -- it is downstream config, not a gathered
157: 	 * input, and is read at many UV sites.) Note SimplePolygons can still GROW during the build: a RoadLane's
158: 	 * PRO polygon-attribute appends to it -- it is stable only by the time layer ops run.
159: 	 */
160: 	struct METAROADEDITOR_API FRoadBuildInputs
161: 	{
162: 		FTransform ActorTransform;
163: 		TArray<TStrongObjectPtr<URoadSplineEditorComponent>> Splines;
164: 		TArray<FRoadPolygonData> SimplePolygons;
165: 		TArray<FRoadFeatureLine> FeatureLines; // profile-follows-spline contributions (curbs/marks); consumed as-is by the sweep ops (not triangulated)
166: 
167: 		/**
168: 		 * The IRoadFeatureInterface components SimplePolygons / FeatureLines were gathered from, held alive for
169: 		 * the whole async build. Same reason Splines above is rooted, and NOT optional:
170: 		 *
171: 		 * This struct is plain C++ held by a TSharedPtr, so no UObject's reference graph reaches it and the
172: 		 * UPROPERTY markers inside FRoadPolygonData / FRoadFeatureLine never get visited. Their
173: 		 * TObjectPtr<UMaterialInterface>s (FRoadZone::OverrideMaterial / OverrideDecal,
174: 		 * FRoadFeatureCurb::OverrideMaterial, FRoadMarkData::OverrideMaterial) therefore keep NOTHING alive -- for
175: 		 * GC they are bare pointers. The gather runs on a worker thread and the sweep ops read the arrays later on
176: 		 * other workers, so a GC in between could free a material still in use.
177: 		 *
178: 		 * Rooting the producing COMPONENT rather than each material is deliberate: every such material is owned by
179: 		 * the component that named it (its own UPROPERTY), so one strong ref covers all of them transitively --
180: 		 * including whatever UObject a future feature or zone type adds, which a hand-written per-type collector
181: 		 * would silently miss.
182: 		 */
183: 		TArray<TStrongObjectPtr<UActorComponent>> FeatureComponents;
184: 
185: 		/**
186: 		 * Cross-section profiles read off road splines that are CONNECTED to this scope but live OUTSIDE it --
187: 		 * a different AMetaRoad, or a different SubGroup of the same actor. Without them the two meshes would
188: 		 * meet at a step wherever their profiles differ.
189: 		 *
190: 		 * Snapshotted as plain data in SetActorWithRoads, which runs on the GAME THREAD
191: 		 * (TBackgroundModelingComputeSource::StartNewCompute calls MakeNewOperator() before StartBackgroundTask),
192: 		 * because the connection graph is unreachable from here: URoadSplineEditorComponent::CloneFrom does not
193: 		 * copy Pred/SuccessorConnection, FRoadLaneSection's copy ctor nulls every lane connection, and
194: 		 * bDontCreateLaneConnections stops UpdateLayout from making new ones -- so IsConnected() on a clone is
195: 		 * ALWAYS false.
196: 		 */
197: 		TArray<FRoadExternalEdgeProfile> ExternalEdgeProfiles;
198: 
199: 		/** The external profile for one end of a spline, or null when that end is free / not connected outward. */
200: 		const FRoadEdgeProfileParams* FindExternalEdgeProfile(int32 SplineIndex, bool bAtStart) const
201: 		{
202: 			const FRoadExternalEdgeProfile* Found = ExternalEdgeProfiles.FindByPredicate(
203: 				[SplineIndex, bAtStart](const FRoadExternalEdgeProfile& It)
204: 				{
205: 					return It.SplineIndex == SplineIndex && It.bAtStart == bAtStart;
206: 				});
207: 			return Found ? &Found->Params : nullptr;
208: 		}
209: 	};

217: 	 * ring: ApplyCurbCuts evaluates every site against every vertex in the scope, and projecting onto a full ring
218: 	 * each time is not affordable on a densified surface. `Influence` is the same window expanded by Depth and is
219: 	 * the cheap reject that keeps the pass linear in practice.
220: 	 */

231: 
232: 		/** Full ring length, so the cut's own evaluation can measure along the shortest arc across the seam. */
233: 		double RingLength = 0.0;
234:

236: 
237: 		/** Chain bounds expanded by Depth â€” a vertex outside cannot be affected. */
238: 		FBox2D Influence = FBox2D(ForceInit);
