245: 	/**
246: 	 * FRoadTriangulationData
247: 	 *
248: 	 * Shared triangulation result produced by FRoadTriangulationOp and consumed by all layer
249: 	 * operators. `Inputs` (what was gathered) is grouped separately from the base-mesh OUTPUTS below;
250: 	 * layer ops receive this whole struct by const& so they cannot mutate the base mesh (DebugDraw, a
251: 	 * thread-safe service, is the one `mutable` escape). Params (downstream config), DebugDraw and
252: 	 * Spatial (mesh/AABB query) are the non-input concerns. Arrangement/Vertices3d/Triangles/Polygons/
253: 	 * Boundaries are the core geometry.
254: 	 */
255: 	/**
256: 	 * FRoadVertexTable -- the 3D lift of the arrangement, indexed 1:1 by arrangement vertex id (VID).
257: 	 *
258: 	 * THE HUG CONTRACT USED TO BE A COMMENT. The rule is
259: 	 *
260: 	 *     Num() == Graph.MaxVertexID()   and   index == VID
261: 	 *
262: 	 * and everything downstream leans on it: a claimed triangle is inserted into a layer mesh by its raw
263: 	 * FIndex3i with no remapping, which only works because a mesh vertex id and an arrangement vertex id are
264: 	 * the same number. A SHORT array was an out-of-bounds read three stages later; a LONG one was caught by
265: 	 * nothing at all.
266: 	 *
267: 	 * This type does not make a violation impossible -- nothing can, while the ids come from a separate graph
268: 	 * object. What it does is remove the two ways it happened: SetNum is not reachable, so the size can only be
269: 	 * set FROM the graph; and MatchesGraph() gives the consumers that a mis-size corrupts silently something to
270: 	 * assert on.
271: 	 *
272: 	 * The read surface is deliberately a drop-in for the TArray it replaces -- Num, IsValidIndex, operator[],
273: 	 * range-for. Across the whole subsystem only three call sites change, and none of them is a read: the two
274: 	 * sizing sites and one signature. A stronger interface (a distinct FRoadVertexId, GetVertex/ModifyVertex
275: 	 * accessors) would have touched forty call sites to buy nothing: the ids already come from the graph, so
276: 	 * there is no second integer namespace to confuse them with.
277: 	 */
278: 	class METAROADEDITOR_API FRoadVertexTable
279: 	{
280: 	public:
281: 		int32 Num() const { return Vertices.Num(); }
282: 		bool IsValidIndex(int32 VID) const { return Vertices.IsValidIndex(VID); }
283: 
284: 		const FArrangementVertex3d& operator[](int32 VID) const { return Vertices[VID]; }
285: 		FArrangementVertex3d& operator[](int32 VID) { return Vertices[VID]; }
286: 
287: 		const FArrangementVertex3d* begin() const { return Vertices.GetData(); }
288: 		const FArrangementVertex3d* end() const { return Vertices.GetData() + Vertices.Num(); }
289: 		FArrangementVertex3d* begin() { return Vertices.GetData(); }
290: 		FArrangementVertex3d* end() { return Vertices.GetData() + Vertices.Num(); }
291: 
292: 		/** For the one consumer that wants the whole run of vertices (the spatial index). */
293: 		TConstArrayView<FArrangementVertex3d> View() const { return Vertices; }
294: 
---
196: 		 */
197: 		TArray<FRoadExternalEdgeProfile> ExternalEdgeProfiles;
198: 
199: 		/** The external profile for one end of a spline, or null when that end is free / not connected outward. */
200: 		const FRoadEdgeProfileParams* FindExternalEdgeProfile(int32 SplineIndex, bool bAtStart) const
201: 		{
202: 			const FRoadExternalEdgeProfile* Found = ExternalEdgeProfiles.FindByPredicate(
203: 				[SplineIndex, bAtStart](const FRoadExternalEdgeProfile& It)
204: 				{
205: 					return It.SplineIndex == SplineIndex && It.bAtStart == bAtStart;
206: 				});
207: 			return Found ? &Found->Params : nullptr;
208: 		}
209: 	};

249: 	 * operators. `Inputs` (what was gathered) is grouped separately from the base-mesh OUTPUTS below;
250: 	 * layer ops receive this whole struct by const& so they cannot mutate the base mesh (DebugDraw, a
251: 	 * thread-safe service, is the one `mutable` escape). Params (downstream config), DebugDraw and
252: 	 * Spatial (mesh/AABB query) are the non-input concerns. Arrangement/Vertices3d/Triangles/Polygons/
253: 	 * Boundaries are the core geometry.
254: 	 */
255: 	/**
256: 	 * FRoadVertexTable -- the 3D lift of the arrangement, indexed 1:1 by arrangement vertex id (VID).
257: 	 *
258: 	 * THE HUG CONTRACT USED TO BE A COMMENT. The rule is
259: 	 *
260: 	 *     Num() == Graph.MaxVertexID()   and   index == VID
261: 	 *
262: 	 * and everything downstream leans on it: a claimed triangle is inserted into a layer mesh by its raw
263: 	 * FIndex3i with no remapping, which only works because a mesh vertex id and an arrangement vertex id are
264: 	 * the same number. A SHORT array was an out-of-bounds read three stages later; a LONG one was caught by
265: 	 * nothing at all.
266: 	 *
267: 	 * This type does not make a violation impossible -- nothing can, while the ids come from a separate graph
268: 	 * object. What it does is remove the two ways it happened: SetNum is not reachable, so the size can only be
269: 	 * set FROM the graph; and MatchesGraph() gives the consumers that a mis-size corrupts silently something to
270: 	 * assert on.
271: 	 *
272: 	 * The read surface is deliberately a drop-in for the TArray it replaces -- Num, IsValidIndex, operator[],
273: 	 * range-for. Across the whole subsystem only three call sites change, and none of them is a read: the two
274: 	 * sizing sites and one signature. A stronger interface (a distinct FRoadVertexId, GetVertex/ModifyVertex
275: 	 * accessors) would have touched forty call sites to buy nothing: the ids already come from the graph, so
276: 	 * there is no second integer namespace to confuse them with.
277: 	 */
278: 	class METAROADEDITOR_API FRoadVertexTable
279: 	{
280: 	public:
281: 		int32 Num() const { return Vertices.Num(); }
282: 		bool IsValidIndex(int32 VID) const { return Vertices.IsValidIndex(VID); }
283: 
284: 		const FArrangementVertex3d& operator[](int32 VID) const { return Vertices[VID]; }
285: 		FArrangementVertex3d& operator[](int32 VID) { return Vertices[VID]; }
286: 
287: 		const FArrangementVertex3d* begin() const { return Vertices.GetData(); }
288: 		const FArrangementVertex3d* end() const { return Vertices.GetData() + Vertices.Num(); }
289: 		FArrangementVertex3d* begin() { return Vertices.GetData(); }
290: 		FArrangementVertex3d* end() { return Vertices.GetData() + Vertices.Num(); }
291: 
292: 		/** For the one consumer that wants the whole run of vertices (the spatial index). */
293: 		TConstArrayView<FArrangementVertex3d> View() const { return Vertices; }
294: 
295: 		/** The contract, as a question. Assert on it wherever a mis-size would corrupt rather than crash. */
296: 		bool MatchesGraph(const MetaRoad::FDynamicGraph2d& Graph) const { return Vertices.Num() == Graph.MaxVertexID(); }
---
197: 		TArray<FRoadExternalEdgeProfile> ExternalEdgeProfiles;
198: 
199: 		/** The external profile for one end of a spline, or null when that end is free / not connected outward. */
200: 		const FRoadEdgeProfileParams* FindExternalEdgeProfile(int32 SplineIndex, bool bAtStart) const
201: 		{
202: 			const FRoadExternalEdgeProfile* Found = ExternalEdgeProfiles.FindByPredicate(
203: 				[SplineIndex, bAtStart](const FRoadExternalEdgeProfile& It)
204: 				{
205: 					return It.SplineIndex == SplineIndex && It.bAtStart == bAtStart;
206: 				});
207: 			return Found ? &Found->Params : nullptr;
208: 		}
209: 	};
210: 
211: 	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
212: 
213: 	/**
214: 	 * FRoadCurbCutSite -- one curb cut, resolved into world space and detached from whoever authored it.
215: 	 *
216: 	 * Carries only the STRETCH of the authoring ring the cut can reach (CurbCutRing::ExtractWindow), not the whole
217: 	 * ring: ApplyCurbCuts evaluates every site against every vertex in the scope, and projecting onto a full ring
218: 	 * each time is not affordable on a densified surface. `Influence` is the same window expanded by Depth and is
219: 	 * the cheap reject that keeps the pass linear in practice.
220: 	 */
221: 	struct METAROADEDITOR_API FRoadCurbCutSite
222: 	{
223: 		/** The reachable stretch of ring, world XY, OPEN (it may wrap the seam). */
224: 		TArray<FVector2D> Chain;
225: 
226: 		/** Absolute, strictly increasing arc length per Chain vertex; past RingLength where the chain wrapped. */
227: 		TArray<double> Arcs;
228: 
229: 		/** Centre of the cut in the ring's arc coordinate. */
230: 		double ArcOffset = 0.0;
231: 
232: 		/** Full ring length, so the cut's own evaluation can measure along the shortest arc across the seam. */
233: 		double RingLength = 0.0;
234: 
235: 		FRoadCurbCut Cut;
236: 
237: 		/** Chain bounds expanded by Depth â€” a vertex outside cannot be affected. */
238: 		FBox2D Influence = FBox2D(ForceInit);
239: 
240: 		bool IsValid() const { return Chain.Num() >= 2 && Arcs.Num() == Chain.Num() && Cut.Depth > UE_DOUBLE_SMALL_NUMBER; }
241: 	};
242:

278: 	class METAROADEDITOR_API FRoadVertexTable
279: 	{
280: 	public:
281: 		int32 Num() const { return Vertices.Num(); }
282: 		bool IsValidIndex(int32 VID) const { return Vertices.IsValidIndex(VID); }
283: 
284: 		const FArrangementVertex3d& operator[](int32 VID) const { return Vertices[VID]; }
285: 		FArrangementVertex3d& operator[](int32 VID) { return Vertices[VID]; }
286: 
287: 		const FArrangementVertex3d* begin() const { return Vertices.GetData(); }
288: 		const FArrangementVertex3d* end() const { return Vertices.GetData() + Vertices.Num(); }
289: 		FArrangementVertex3d* begin() { return Vertices.GetData(); }
290: 		FArrangementVertex3d* end() { return Vertices.GetData() + Vertices.Num(); }
291: 
292: 		/** For the one consumer that wants the whole run of vertices (the spatial index). */
293: 		TConstArrayView<FArrangementVertex3d> View() const { return Vertices; }
294: 
295: 		/** The contract, as a question. Assert on it wherever a mis-size would corrupt rather than crash. */
296: 		bool MatchesGraph(const MetaRoad::FDynamicGraph2d& Graph) const { return Vertices.Num() == Graph.MaxVertexID(); }
297: 
298: 		/**
299: 		 * Grow to cover every vertex now in Graph. THE ONLY OPERATION THAT CHANGES Num(), and it takes the size
300: 		 * FROM the graph rather than from the caller -- which is the whole point, because both historical bugs
301: 		 * were a caller computing that number itself.
302: 		 *
303: 		 * Idempotent and monotone. A shrink ensures rather than truncating: silently dropping vertices is
304: 		 * exactly the failure this exists to prevent, and there is no legitimate caller for it.
305: 		 */
306: 		void SyncToGraph(const MetaRoad::FDynamicGraph2d& Graph)
307: 		{
308: 			const int32 Target = Graph.MaxVertexID();
309: 			if (!ensureMsgf(Target >= Vertices.Num(),
310: 				TEXT("FRoadVertexTable::SyncToGraph asked to SHRINK %d -> %d; refusing"), Vertices.Num(), Target))
311: 			{
312: 				return;
313: 			}
314: 			Vertices.SetNum(Target);
315: 		}
---
339: 		 * free -- which is exactly what FindPolyline, MakeDelaunay2, OpUtils::FindBoundaries, SyncToGraph,
340: 		 * MatchesGraph, FindPinnedVertex and MatchPolyline all take already.
341: 		 *
342: 		 * A GetGraph()-only surface would NOT have been enough: PointHash and VertexSnapTol are SIBLINGS of
343: 		 * Graph inside FArrangement2d, unreachable from it, and six sites pass the whole arrangement.
344: 		 */
345: 		const MetaRoad::FArrangement2d& GetArrangement() const { check(Arrangement.IsValid()); return *Arrangement; }
346: 
347: 		/** Sugar for the ~30 sites that only want the graph. Const, so it cannot quietly become a second
348: 		 *  spelling of a mutable path. */
349: 		const MetaRoad::FDynamicGraph2d& GetGraph() const { return GetArrangement().Graph; }
350: 
351: 		/** The arrangement does not exist until FRoadArrangementBuilder creates it, so a build that bailed out
352: 		 *  during Initialize never gets one. Ask before reading on any path that can run after a bail. */
353: 		bool HasArrangement() const { return Arrangement.IsValid(); }
354: 
355: 		FRoadVertexTable Vertices3d; // 1:1 with the arrangement by VID -- see FRoadVertexTable
356: 
357: 		/**
358: 		 * Every surface polygon this build will triangulate: the GATHERED ones first (copied from
359: 		 * Inputs.SimplePolygons by the Initialize stage), then the ones a lane's PRO Polygon attribute derives
360: 		 * while its constructor runs. Order is therefore unchanged from when the two lived in one array, which
361: 		 * matters -- FProceduralPolygon_Simple's index and GetObjectIndex() are both positions in it.
362: 		 *
363: 		 * It lives HERE rather than on FRoadBuildInputs because it grows during the build, and an input that
364: 		 * grows is not a snapshot. That was the state of affairs before: the inputs struct carried a caveat
365: 		 * saying so, and layer ops received the whole thing as const& while one polygon constructor had already
366: 		 * been appending to it.
367: 		 */
368: 		TArray<FRoadPolygonData> SurfacePolygons;
369: 		TArray<TArray<FIndex2i>> Boundaries;
370: 		TArray<FIndex3i> Triangles;
371: 		TArray<TUniquePtr<FProceduralPolygon>> Polygons;
372: 
373: 		/** For each FRoadFeatureLine that hugs the mesh (HugMode == Weld OR Magnetize), the EXACT ordered arrangement VID
374: 		 *  chain -- recovered by FProceduralPolygon_FeatureLine (FindPolyline over its PID), so it includes every crossing
375: 		 *  vertex triangulation added. Weld INSERTS the chain as CenterLine edges; Magnetize TAGS the existing edges its
376: 		 *  approximation matched (inserting nothing). Indexed 1:1 with FeatureLines; empty for Drape lines or when the
377: 		 *  weld/match failed. The sweep ops walk this MODE-BLIND so a mark/curb centerline hugs the mesh edges EXACTLY,
378: 		 *  instead of a chord between the authored vertices (which cuts across crossings where the surface bends).
379: 		 *  Indexes into Vertices3d. */
380: 		TArray<TArray<int32>> FeatureLineWeldedPaths;
381: 
382: 		/**
383: 		 * Every curb cut authored on a closed RING in this scope, as a SCOPE-WIDE field rather than a property of
384: 		 * the polygon that authored it. Registered during the open phase (FRoadArrangementBuilder::AddCurbCutSite)
385: 		 * and consumed once by BuildStages::ApplyCurbCuts, which writes FArrangementVertex3d::HeightScale.
386: 		 *
387: 		 * IT HAS TO BE SCOPE-WIDE, and that is the whole point of the type. A cut used to be applied inside the
388: 		 * authoring polygon's FillVertexInfo, so it reached only the vertices THAT polygon owned. At the seam where
389: 		 * a sidewalk spline meets a road sidewalk lane the two share their boundary vertices but nothing else, so
390: 		 * the lane kept full height a centimetre inside the ramp: its surface did not match the fill's, and its
391: 		 * curb -- which reads its own polygon's lift -- followed the cut only where a shared vertex happened to
392: 		 * exist. A ramp is a property of a PLACE, not of an owner.
393: 		 */
394: 		TArray<FRoadCurbCutSite> CurbCutSites;
395: 		// mutable: a thread-safe debug channel (its own FCriticalSection). The one field a layer op may write while
396: 		// holding the data by const& (FSplineMeshOp's ref-spline debug draw) -- it is not part of the base mesh.
397: 		mutable FDebugLineBuffer DebugDraw;
398: 		FSpatialQuery Spatial;
399: 
400: 		//~ ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
401: 		//~ Diagnostics only, and only while meta.road.BuildFingerprint is on: a running digest of every 2D point the
402: 		//~ polygon contours hand to the arrangement, hashed by RAW BITS.
403: 		//~
404: 		//~ It exists to split one specific question. The fingerprint showed that the arrangement's stored vertex
405: 		//~ coordinates drift below 1e-4 between a fresh editor process and one that has already built other roads,
406: 		//~ while the gathered spline input is bit-identical -- so the drift is manufactured somewhere between the two.
407: 		//~ Only two steps sit in that gap: sampling the spline into contours, and inserting those contours (with
408: 		//~ snapping and intersection splitting). Hashing the points AS HANDED IN separates them: this digest moving
409: 		//~ means the sampling drifts; this digest holding while graphVx moves means the arrangement does.
410: 		//~
411: 		//~ Seeded with the FNV-1a offset basis so an empty build hashes to the same value the fingerprint prints for
412: 		//~ any other empty sequence.
413: 		uint64 SampleDigestSequential = 0xcbf29ce484222325ull;
414: 		uint64 SampleDigestCommutative = 0;
415: 		int32  SampleDigestCount = 0;
416: 
417: 		//~ -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
418: 		//~ Derived lookups. Not inputs and not outputs -- answers to questions asked once per vertex or per
419: 		//~ triangle by passes that would otherwise each re-derive them by linear scan. Both are built by the
420: 		//~ build stage that produces the data they summarise, and both are pure functions of it, so a pass that
421: 		//~ mutates Boundaries or a polygon's TrianglesIDs must rebuild the matching one.
422: 
423: 		/** Set for every VID that appears in any Boundaries edge. Built by BuildBoundaryVertexIndex(). */
424: 		TBitArray<> bBoundaryVertex;
425: 
426: 		/** Set for every TID some polygon claimed. Built by BuildClaimedTriangleIndex(). */
427: 		TBitArray<> bClaimedTriangle;
428: 
429: 		/** Call after Boundaries is final. IsBoundaryVertex() answers from a linear scan until this runs, which
430: 		 *  is correct but O(boundary edges) per call -- and its callers ask once per neighbour per vertex. */
431: 		void BuildBoundaryVertexIndex();
432: 
433: 		/** Call after every polygon has claimed its triangles. */
434: 		void BuildClaimedTriangleIndex();
435: 
436: 		/** A triangle no polygon claimed at all -- a gap left inside a junction. Requires the index. */
437: 		bool IsTriangleClaimed(int TID) const;
438: 
439: 		void AddDebugLines(const TArray<FIndex2i>& InBoundaries, const FColor& Color, float Thickness);
---
364: 		 * grows is not a snapshot. That was the state of affairs before: the inputs struct carried a caveat
365: 		 * saying so, and layer ops received the whole thing as const& while one polygon constructor had already
366: 		 * been appending to it.
367: 		 */
368: 		TArray<FRoadPolygonData> SurfacePolygons;
369: 		TArray<TArray<FIndex2i>> Boundaries;
370: 		TArray<FIndex3i> Triangles;
371: 		TArray<TUniquePtr<FProceduralPolygon>> Polygons;
372: 
373: 		/** For each FRoadFeatureLine that hugs the mesh (HugMode == Weld OR Magnetize), the EXACT ordered arrangement VID
374: 		 *  chain -- recovered by FProceduralPolygon_FeatureLine (FindPolyline over its PID), so it includes every crossing
375: 		 *  vertex triangulation added. Weld INSERTS the chain as CenterLine edges; Magnetize TAGS the existing edges its
376: 		 *  approximation matched (inserting nothing). Indexed 1:1 with FeatureLines; empty for Drape lines or when the
377: 		 *  weld/match failed. The sweep ops walk this MODE-BLIND so a mark/curb centerline hugs the mesh edges EXACTLY,
378: 		 *  instead of a chord between the authored vertices (which cuts across crossings where the surface bends).
379: 		 *  Indexes into Vertices3d. */
380: 		TArray<TArray<int32>> FeatureLineWeldedPaths;
381: 
382: 		/**
383: 		 * Every curb cut authored on a closed RING in this scope, as a SCOPE-WIDE field rather than a property of
384: 		 * the polygon that authored it. Registered during the open phase (FRoadArrangementBuilder::AddCurbCutSite)
385: 		 * and consumed once by BuildStages::ApplyCurbCuts, which writes FArrangementVertex3d::HeightScale.
386: 		 *
387: 		 * IT HAS TO BE SCOPE-WIDE, and that is the whole point of the type. A cut used to be applied inside the
388: 		 * authoring polygon's FillVertexInfo, so it reached only the vertices THAT polygon owned. At the seam where
389: 		 * a sidewalk spline meets a road sidewalk lane the two share their boundary vertices but nothing else, so
390: 		 * the lane kept full height a centimetre inside the ramp: its surface did not match the fill's, and its
391: 		 * curb -- which reads its own polygon's lift -- followed the cut only where a shared vertex happened to
392: 		 * exist. A ramp is a property of a PLACE, not of an owner.
393: 		 */
394: 		TArray<FRoadCurbCutSite> CurbCutSites;
395: 		// mutable: a thread-safe debug channel (its own FCriticalSection). The one field a layer op may write while
396: 		// holding the data by const& (FSplineMeshOp's ref-spline debug draw) -- it is not part of the base mesh.
397: 		mutable FDebugLineBuffer DebugDraw;
398: 		FSpatialQuery Spatial;
399: 
400: 		//~ ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
401: 		//~ Diagnostics only, and only while meta.road.BuildFingerprint is on: a running digest of every 2D point the
402: 		//~ polygon contours hand to the arrangement, hashed by RAW BITS.
403: 		//~
404: 		//~ It exists to split one specific question. The fingerprint showed that the arrangement's stored vertex
405: 		//~ coordinates drift below 1e-4 between a fresh editor process and one that has already built other roads,
406: 		//~ while the gathered spline input is bit-identical -- so the drift is manufactured somewhere between the two.
407: 		//~ Only two steps sit in that gap: sampling the spline into contours, and inserting those contours (with
408: 		//~ snapping and intersection splitting). Hashing the points AS HANDED IN separates them: this digest moving
409: 		//~ means the sampling drifts; this digest holding while graphVx moves means the arrangement does.
410: 		//~
411: 		//~ Seeded with the FNV-1a offset basis so an empty build hashes to the same value the fingerprint prints for
412: 		//~ any other empty sequence.
413: 		uint64 SampleDigestSequential = 0xcbf29ce484222325ull;
414: 		uint64 SampleDigestCommutative = 0;
415: 		int32  SampleDigestCount = 0;
416: 
417: 		//~ -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
418: 		//~ Derived lookups. Not inputs and not outputs -- answers to questions asked once per vertex or per
419: 		//~ triangle by passes that would otherwise each re-derive them by linear scan. Both are built by the
420: 		//~ build stage that produces the data they summarise, and both are pure functions of it, so a pass that
421: 		//~ mutates Boundaries or a polygon's TrianglesIDs must rebuild the matching one.

445: 		bool IsInsideMesh(const FVector2D& Point, double TolCm) const;

447: 		/**
448: 		 * Rebuilds vertex normals from the current Vertices3d + Triangles (optionally CotanSmoothing the
449: 		 * Z first), then (optionally) rebuilds the Spatial AABB tree. Pure geometry â€” never touches UWorld,
450: 		 * so it is safe both on the op's background thread and on the game thread (e.g. after the
451: 		 * Snap to Ground line-trace pass overrides per-vertex Z). Behaviour matches the inline code that
452: 		 * previously lived at the end of FRoadTriangulationOp::CalculateResult().
453: 		 */
454: 		void FinalizeVertexGeometry(bool bCotanSmoothZ, float SmoothSpeed, float Smoothness,
455: 		                            bool bRebuildSpatial, FProgressCancel* Progress = nullptr);

458: 		/**
459: 		 * PRIVATE, because const did not reach it. TUniquePtr::operator->() const hands back a NON-const
460: 		 * FArrangement2d*, so `const FRoadTriangulationData&` -- what a polygon's Owner holds, what every layer
461: 		 * op holds as TSharedPtr<const ...>, what every diagnostic holds -- did not stop a write to the graph.
462: 		 * Every other write into this struct is caught by const; this one was not, and it was not theoretical:
463: 		 * FDriveSurfaceOp and FDecalsOp each bound a live NON-const FDynamicGraph2d& out of a const build, on
464: 		 * every build, because `auto&` gave them one for the asking.
465: 		 *
466: 		 * THREE TYPES MAY WRITE, ONE PER PHASE, and this list is the answer to "who". A fourth entry needs a new
467: 		 * PHASE, not a new caller: a friend added because some function wants to write is GetMutableArrangement()
468: 		 * with extra steps, which is the thing this is here to refuse.
469: 		 *
470: 		 * What it makes impossible, concretely -- each of these used to be a silent corruption:
471: 		 *   - an Insert after Close(): Vertices3d ends up short of MaxVertexID, read out of bounds three stages later;
472: 		 *   - a SplitEdgeAtPoint after CompleteArrangement: every recovered VID chain, FeatureLineWeldedPaths and
473: 		 *     the cap-edge adjacency go stale while still looking well-formed;
474: 		 *   - an edge added during densification: FRoadSurfacePointSink's segment index is a one-shot snapshot, so
---
